# WORKLOG: era / market stem analysis (topic 2)

Newest entries at the bottom. Every entry states config, data and the measured result; interpretation is labeled.

## 2026-10-25 setup
- Data: PA1 `dataset_A` (decade, 6 classes: 1960s to 2010s) and `dataset_B` (market, 6 classes: US, UK, Brazil, Spain, Germany, Italy; all 1980s). 30 s middle excerpts, WAV mono 24 kHz, artists disjoint across splits, test labels hidden. Labeled: A 1026 train / 132 val, B 798 train / 102 val. Source: Discogs-VI recordings with YouTube links (so audio is YouTube-quality).
- Prior result on the same data (PA1 README, validation): MERT-v2-30s frozen probe 0.5455 top-1 on A, 0.6471 on B (single-model numbers). Those are the reference points for the mix condition here.
- Server: `~/era_market/` with `venv` (system-site-packages of conda env musicgen_steer: torch 2.5.1, transformers 5.18; added demucs 4.1.0, pyloudnorm). Data zips copied by rsync from the Mac PA1 folder.
- Disk on server: 136 GB free of 1.8 TB (shared, 92% used). Stems and embeddings for 2292 clips are small (about 1 GB), keep it that way.

## 2026-10-25 pipeline smoke tests and launch
- Code: `src/01_separate.py` (HT Demucs `htdemucs`, shifts 1, overlap .25; clip 24k mono -> 44.1k stereo -> stems -> 24k mono fp16; accompaniment = drums + bass + other), `src/02_embed.py` (m-a-p/MERT-v2-30s, 24 layers x 1024, time-mean with feature_attention_mask; conditions mix_raw, mix_norm, voc_raw, voc_norm, acc_raw, acc_norm, recomb_norm; norm = -23 LUFS via pyloudnorm), `src/04_probe.py` (logistic regression on standardized layer features; layer and C chosen by 5-fold CV on train only, scored once on validation; bootstrap CI; exact McNemar between conditions), `src/run_stems.sh`.
- Smoke test (4 clips, GPU 2 shared with other users at ~100% utilization): separation about 1 s per clip including model load amortized (8.7 s wall for 4); MERT-v2-30s works under transformers 5.18 with output_hidden_states (no hook workaround), shape (24, 1024) per clip.
- Launched tmux `era` on the server: separate A then B, then embed A then B (all 7 conditions). Done flag: `~/era_market/stems_emb.done`. Estimated 40 min separation + embedding time.
- Added `src/03_stats.py` (9 signal statistics per clip for mix, vocal and accompaniment: LUFS, loudness range, crest factor, RMS, bandwidth, centroid, rolloff-85, zcr, noise floor), `src/05_langid.py` (openai/whisper-small language-token posterior over 100 languages on the vocal stem and the mix), `src/run_analysis.sh` (waits for `stems_emb.done`, then stats, language ID, probes). Language ID smoke test passed (3 clips).
- tmux on server: `era` (separate A then B, then embed), `era2` (waits, then analysis chain, writes `analysis.done`), `era3` (separate B in parallel on GPU 1; the script skips clips already separated). Measured about 20 clips/min per process on the shared GPUs, so separation takes roughly 1 to 1.5 hours.

## 2026-10-25 first results: stem-wise probes (MERT-v2-30s, validation split)
Config: `src/04_probe.py`. Features: MERT-v2-30s, time-mean per layer (24 x 1024). Probe: standardized logistic regression; layer and C chosen by 5-fold CV on the train split only (CV is not artist-grouped, so it only selects hyperparameters), then scored once on the validation split (A: n=132, 22 per class; B: n=102, 17 per class). 95% CI = bootstrap over clips (2000). McNemar = exact, paired on the same validation clips, uncorrected for multiple comparisons. Chance: 1/6 = 0.167. Separation: HT Demucs (vocals vs drums+bass+other), norm = -23 LUFS per signal. Clips come from YouTube so all audio is lossy and old recordings are transfers.

Validation accuracy (best layer in brackets):
| condition | A decade | B market |
|---|---|---|
| mix_raw | 0.515 [L17] (CI 0.43-0.60) | 0.578 [L14] (0.48-0.68) |
| mix_norm | 0.500 [L17] | 0.578 [L14] |
| vocals_raw | 0.394 [L20] | 0.549 [L15] |
| vocals_norm | 0.288 [L10] | 0.588 [L15] |
| accomp_raw | 0.508 [L10] | 0.412 [L13] |
| accomp_norm | 0.432 [L12] | 0.382 [L14] |
| recombined_norm | 0.508 [L17] | 0.569 [L14] |
Paired McNemar (validation): decade, mix_norm vs vocals_norm 41 vs 13 clips p=0.0002; accomp_raw vs vocals_norm 39 vs 10 p<0.0001; mix vs accomp not significant (p>=0.09). Market, mix_norm vs accomp_norm 25 vs 5 p=0.0003; vocals_norm vs accomp_norm 32 vs 11 p=0.002; mix vs vocals not significant. Recombined (vocals+accomp re-summed) is not distinguishable from the original mix in either task (accuracy 0.508 vs 0.515 and 0.569 vs 0.578), so separation processing itself does not change the probe result here.

Shortcut baselines (`src/03_stats.py`, `src/05_langid.py`, `src/06_extra_probes.py`; same protocol):
| input | A decade | B market |
|---|---|---|
| 9 signal statistics, mix (LUFS, loudness range, crest factor, RMS, bandwidth, centroid, rolloff, zcr, noise floor) | 0.311 | 0.255 |
| signal statistics, vocal stem / accomp stem | 0.227 / 0.303 | 0.225 / 0.225 |
| Whisper-small language posterior, vocal stem | 0.273 | **0.529** |
| Whisper-small language posterior, mix | 0.318 | **0.539** |
| accomp_norm embedding + vocal language posterior | 0.462 | 0.529 |
| mix_norm embedding + vocal language posterior | 0.485 | 0.578 |
Market: language posterior alone (0.53) is not significantly different from the full-mix embedding probe (0.578) by McNemar on 102 clips; adding the vocal-stem language posterior to the accomp embedding raises accuracy from 0.382 to 0.529 (19 vs 4 clips, p=0.003) and adding it to the mix embedding does not change accuracy (0.578). Decade: signal statistics and language posteriors reach 0.23-0.32, above chance but far below the embeddings (p<=0.002 vs mix_norm).

Per-class validation recall and confusions: `results/confusion_summary.txt`. Decade: errors are mostly adjacent decades (mix_norm within-one-decade accuracy 0.841, mean absolute decade error 0.75; 2000s is the weakest class at 0.27 recall, confused with 1990s and 2010s). Vocal stem decade error is larger (mean 1.23, within-one 0.705). Market: Brazil and Spain are easiest (mix 0.94, 0.65), Germany/US/Italy hardest; US->Germany and UK->US are the top mix confusions.

Reading (interpretation, one encoder, small validation sets, uncorrected pairwise tests):
- Decade information in this setup sits mostly in the accompaniment: accompaniment alone matches the mix, the vocal stem is clearly worse (but above chance). Market information sits mostly in the vocals and is largely explained by language: the vocal stem matches the mix, accompaniment is much worse, and a language-ID posterior alone almost reaches the mix accuracy.
- The accompaniment still carries some market information (0.38-0.41 vs chance 0.167) that is not language, since the accompaniment stem has no singing language except separation bleed; bleed of vocals into the accompaniment stem was not measured.
- Simple signal statistics do not explain the decade result (0.31 vs 0.50), but the loudness cue is partly removed already by the -23 LUFS normalization used in all norm conditions; raw and normalized conditions are not significantly different.
- Caveats: validation n is 102 to 132 so CIs span about +-9 points and differences below about 10 points are not resolved; 16+ pairwise tests were not corrected; label noise from reissues (Discogs release year) is not handled; PA1 test labels are hidden so there is no untouched test split here.

## 2026-10-25 second encoder and generation launch
- MuQ (OpenMuQ/MuQ-large-msd-iter, self-supervised on MSD, independent of Discogs-supervised MAEST) needed two workarounds under transformers 5.18: `_attn_implementation` on its EasyDict configs, and forward hooks on `m.model.conformer.layers` to rebuild hidden states (13 states x 1024). `src/muq_util.py`, `src/02b_embed_muq.py`. Embeddings for all 7 conditions of A and B running (tmux `era_muq`, flag `muq.done`). Purpose: (i) check the stem pattern is not MERT-specific, (ii) an era probe on an encoder different from the analysis encoder for evaluating generated covers.
- Generation: `src/07_generate.py`, MusicGen-Melody 1.5B fp16 (transformers MusicgenMelodyForConditionalGeneration), guidance 3.0, 1500 new tokens (30 s), sampling. Conditioning chroma = original MIX of a validation clip of dataset A; the generated audio replaces the accompaniment stem; remix = original vocal stem + generated audio RMS-matched to the original accompaniment. 12 clips (2 per decade, first in manifest order) x 4 prompts (neutral, 1960s, 1980s, 2010s) x 2 seeds = 96 generations; prompts in the script. Smoke test: 2 generations in about 1 minute after model download (tmux `era_gen`, flag `gen.done`, outputs `~/era_market/gen/`, local only: PA1 audio is licensed for the course, so no generated audio from it goes into git).

## 2026-10-25 vocal-bleed diagnostics (`src/08_bleed.py`, `src/09_bleed_analysis.py`, `results/bleed_summary.txt`)
Config: per clip vocal energy share = rms(vocal stem) / (rms(vocal) + rms(accomp)); Whisper-small language posterior computed on the loudness-normalized ACCOMPANIMENT stem, then the same logistic-regression protocol as the vocal-stem language probe (CV on train, scored once on validation).
- Language posterior of the accompaniment stem predicts market at 0.235 validation accuracy (cv 0.267; chance 0.167), versus 0.529 for the vocal stem. Reading (interpretation): little language information is left in the accompaniment stem, so the 0.38-0.41 market accuracy of accompaniment-only probes is probably not explained by bleed of sung language; it could still come from vocal timbre bleed or from musical cues. Not tested directly.
- Same posterior on decade: 0.326 (cv 0.307), which is about the level of the other weak shortcut baselines (0.27-0.32).
- Vocal share per clip: mean 0.35 (A) and 0.36 (B), full range 0 to 1 (some clips have almost no separated vocal). Validation accuracy by vocal-share tercile (n=34 to 44 per tercile, so differences below about 15 points are not resolved): market mix_norm 0.35 / 0.65 / 0.74, vocal 0.41 / 0.68 / 0.68, accompaniment 0.29 / 0.41 / 0.44 (low / mid / high); decade accompaniment 0.25 / 0.57 / 0.48. Market accuracy of the mix rises with vocal share (spearman 0.24 between vocal share and correctness), consistent with the language reading; the least-vocal tercile is also the least accurate for the accompaniment decade probe, which was not expected and is not explained (could be quiet or old recordings where separation finds little vocal).

## 2026-10-25 second encoder: MuQ stem probes (`src/04_probe.py --prefix muq_`, `results/probe_muq_{A,B}.json`)
Config as for MERT-v2 (standardized logistic regression, layer and C by 5-fold CV on train, scored once on validation, bootstrap CI, exact McNemar). MuQ-large-msd-iter, 13 hidden states of 1024-d, time-mean.
Validation accuracy (norm conditions; layer in brackets):
| condition | A decade | B market |
|---|---|---|
| mix_norm | 0.477 [L8] (CI 0.40-0.56) | 0.471 [L3] (0.37-0.57) |
| vocals_norm | 0.318 [L2] (0.24-0.40) | 0.471 [L2] (0.37-0.56) |
| accomp_norm | 0.424 [L9] (0.34-0.51) | 0.392 [L8] (0.29-0.49) |
| recombined_norm | 0.485 | 0.461 |
Paired McNemar: decade mix vs vocals 33 vs 12 clips, p=0.0025; mix vs accomp p=0.17; vocals vs accomp 20 vs 34, p=0.076. Market: mix vs vocals 15 vs 15, p=1.0; mix vs accomp 20 vs 12, p=0.22; vocals vs accomp p=0.24.
Reading (interpretation): the same ordering as with MERT-v2 appears with an independent self-supervised encoder, so the pattern (decade carried by the accompaniment more than by the vocals, market carried by the vocals at least as much as by the accompaniment) is not specific to MERT-v2. The effect sizes are smaller and several contrasts are not significant with n=102 to 132; in particular the market accompaniment deficit (0.39 vs 0.47) is not resolved with MuQ, whereas with MERT-v2 it was (0.38 vs 0.58, p=0.0003). MuQ overall accuracy is below MERT-v2 (0.48 vs 0.50-0.52 decade, 0.47 vs 0.58 market). Recombined audio is again indistinguishable from the original mix.

## 2026-10-25 era-targeted accompaniment regeneration, first evaluation (`src/07_generate.py`, `src/10_eval_gen.py`, `src/11_gen_analysis.py`, `results/gen_eval.json`, `results/gen_summary.txt`)
Config: MusicGen-Melody 1.5B fp16, guidance 3.0, 30 s, sampling. 12 validation clips of dataset A (2 per decade, first in manifest order), conditioning chroma = original mix, generated audio replaces the accompaniment stem; 4 prompts (neutral "a song recording"; 1960s, 1980s, 2010s prompts in `07_generate.py`) x 2 seeds = 96 generations. Era probe: logistic regression (C=0.01, standardized) trained on dataset-A TRAIN accompaniment stems (acc_norm), MERT-v2-30s layer 12 and MuQ layer 9, applied to loudness-normalized generated accompaniment. Statistic = expected decade index (0 = 1960s .. 5 = 2010s); unit = song (seeds averaged), Wilcoxon signed-rank across 12 songs, no multiplicity correction. Melody preservation = chroma-CQT cosine between original mix and generated remix (frame-aligned and DTW), with a control that pairs each remix with another song's original mix.
Fix on the way: loading MuQ before running MERT in one process made MERT hidden states NaN (importing the `muq` package changes MERT outputs; cause not investigated); the script now embeds with MERT first and imports MuQ afterwards.

Results (12 songs):
- Probe sanity on original accompaniment stems: expected decade vs true decade spearman 0.74 (MERT) and 0.83 (MuQ).
- Generated audio sits at a late decade whatever the prompt: mean expected decade neutral 3.89 (MERT) / 3.68 (MuQ) vs 2.3 for the originals. Interpretation: the probe treats MusicGen output (32 kHz, clean, synthetic) as recent, so the probe is shifted on this domain; prompts must be compared against the neutral prompt, not against true decades.
- MERT probe, expected decade by prompt: 1960s 4.08, 1980s 4.31, 2010s 4.59. 2010s minus neutral +0.70 (11/12 songs, p=0.001); 2010s minus 1960s +0.51 (10/12, p=0.005); 1980s minus 1960s +0.23 (p=0.052); 1960s minus neutral +0.19 (p=0.52). Probability mass on the target decade: 2010s prompt 0.84 (neutral prompt 0.52), 1980s prompt 0.19 (neutral 0.16), 1960s prompt 0.006 (neutral 0.04). Top-1 equals the prompt decade for 96% (2010s), 12% (1980s), 0% (1960s).
- MuQ probe: expected decade 1960s 3.97, 1980s 3.58, 2010s 3.89, neutral 3.68; 1980s minus 1960s -0.39 (1/12, p=0.0015, opposite to the prompt order), 2010s minus 1960s -0.08 (p=0.79), 2010s minus 1980s +0.31 (p=0.034). Mass on the 1980s decade with the 1980s prompt 0.33 vs 0.15 neutral; 2010s prompt 0.43 vs 0.39 neutral.
- DSP-only baselines applied to the original accompaniment (old: band-limit 60 Hz to 7 kHz, tape-style soft clipping, hiss; new: soft limiter and high shelf): MERT probe does not move (old 2.19, new 2.33, identity 2.32, p=0.30); MuQ probe moves strongly (old 1.80, new 2.42, 12/12 songs, p=0.0005). Reading: MuQ's decade probe is sensitive to simple production changes, MERT's is not; so an "era" shift seen with MuQ cannot be separated from production character, and a shift seen with MERT is at least not explained by those two filters.
- Melody/harmony: own remix beats the other-song control in 91/96 clips (frame-aligned cosine +0.107, p=2e-16) and 85/96 (DTW +0.048, p=2e-14). Absolute values: frame-aligned 0.78 (neutral) to 0.85 (1980s), DTW 0.85 to 0.91; the control is already 0.63 to 0.77 (0.78 to 0.88 DTW), so chroma similarity has a high floor on this data and the effect is moderate in size. Not a transcription-level melody metric.
Reading (interpretation, small n): the prompted regeneration keeps the harmony/melody contour above a control and moves the probe toward "modern" for a 2010s prompt (MERT), but it does not produce clear 1960s or 1980s presence by either probe, and the two encoders disagree about the ordering. This is weak evidence of controllable era style in off-the-shelf MusicGen-Melody and strong evidence that probe-based era evaluation is domain-shifted on generated audio.
Not done: listening test; real target-era versions as references (needs Discogs-VI cliques); a probe retrained on generated audio; vocal-pitch RPA (vocals are untouched by construction).

## 2026-10-25 metrics survey (survey2) synthesized
Two deep-research replies merged in `SURVEY_metrics.md` (reliability notes, decided metric suite, validation controls with acceptance criteria, statistics, implementation order). Main decisions: relative codec-matched era scores instead of raw probe output; song-identification rank instead of raw chroma similarity; KAD instead of FAD at this n; Audiobox Aesthetics per item for quality; COCOLA for vocal-accompaniment fit; QWK, MAE and log-loss plus MDL/selectivity for the analysis half; 24 songs instead of 12. No experiment run yet for this item.

## 2026-10-25 content-preservation metrics on the 96 generations (`src/13_content_metrics.py`, `src/14_content_analysis.py`, `results/content_metrics.json`, `results/content_summary.txt`)
Config (per SURVEY_metrics.md): chroma-CQT (hop 1024) of the signal high-passed at 261 Hz (middle C); generated REMIX vs the 12 ORIGINAL mixes of the dataset-A clips used (2 per decade); similarity = 1 - mean cosine cost along the DTW path. Chord labels: 24 major/minor template classes per frame from the same chroma; agreement along the DTW path; rotation floor = mean agreement after rotating the generated labels by 1..11 semitones. Tempo: librosa tempo, octave-folded absolute difference in BPM.
- Song identification (the generated remix's own original ranked first among 12 originals): top-1 0.865 over all 96 generations (chance 0.083; binomial p=3.5e-75), MRR 0.926 (chance 0.259). By prompt: neutral 0.75, 1960s 0.92, 1980s 0.92, 2010s 0.875 (24 generations each). Era prompts vs neutral, reciprocal rank per song: 1960s +0.103 (p=0.11), 1980s +0.103 (p=0.20), 2010s +0.075 (p=0.47), n=12 songs, not significant; the sign (era prompts are no worse than the neutral prompt) is the only reading.
- Chord agreement along the DTW path: 0.301 overall versus 0.024 for the rotation floor (positive lift in 96/96, p=1.8e-17); era prompts vs neutral differ by -0.02 to -0.004 (p>=0.23).
- Folded delta-BPM: mean 14.1 BPM overall; 1980s prompt +3.7 BPM versus neutral (8/12 songs, p=0.059), other prompts about 0.
Reading (interpretation): unlike raw chroma cosine (floor 0.63 to 0.88), the identification score has a clean floor (1/12) and the generated covers sit far above it, so the MusicGen-Melody conditioning does preserve which song it is, for all prompts equally. Chord and tempo preservation are moderate (tempo is not locked by the chroma condition). The prompts do not trade content for style within the resolution of 12 songs. Limits: 12 originals is a small candidate pool (the harder test needs more songs and same-key distractors), template chord labels are crude, and the reference for the remix is the original mix (which includes the kept vocal).

## 2026-10-25 codec-matched era probes (`src/12_codec_resynth.py`, `src/run_codec.sh`, `src/10_eval_gen.py --train_cond acccodec_norm`, `results/gen_eval_codec.json`, `results/gen_summary_codec.txt`, `results/probe_codec_{mert,muq}_A.json`)
Config: real dataset-A accompaniment stems (all 1290 clips) passed through the EnCodec 32 kHz codec of MusicGen (facebook/encodec_32khz, bandwidth 2.2 kbps = 4 codebooks), resampled to 24 kHz, loudness-normalized, embedded (MERT-v2-30s, MuQ) and used to train the decade probes (same protocol: layer and C by CV on train, validation scored once). The generated accompaniment is scored with these probes. Everything else as in the earlier generation evaluation (12 songs, 96 generations, neutral/1960s/1980s/2010s prompts x 2 seeds; unit = song; Wilcoxon over 12 songs, no multiplicity correction). New condition `identity_codec` = original accompaniment through the codec.
Sanity: the codec does not hurt the real-audio decade probe: validation accuracy acc_norm vs acccodec_norm 0.432 vs 0.424 (MERT, layer 12 vs 18) and 0.424 vs 0.432 (MuQ, layer 9 both). Probe ordering on the original stems versus true decade: spearman 0.82 (identity) and 0.82 (identity_codec) for MERT, 0.81 and 0.85 for MuQ, so the positive control a1 of SURVEY_metrics.md passes (criterion >= 0.5).
Incident: the first MERT embedding run for acccodec_norm saved all-NaN features (cause not found: the same code gave finite output on 2 clips and on a full rerun); embedding and probe scripts now assert finiteness.
Results (expected decade index, 0 = 1960s .. 5 = 2010s, mean over 12 songs):
- The "everything looks 2000s" offset shrinks: neutral prompt 3.89 -> 2.81 (MERT), 3.68 -> 3.43 (MuQ); originals 2.57 / 2.48.
- Prompts, MERT / MuQ: 1960s 3.06 / 3.43, 1980s 2.28 / 2.62, 2010s 3.21 / 3.56. 1980s minus 1960s -0.79 / -0.81 (0/12 songs higher, p=0.0005 both); 2010s minus 1980s +0.93 / +0.94 (12/12, p=0.0005 both). Both encoders now agree in sign on these contrasts (before: MuQ gave the reverse ordering).
- 1980s prompt: top-1 predicted decade = 1980s in 96% (MERT) and 79% (MuQ) of generations; probability mass on the 1980s 0.825 vs 0.498 (neutral) for MERT, 0.571 vs 0.089 for MuQ.
- 1960s prompt: no 1960s presence (top-1 0%, mass 0.001 MERT / 0.094 MuQ); 1960s minus neutral +0.25 / 0.00 (p=0.20 / 0.97). 2010s prompt: 2010s minus neutral +0.40 (p=0.043, 10/12) MERT, +0.13 (p=0.42) MuQ; top-1 2010s only 17-21%.
- DSP baselines on the original accompaniment (old vs new filter): MERT +0.23 (p=0.15), MuQ +0.62 (12/12, p=0.0005), unchanged from before: the MuQ probe still responds to simple production changes, so a MuQ shift is not separable from production character.
Reading (interpretation, small n, no multiplicity correction): matching the codec removes most of the domain offset and makes the two encoders agree; the 1980s prompt is the clearest controllable era (a distinct, probe-recognized 1980s sound, consistent with strongly identifiable 1980s production such as gated reverb and synths), the 2010s prompt moves the probe modestly, and the 1960s prompt gives nothing detectable (MusicGen output is rarely recognized as 1960s by probes trained on real stems). Which of these reflect "style" versus production character is still open.
Still to do for the metric suite: relative Delta scores per song as the primary statistic with the other controls (negative controls, sensitivity curve, seed ICC), KAD with codec-matched references, tagger-based era score, Audiobox Aesthetics, COCOLA.

## 2026-10-25 era-metric validation (12 songs; `src/eraprobe.py`, `src/16_controls.py`, `src/17_metric_validation.py`, `results/controls_12.json`, `results/metric_validation_12.txt`)
Metric: Delta_X per song = P(decade X | prompt X) - P(decade X | neutral prompt) from the codec-matched probes (acccodec_norm, MERT-v2 layer 18, MuQ layer 9), seeds averaged, mean over the 12 songs with 95% bootstrap CI over songs (5000 resamples), exact Wilcoxon over songs. Controls from SURVEY_metrics.md section 4: seed ICC(1,1), sensitivity curve (crossfade original codec-resynthesized accompaniment to generated accompaniment, alpha 0 .25 .5 .75 1, both seeds), noise controls (12 white and 12 pink noise clips at -23 LUFS).
- Delta (MERT / MuQ): 1980s prompt +0.327 [+0.207, +0.453], 10/12 songs, p=0.0024 / +0.482 [+0.400, +0.572], 12/12, p=0.0005. 2010s prompt +0.082 [+0.024, +0.148], 9/12, p=0.027 / +0.051 [-0.046, +0.152], p=0.38. 1960s prompt -0.014 [-0.029, -0.002] (p=0.052) / +0.026 [-0.021, +0.077] (p=0.68). Only the 1980s prompt gives a positive Delta that both encoders agree on, outside zero in both CIs.
- Seed ICC of the expected decade over the 2 seeds of 48 (song, prompt) groups: 0.43 (MERT), 0.48 (MuQ); for P(1980s | 1980s prompt): -0.05 (MERT), 0.24 (MuQ). Below the proposed 0.5 criterion: more seeds per prompt are needed before the per-clip metric is used, although the mean Delta over songs is stable.
- Sensitivity (P(target decade) vs alpha, mean over songs): 1980s MERT 0.15, 0.09, 0.16, 0.35, 0.66 (per-song spearman +0.76) and MuQ 0.15, 0.12, 0.11, 0.23, 0.57 (+0.57): rises at high alpha, not monotone at the low end and below the proposed 0.8 criterion. 2010s: +0.18 / +0.16, 1960s: -0.06 / +0.08 (no dose response).
- Noise controls: white and pink noise are classified as 2000s (MERT 0.81 and 0.58) or 1990s (MuQ pink 0.69); no mass on the 1980s (MERT white 0.002, pink 0.001; MuQ white 0.002, pink 0.005). So the 1980s result is not a generic "synthetic or noisy audio" artifact of the probe.
Acceptance check (criteria from SURVEY_metrics.md section 4, proposed before running): (1) positive control passes (identity_codec spearman 0.82 / 0.85, accuracy unchanged); (2) negative control passes for the 1980s signal (noise does not produce 1980s); (3) sensitivity monotone >= 0.8: not met (0.76 / 0.57); (4) seed ICC >= 0.5: not met (0.43 / 0.48) -> use more seeds; (5) encoder agreement in sign: met for 1980s, partially for 2010s, not for 1960s. Verdict (interpretation): usable as a relative, 12-song, group-level metric for the 1980s prompt; weak or absent for 2010s and 1960s; not usable per clip.
Next: wait for the 24-song generation (tmux era_gen2, flag gen24.done), rerun gen_eval_codec / controls on 24 songs with 3 seeds, then KAD, Audiobox Aesthetics, COCOLA.

## 2026-10-26 24-song replication (same pipeline; `results/gen_eval_codec_24.json`, `controls_24.json`, `content_metrics_24.json`, summaries `*_24.txt`, `metric_validation_24.txt`)
Generation finished for 24 validation clips of dataset A (4 per decade, first in manifest order; the earlier 12 are a subset), 4 prompts x seeds 0 and 1 = 192 generations; a third seed for all 24 songs is generating (tmux era_gen4/era_gen5, flags gen24s2a.done and gen24s2b.done) to raise the seed ICC. Statistics as before: unit = song, exact Wilcoxon, bootstrap CI over songs, no multiplicity correction. Summary scripts had hard-coded "/12" denominators in printed counts and the identification chance level (1/12); fixed to use the number of songs (the identification pool is now 24 originals: chance top-1 0.042, MRR 0.157).
- Delta_X (MERT / MuQ), 24 songs: 1980s +0.318 [+0.228, +0.405], 21/24, p<0.0001 / +0.412 [+0.357, +0.471], 24/24, p<0.0001. 2010s +0.035 [-0.012, +0.078], 16/24, p=0.049 / +0.069 [-0.006, +0.146], p=0.10 (CI includes 0 in both). 1960s -0.009 [-0.018, -0.003] / +0.008 [-0.018, +0.036] (no positive Delta). The 12-song result for the 1980s replicates with a tighter CI; the 2010s effect shrank (12 songs: +0.082 / +0.051).
- Expected decade (MERT / MuQ): neutral 2.85 / 3.43, 1960s 3.08 / 3.53, 1980s 2.36 / 2.78, 2010s 3.17 / 3.64; 1980s minus 1960s -0.72 / -0.75 (1/24 and 0/24 songs higher, p<0.0001); 2010s minus 1980s +0.81 / +0.86 (24/24, p<0.0001); 2010s minus neutral +0.32 (p=0.003) / +0.21 (p=0.084). 1980s prompt top-1 = 1980s in 94% (MERT) and 71% (MuQ) of generations; 1960s 0%; 2010s 2% / 33%. Probe sanity on original stems: identity vs true decade spearman 0.54 / 0.76, identity_codec 0.71 / 0.87.
- Controls: seed ICC of the expected decade 0.31 (MERT) and 0.48 (MuQ) with 2 seeds (target 0.5, not met); sensitivity for the 1980s prompt rises with alpha (P(1980s): MERT 0.23, 0.26, 0.35, 0.57, 0.73, per-song spearman +0.78; MuQ 0.14, 0.12, 0.14, 0.26, 0.52, +0.63); 2010s +0.18 / +0.21 and 1960s -0.05 / +0.03 (no dose response). Noise controls: white noise predicted as 2000s by both probes; pink noise as 2010s (9/12) and 2000s by MERT, 1990s by MuQ; no 1980s mass from noise.
- DSP baseline old vs new (MuQ): +0.65, 23/24 songs, p<0.0001; MERT +0.17, 17/24, p=0.0096. MuQ remains sensitive to production-only changes; MERT less so, but now measurably.
- Content (24 originals pool): song identification top-1 0.885 (chance 0.042), MRR 0.931 (0.157); by prompt neutral 0.854, 1960s 0.917, 1980s 0.896, 2010s 0.875; chord agreement 0.334 vs rotation floor 0.022 (192/192 above); era prompts vs neutral not significantly different on identification rank, tempo (the 2010s prompt slightly lowers chord agreement, -0.020, p=0.042).
Reading (interpretation): with 24 songs the only robust controllable era is the 1980s (both encoders, CI excluding 0, dose response with crossfade, noise does not mimic it); 2010s is marginal and encoder-dependent; 1960s is absent. Content preservation does not depend on the prompt era within the resolution of this test.

## 2026-10-26 quality (Audiobox Aesthetics) and distribution (KAD-style) metrics on the 24 songs, seeds 0 and 1
- Audiobox Aesthetics (`src/18_quality.py`, `src/19_quality_analysis.py`, `results/quality_24.json`, `results/quality_summary_24.txt`; package audiobox_aesthetics 0.0.4 in an isolated venv `~/era_market/venv2`, per-clip, no reference; axes PQ production quality, PC production complexity, CE content enjoyment, CU content usefulness, 0-10; accompaniment stems only). Mean over 24 songs (seeds averaged): original accompaniment PQ 7.43 / CE 7.36; codec-resynthesized original 7.44 / 7.43 (codec alone does not change the score, p=0.90); crude DSP old/new filters PQ 6.60 / 6.54; generated, neutral prompt PQ 7.13 / CE 7.03; 1960s 7.17 / 7.25; 1980s 7.67 / 6.96; 2010s 7.78 / 6.96; white noise PQ 4.97. Paired Wilcoxon over songs: 1980s vs neutral PQ +0.54 (21/24, p<0.001), 2010s vs neutral +0.65 (24/24, p<0.001), 1960s +0.03 (p=0.90); CE 1960s +0.21 (p=0.037), 1980s and 2010s -0.07 (n.s.); neutral generation vs original PQ -0.30 (p=0.012), CE -0.33 (p=0.021). Reading (interpretation): the metric separates noise and the crude filters from music and is insensitive to the codec; era prompts with concrete production vocabulary yield higher predicted production quality than the neutral prompt, so a quality gain is not a cost of style control here; the era prompts are not worse in enjoyment.
- KAD-style margin (own implementation, `src/20_kad.py`, `results/kad_24.json`; unbiased MMD^2 with an RBF kernel, bandwidth by the median heuristic on a 600-sample pool of standardized embeddings; references = codec-resynthesized real TRAIN accompaniment per decade, MERT layer 18 and MuQ layer 9 as in the codec probes; this is the KAD estimator in spirit, not the kadtk package). Own-era margin = mean MMD^2 to the other decades minus MMD^2 to the target decade; reported minus the neutral-prompt margin, 95% CI from 200 bootstrap resamples of songs on the generated side (references fixed; neutral and prompt resampled independently, so CIs are rough). MERT / MuQ: 1980s +0.0377 [+0.0337, +0.0410] / +0.0252 [+0.0231, +0.0275]; 2010s +0.0181 [+0.0141, +0.0228] / +0.0063 [+0.0030, +0.0089]; 1960s -0.0182 [-0.0300, -0.0067] / -0.0117 [-0.0182, -0.0042]. Baselines (margins): DSP old/new filters give no 1980s margin (about -0.005 MERT, +0.004 to +0.007 MuQ). Reading: the distribution metric agrees with the probes on the 1980s (strong), adds modest evidence for the 2010s (positive, CI excludes 0 in both encoders, whereas the probe Delta CI included 0), and says the 1960s prompt moves generated audio away from real 1960s accompaniment relative to the neutral prompt. The margin has no per-clip validation yet (seed ICC and dose-response not computed for it).
Still open in the metric plan: COCOLA vocal-accompaniment fit (installation not tried yet), seed-2 rerun of everything (generation in progress), the Discogs-style tagger era score, analysis-half metrics (QWK, MAE-decade, log-loss, MDL, selectivity).

## 2026-10-26 three-seed rerun (24 songs x 4 prompts x seeds 0, 1, 2 = 288 generations; `*_24s3.*` files in `results/`, `metric_validation_24s3.txt`)
Same codec-matched probes, controls and statistics as above; only the seed count changed (seed 2 added for all 24 songs; the content-metrics script is hard-coded to seeds 0 and 1 and was not rerun).
- Delta_X (MERT / MuQ), mean over 24 songs [95% bootstrap CI], songs with Delta>0, exact Wilcoxon: 1980s +0.375 [+0.300, +0.446], 22/24, p<0.0001 / +0.414 [+0.363, +0.469], 24/24, p<0.0001; **2010s +0.106 [+0.051, +0.158], 21/24, p=0.0004 / +0.094 [+0.032, +0.155], 18/24, p=0.007**; 1960s -0.009 [-0.014, -0.005] (5/24) / +0.006 [-0.018, +0.030], p=0.75. With a third seed the 2010s prompt becomes a significant positive Delta in both encoders (with two seeds the CI included 0); 1980s stays strongest; 1960s absent.
- Seed ICC(1,1) of the expected decade with 3 seeds: 0.46 (MERT), 0.46 (MuQ), single-clip reliability still below 0.5; the Spearman-Brown reliability of the mean of 3 seeds is about 0.72 (computed from the ICC), which is the quantity the song-level statistics use. P(1980s | 1980s prompt) single-seed ICC 0.06 / 0.11 (a per-clip probability is noisy; only song-level means are interpretable).
- Sensitivity (crossfade, P(target decade) vs alpha): 1980s MERT 0.16, 0.26, 0.30, 0.47, 0.70 (per-song spearman +0.82, meets the 0.8 criterion), MuQ +0.63 (below); 2010s +0.16 / +0.23; 1960s -0.02 / +0.01.
- KAD-style margin minus neutral (MERT / MuQ): 1980s +0.0292 [+0.0264, +0.0320] / +0.0250 [+0.0229, +0.0273]; 2010s +0.0157 [+0.0123, +0.0193] / +0.0057 [+0.0023, +0.0084]; 1960s -0.0163 [-0.0249, -0.0087] / -0.0109 [-0.0181, -0.0035]. Same signs as with two seeds.
- Audiobox PQ vs neutral prompt: 1980s +0.60 (21/24), 2010s +0.75 (23/24), 1960s +0.07 (p=0.53).
Acceptance check update (SURVEY_metrics.md criteria): positive control (spearman 0.71 / 0.87 on the codec originals) passes; noise control passes for 1980s (no 1980s mass); sensitivity monotone >= 0.8 passes for MERT 1980s only; seed ICC >= 0.5 not met for single clips but met for the mean of three seeds; encoder agreement in sign: 1980s and 2010s agree in sign and significance, 1960s agrees in having no positive effect.
Reading (interpretation): in this setup MusicGen-Melody follows a 1980s prompt (strong) and a 2010s prompt (moderate) in the sense measured by two probes and a distribution margin, and does not follow a 1960s prompt; content preservation (song identification, chords) is unaffected by the prompt era. Open: whether the 1980s and 2010s effects are style or just production character (MuQ and the DSP controls say production moves the metric), COCOLA fit, the Discogs-style tagger era score, and listening checks.

## 2026-10-26 guidance-scale sweep (`src/07_generate.py` with `GENDIR`/`--guidance`, `src/run_cfg_eval.sh`, `src/21_cfg_analysis.py`, `results/cfg{1,3,5,7}_{eval,content}.json`, `results/cfg_summary.txt`)
Config: same 24 validation songs of dataset A and 4 prompts as the main generation, seed 0 only (96 clips per scale), MusicGen-Melody 1.5B fp16, guidance scale g in {1, 3, 5, 7} (g=3 = the main run's seed-0 clips). Scoring as in the codec-matched evaluation: MERT L18 / MuQ L9 probes trained on EnCodec-resynthesized accompaniment; Delta = P(target decade | prompt) - P(target decade | neutral prompt) per song, Wilcoxon over 24 songs, no multiplicity correction. Content = song-identification top-1 / MRR and chord agreement of remix versus original mix. Audiobox, KAD and the three-seed ICC were not rerun for the sweep.
Incident: the first g=5 MERT embedding run saved all-NaN features (the intermittent issue seen before; finiteness is asserted/reported in the log); it was rerun and is finite. The reported g=5 numbers are from the rerun.
Results (mean Delta; songs with Delta>0 of 24):
- 1980s, MERT: g=1 +0.203 (17), g=3 +0.374 (21), g=5 +0.346 (21), g=7 +0.369 (23). MuQ: +0.096 (15), +0.442 (24), +0.353 (23), +0.407 (24).
- 2010s, MERT: +0.086, +0.072, +0.135, +0.152 (g=1..7); MuQ: +0.145, +0.115, +0.137, +0.199. All 2010s p<0.01 (g=1..7, both encoders); per-test p in `cfg_summary.txt`.
- 1960s: MERT -0.031, -0.019, -0.018, +0.008 (no scale gives a detectable 1960s presence); MuQ -0.058, +0.001, +0.040, +0.072 (p=0.0016 only at g=7).
- Content preservation: song-identification top-1 0.562 (g=1), 0.906 (g=3), 0.927 (g=5), 0.906 (g=7); MRR 0.693 / 0.943 / 0.957 / 0.948; chord agreement 0.283 / 0.335 / 0.341 / 0.337 (rotation floor 0.02-0.03); |dBPM| 17.3 / 12.0 / 14.2 / 14.3.
Reading (interpretation, one seed, 24 songs): g=1 weakens both the era effect (1980s Delta roughly halves under MERT, falls to 0.10 under MuQ) and the content preservation (top-1 0.56); from g=3 up the 1980s effect is on a plateau and content preservation does not fall, so the default g=3 is not obviously suboptimal, and a larger g mainly raised the 2010s Delta (MERT +0.07 -> +0.15, MuQ +0.12 -> +0.20). Differences between g=3, 5, 7 are within what single-seed noise could produce (seed ICC single-clip 0.46) and were not tested.

## 2026-10-26 BUG FOUND: MERT embeddings were not reproducible across processes (`src/mert_util.py`, `src/diag_mert_orig_loader.py`, `src/diag_mert_fixed.py`, `src/diag_muq.py`)
What: under transformers 5 the remote-code MERT-v2-30s model leaves the non-persistent buffer `embed_positions.inv_freq` (rotary frequencies) uninitialized (values such as 0, 2e-43 observed; sometimes NaN/inf). Consequences: (1) every process that loaded MERT used different random rotary frequencies; (2) the earlier "intermittent all-NaN MERT embeddings" (acccodec_norm, cfg5, fx runs) were this bug, not an import-order effect; (3) in the diagnostic, two loads of the same clip gave cosine 0.9855 (max abs layer-18 difference 0.47-0.77) versus cosine 0.868 between two different clips, params identical, a second forward pass in one load identical.
Scope: every MERT number in this log before this entry (stem probes A/B, extra probes, codec probes, all generation evaluations, controls, KAD, bleed analysis, LWS/analysis-half metrics run on those embeddings) was computed with an uncontrolled per-process rotary buffer. Within one embedding run (one `02_embed.py` call) the buffer was constant, so within-condition probe training/validation was internally consistent (the probe results were stable and sensible), but generated-audio embeddings (`10_eval_gen.py`, another process) were NOT in the same feature space as the probes trained on `emb/` files. MuQ was checked (`diag_muq.py`, 3 loads, max abs diff 0.0, no non-persistent buffers): MuQ numbers are unaffected, and the MuQ-based results (e.g. 1980s Delta +0.41, 2010s +0.09) are the unaffected evidence for the generation findings.
Fix: `mert_util.load_mert()` recomputes `inv_freq` exactly as `RotaryEmbedding.__init__` does and asserts finiteness; `02_embed.py`, `10_eval_gen.py`, `eraprobe.py` use it; after the fix two loads agree exactly (cosine 1.0000, max diff 0.0). Old MERT embeddings were moved to `emb_buggy_mert/` on the server; all MERT embeddings, probes and downstream evaluations are being recomputed and will supersede the earlier MERT numbers (the earlier MERT numbers in this log stay as written for the record and are marked superseded as each is replaced).

MuQ numbers are identical to before (e.g. probe_muq_A mix/voc/acc 0.477/0.318/0.424), which also confirms the bug was MERT-only. The earlier MERT-based numbers in this log are SUPERSEDED by this entry. Probe protocol unchanged (layer and C by 5-fold CV on train, validation scored once).

### 3. Guidance-scale sweep (seed 0, `results/cfg_summary.txt`)
MERT 1980s Delta g=1/3/5/7: +0.216/+0.615/+0.586/+0.562; 2010s: +0.002/+0.354/+0.494/+0.455; 1960s: -0.004/-0.034/+0.060/+0.100. ### 4. Production-only control (hand-built pedalboard chains on the REAL accompaniment, no generation; `results/fx_eval.json`, `fx_content_summary.txt`, `src/23_derive.py fx`)
Chains are our own approximations of era production (not validated): 1960s band-limit + drive + small reverb; 1980s compression + high shelf + chorus + large reverb; 2010s low shelf + heavy compression + limiter. Delta vs unprocessed, MERT: 1960s +0.053 (21/24), 1980s -0.036 (5/24), 2010s +0.273 (24/24); MuQ: 1960s +0.045, 1980s +0.002 (p=0.66), 2010s +0.110. Reading: a generic compression/brightness chain reproduces most of the 2010s Delta (+0.27 vs +0.33 for MusicGen, MERT; +0.11 vs +0.09 MuQ), so the 2010s effect is largely production character; the 1980s Delta (+0.59 MERT) is NOT reproduced by the chain (-0.04), i.e. the generator changes more than this production chain for 1980s.

### 5. Second generator, MuseControlLite (Stable Audio Open 1.0 + melody_stereo adapter; melody from the original mix, same prompts, 3 seeds; `results/mcl_*`) and text-only MusicGen baseline (no melody condition; `results/textonly_*`); `results/generators_summary.txt`
- MuseControlLite Delta, MERT: 1960s +0.015, 1980s +0.014 (p=0.14), 2010s +0.171 (23/24, p<1e-4); MuQ: 1960s +0.071, 1980s +0.052, 2010s +0.069 (all p<0.001). CLAP zero-shot Delta: 1960s +0.194, 1980s 0.000, 2010s +0.010. Content: song-id top-1 1.000, chord agreement 0.372, |dBPM| 25.7. COCOLA margin 0.4-0.5 (original +6.0). Reading: the era prompts (written for MusicGen) have a much smaller effect on this generator under all three evaluators while it follows the melody more strictly.
- MusicGen text-only: Delta MERT 1960s +0.244, 1980s +0.369, 2010s -0.117 (0/24); MuQ 1960s +0.073, 1980s +0.628, 2010s -0.144 (0/24); CLAP 1960s -0.492, 1980s +0.273, 2010s +0.631. Content with the original vocal stem kept: song-id top-1 0.188 (chance 0.042: this is what the vocal stem alone carries), chord agreement 0.205 (floor 0.030), versus 0.892 / 0.336 with the melody condition. Reading: the melody condition is what preserves the song (top-1 0.19 -> 0.89); the era Delta of the text-only model has a different pattern (2010s prompt scored LESS 2010s than the neutral prompt), so the era effect depends on the conditioning, and prompt-vs-neutral Delta is relative to each system's own neutral output.

### 6. Independent zero-shot evaluator and vocal-accompaniment coherence (no MERT involved)
- CLAP (laion/clap-htsat-unfused, text prompts of the decades; `results/clap_era.json`, `clap_summary.txt`): positive control on 132 real validation clips is weak (zero-shot top-1 0.242 accompaniment / 0.235 mix, chance 0.167; Spearman with true decade 0.30/0.32; predictions concentrate on 1960s/1970s), so CLAP is a weak independent era evaluator. MusicGen prompt Delta under CLAP: 1980s +0.109 (22/24), 2010s +0.507 (24/24), 1960s -0.098 (6/24). CLAP is largely sensitive to the prompt words themselves (text-audio alignment), so agreement with the probes is supportive but not independent evidence of era realism.
- COCOLA (COCOLA_HP_v1, original vocal vs accompaniment, 5 s windows, `results/cocola.json`, `cocola_summary.txt`; first version had an in-place masking bug, fixed by cloning the features): matched-minus-mismatched margin (both-mode) original accompaniment +6.12 (22/24 songs > 0), codec-resynthesized original +6.00, generated: neutral +2.13, 1960s +2.69, 1980s +2.22, 2010s +2.32; era prompts do not change it (Wilcoxon p>0.3 for each vs neutral). Reading: generated accompaniment fits the original vocal about one third as well as the original (margin 2.1-2.7 vs 6.0), independent of the era prompt.
- Audiobox Aesthetics and KAD-style margin (earlier entries) are unchanged except the MERT KAD numbers above.

## 2026-10-27: probe-architecture ablation (`src/45_probe_models.py`, `results/probe_models_{A,B}.json`)
Same stored mean-pooled embeddings; layer chosen by 3-fold CV on train; val accuracy (A n=132, B n=102; one run each, so differences of a few points are within noise). 
- Dataset A decade: single-layer LR mix .523 / vocals .402 / accompaniment .485 (MERT); top-3-layer concat mix .545, vocals .402, accompaniment .477; RBF-SVM and MLP lower (.33-.49). Stem fusion (concat voc+acc) .462, (mix+voc+acc) .477, late average .455. MERT+MuQ concat on mix .515. All four stems of both encoders concatenated .455.
- Dataset B market: LR mix .618 / vocals .569 / accompaniment .431; concat top-3 vocals .618; SVM mix .637; stem fusion mix+voc+acc .647; MERT+MuQ concat on mix .647; everything concatenated .608.
- Reading (interpretation): none of the richer probes (MLP, SVM, layer concat, stem or encoder fusion) gives a clear gain over the single-layer linear probe; the stem ordering (A: accompaniment about equal to mix; B: vocals above accompaniment) is unchanged. The layer picked by 3-fold CV differs from the 5-fold choice used earlier (mix .523 vs .477), a reminder that single-split differences of this size are noise.
- Server cleanup: removed superseded buggy-embedding folders, temp dirs and pip cache (free disk 94 -> 119 GB); kept stems, embeddings, venvs and generation outputs (all backed up on the restricted Drive except `gen_lvl_*`).

## 2026-10-27 encoder comparison (src/46_embed_more.py, 47_probe_encoders.py; results/probe_encoders_{A,B}.json)
Protocol as the probe ablation: per (encoder, condition) layer by 3-fold CV on TRAIN, linear probe C=0.01, val acc with 2000-resample bootstrap 95% CI. A n=132 (chance .167), B n=102 (chance .167). Encoders: MERT-v2-30s, MuQ-large, Whisper-medium encoder, AST-AudioSet, CLAP-htsat-fused (MERT-v1-330M and larger_clap_music not used: pickle-only weights).
Val acc mix/voc/acc. A: MERT .523/.402/.485; MuQ .477/.318/.409; Whisper .242/.326/.318; AST .455/.348/.402; CLAP .394/.303/.371. B: MERT .618/.569/.431; MuQ .471/.471/.392; Whisper .500/.520/.304; AST .363/.333/.343; CLAP .333/.324/.294.
Interpretation: MERT is best or tied-best on every condition; Whisper is competitive only on B vocals/mix (voice/language cue), weak on A. Stem ordering (A: mix>acc>voc; B: mix>=voc>acc) holds for MERT; CIs overlap widely.

## 2026-10-27 LoRA fine-tune of MusicGen-Melody on era accompaniment (src/48_lora_musicgen.py; adapters ~/era_market/lora/v1; results/lora_eval.json, lora_content.json)
Config: dataset-A TRAIN stems (1026 clips), text = clip's own decade prompt (10% neutral dropout), chroma from original mix, 10 s crops, LoRA r=16 on decoder q/k/v/out_proj (9.4M trainable), 3000 steps, bs 8, lr 1e-4, model kept in eval mode. Generation: 24 val songs x {neutral,1960s,1980s,2010s} x seeds 0-2, guidance 3, merged adapters. Scoring as base: codec-matched MERT L22 / MuQ L9 probes.
Delta P(target decade) vs neutral prompt, mean over 24 songs [songs >0], Wilcoxon p. Seed 0 only, MERT / MuQ:
- base: 1960s -.034 [13] p=.53 / +.001 [15] p=.58; 1980s +.615 [24] / +.442 [24]; 2010s +.354 [23] / +.115 [19] (p=.008)
- LoRA: 1960s +.422 [20] p<.001 / +.297 [21]; 1980s +.250 [20] / +.170 [19]; 2010s +.186 [18] / +.134 [19]
LoRA 3 seeds: 1960s +.396 / +.296; 1980s +.246 / +.220; 2010s +.171 / +.102 (all p<.0001).
Content (remix vs original): song-id top-1 base .906 (seed 0, n=96) vs LoRA .865 (seed 0, n=96) and .878 (3 seeds, n=288); chord agreement .335 vs .355 / .355. (13_content_metrics.py defaults to SEEDS=0,1; rerun with SEEDS=0,1,2.)
Interpretation: the 1960s prompt, ineffective in the base model, now shifts the probe; 1980s/2010s effects shrink (adapter is trained to all decades, so contrasts to neutral are more even). Energy of the effect is spread more evenly across decades rather than larger overall. One adapter, one run; no hyperparameter search.

## 2026-10-27 gap analyses against the TA description (src/49a_tags_real.py, 49b_gap_analyses.py; results/gap_{A,B}.txt/.json, gap_{A,B}_clips.csv)
Config: pooled labeled clips (train+val) A n=1158, B n=900; 5-fold stratified out-of-fold (OOF) logistic probes (C=0.01) on MERT-v2 at the per-stem layer chosen on TRAIN, mean of 3 fold seeds. NOT artist-grouped (manifest has no artist column), so absolute OOF accuracies may be optimistic; use for relative comparisons. Style proxy = Essentia Discogs-EffNet (400 Discogs style tags, 1280-d embedding) on the original mix, because the manifest has no genre column. Sung-language proxy = Whisper LID argmax on the vocal stem (labels such as nn/la/jw are likely non-speech/instrumental detections; not verified). Native language per market (US en, UK en, Brazil pt, Spain es, Germany de, Italy it) is our own assumption. Artist origin is not available in the data, so "market vs origin" cannot be tested directly; only language-vs-market.
OOF acc mix/voc/acc: A .507/.373/.490; B .591/.556/.417.
Song level: clips wrong under all three of mix, vocals, accompaniment: A 377/1158 (independent-errors expectation 182.7), B 237/900 (95.4); right under all three: A 259, B 247. Errors are therefore strongly shared across stems. Hard vs easy: lower vocal share (A median .356 vs .405, p=.0006; B .349 vs .434, p=7e-9); B also lower loudness-like stat (-14.5 vs -13.1, p=.0004), A p=.08. Hard A clips are mostly Whisper "en" (317/377).
Decade confusions (mix OOF): errors concentrate on adjacent decades (A mean |decade err| .761, within-1 .826); biggest off-diagonals 2000s->2010s 61, 2010s->2000s 50, 2000s->1990s 35, 1990s->1980s 33, 1960s->1970s 33.
Genre/style proxy A: style-tags-only .455, EffNet embedding .513, MERT mix .507, MERT+tags .505 (concat-right-only 74 vs MERT-right-only 76). B: tags-only .370, EffNet .386, MERT .591, MERT+tags .572. Interpretation: on A, a genre/style tagger carries about as much decade information as MERT, so a large part of what a decade probe uses can be genre/style (confound not removed); on B the tagger is far below MERT. Over-represented styles per decade/market are in gap_*.txt (e.g. A 1960s Rock & Roll/Doo Wop, 1970s Funk/Soul, 1980s Punk/Pop Rock; B Brazil MPB/Samba/Forro, UK Punk/Oi/Mod). Inside 6 EffNet-style clusters MERT accuracy varies 0.41-0.62 (A) and 0.33-0.86 (B).
Market vs language (B): native-language-sung clips n=533 mix/voc/acc acc .743/.709/.482; English-sung clips in Spain/Germany/Italy n=223 .278/.247/.269; other detected language n=144 .514/.465/.403. Per market, English-sung vs native-language mix acc: Spain n=49 .224 vs n=61 1.000; Germany n=95 .284 vs n=24 1.000; Italy n=67 .328 vs n=54 .963. US<->UK confusions 55 of 368 mix errors. Interpretation: market recognition on B is largely sung-language recognition; for English-sung clips the probe is near chance level (.167) to .33.
Limit: Whisper LID is a proxy and was not validated against ground truth language here.
