## 2026-10-27 survey 3 follow-ups: shuffle-and-remix and LEACE language erasure (src/50_shuffle_remix.py, 51_leace.py; results/shuffle_remix_{A,B}.json, leace_{A,B}.json; reasoning and verified citations in SURVEY3.md)
### Shuffle-and-remix (design after Hsieh et al. 2020, arXiv 2002.06817)
Config: validation clips only; vocal stem of clip i + accompaniment stem of clip j with a different label (3 random j per i, seed 0; HTDemucs stems), loudness-normalized to -23 LUFS, MERT-v2-30s, TRAIN mix_norm probe (A layer 12, B layer 18, C=0.01). A n=396 pairs (132 vocal clips), B n=306 (102). Probe val acc on real mixes A .477, B .588; on same-clip recombination A .492, B .598.
Dataset A (decade): predicted label = accompaniment-source label 34.8% [29.5, 39.9] vs = vocal-source label 16.4% [12.1, 21.0] (cluster bootstrap over vocal clips); mean P(accomp label) .299 vs P(vocal label) .165, difference -.134 [-.179, -.088]. Expected-decade regression: beta(accompaniment decade) .410 vs beta(vocal decade) .121, difference .288 [.213, .357]. Per vocal decade the accompaniment label is followed more than the vocal label for 1960s-1990s and 2010s; 2000s is the exception (vocal-label match .41 vs accompaniment .27, probe predicts 2000s often).
Dataset B (market): predicted label = vocal-source market 50.0% [42.2, 58.2] vs accompaniment-source 18.3% [13.1, 23.5]; mean P(vocal label) .422 vs P(accomp label) .182, difference +.240 [.158, .322]. Per vocal market vocal-follow / accomp-follow: Brazil .80/.04, Spain .69/.12, UK .49/.24, US .45/.22, Italy .33/.20, Germany .24/.29.
Interpretation: this is a causal-style intervention on the stems; decade follows the accompaniment, market follows the vocal stem, consistent with the correlational stem probes. Limitation: separator artifacts remain (HTDemucs), recombined clips mix stems that were never recorded together.
### LEACE erasure of sung language (Belrose et al. 2023, arXiv 2306.03819; library concept-erasure 0.2.4)
Language label = Whisper LID argmax on the vocal stem, grouped (B: en 431, pt 116, es 68, it 53, de 22, other 108 on TRAIN; A: en 877, es 37, other 112); a proxy, not ground truth. Eraser fitted on TRAIN, probes re-fitted on erased TRAIN, same layers/C as probe_<ds>.json, validation accuracy. Controls: 50 random orthogonal rank-5 (B) / rank-2 (A) projections; erasing the target label (sanity).
B market, val acc before -> after language erasure -> random same-rank erasure (mean [min,max]): mix .588 [.490,.686] -> .353 [.265,.451] -> .591 [.578,.608]; vocals .608 -> .402 [.304,.500] -> .606; accompaniment .422 -> .314 [.225,.412] -> .413. Language-group probe accuracy after erasure falls to the majority share .569 (before: .843 mix, .873 vocals, .676 accomp). Erasing the market label itself gives .167 (chance).
A decade: mix .477 -> .462; vocals .394 -> .394; accompaniment .485 -> .500 (random: .482/.397/.483). Language groups on A are 85% English, so the test is weak there.
Interpretation: on market, most of what a linear probe uses lies in a 5-dimensional subspace aligned with sung language (random erasure of the same rank costs nothing), and market accuracy stays above chance (.167) after erasure, so some non-linear or language-independent information remains. Caveat from the LEACE literature (reply, recalled, arXiv 2307.15054): erasing language also removes market information that is merely correlated with it, so this bounds language reliance from above, it does not prove the remainder is musical.
Shuffle-and-remix repeated with the second separator (BS-RoFormer stems, same 396/306 pairs, STEMS=stems_bsr; results/shuffle_remix_{A,B}_stems_bsr.json): A predicted label = accompaniment-source .348 vs vocal-source .172, mean P(vocal)-P(accomp) -.140 [-.185, -.095], accompaniment-minus-vocal decade beta .330 [.255, .398]; B vocal-source .500 vs accompaniment-source .180, mean difference +.235 [.153, .320]. Same conclusion as HTDemucs. (The field val_acc_same_clip_recomb in the BSR files is copied from the HTDemucs recomb_norm embeddings, not recomputed.)

## 2026-10-27 further controls from survey 3 (src/52_extra_controls.py; results/extra_controls_{A,B}.json; strata computed from results/gap_*_clips.csv)
Same MERT-v2 layers/C as probe_<ds>.json; A n_val=132, B n_val=102 (wide CIs).
### Dataset cartography on probe training dynamics (Swayamdipta et al. 2020 is "recalled" in the survey, not re-opened)
Linear softmax probe, 30 epochs, TRAIN mix_norm. Confidence = mean P(true) over epochs, variability = std. Regions (our thresholds): ambiguous = variability >= 67th pct; hard = confidence <= 33rd pct otherwise; else easy. A (n=1026): easy 429 / ambiguous 339 / hard 258; B (n=798): easy 403 / ambiguous 264 / hard 131. By class: A easy share 1960s 134/171, 1970s 102/171, 1980s 56/171, 1990s 29/171, 2000s 40/171, 2010s 68/171; B Brazil 119/133 easy, hard 0; Germany 29/133 easy, 30 hard, 74 ambiguous; US 32 hard, UK 38 hard. Spearman(confidence, number of stems wrong in the pooled OOF analysis) A -0.756, B -0.801 (p<1e-100; the same clips appear in both analyses).
### INLP removal of sung language (Ravfogel et al. 2020, "recalled")
Language proxy = Whisper LID group. 8 iterations, each removes the row space of a language classifier. B market val acc iteration 0 -> 8 (language-probe acc): mix .588 -> .451 (.843 -> .676), vocals .608 -> .520 (.873 -> .824), accompaniment .422 -> .382 (.676 -> .696); random same-rank projections (rank 40-41): mix .594, vocals .601, accompaniment .409. A decade: no change (.477 -> .485 mix, .394 -> .386 vocals, .485 -> .485 accompaniment; random .499/.386/.486). INLP removes about 41 dimensions and market accuracy falls less than with LEACE (rank 5): iterations leave language still decodable (.676 mix), so LEACE is the stronger test of linear language information.
### Cross-separator transfer (HTDemucs vs BS-RoFormer stems, same clips)
Val acc, train on HTDemucs embeddings: A vocals test HTD .394 / BSR .311; accompaniment .485 / .477. B vocals .608 / .500; accompaniment .422 / .353. Train on BSR: A vocals BSR .394 / HTD .265; accompaniment BSR .515 / HTD .477; B vocals BSR .559 / HTD .510; accompaniment BSR .392 / HTD .373. Interpretation: vocal-stem probes lose 0.08 to 0.13 accuracy when the separator changes, accompaniment probes less (0.01 to 0.07), so absolute stem accuracies are separator-dependent; the stem ordering (A mix > accompaniment > vocals; B mix >= vocals > accompaniment) held under each separator in earlier runs (probe_bsr_*).
### Vocal-share strata (OOF, pooled train+val, from gap_*_clips.csv; terciles of Demucs vocal share)
A low / mid / high vocal share (n=385/385/388): mix .460/.527/.534, vocals .317/.387/.415, accompaniment .434/.525/.510. B (n=299/299/302): mix .438/.666/.669, vocals .348/.632/.685, accompaniment .368/.455/.427.
### Market among English-sung clips only (OOF, B, Whisper LID argmax = en, n=489: UK 134, US 132, Germany 95, Italy 67, Spain 49, Brazil 12)
mix .397, vocals .358, accompaniment .348 (majority-class share .274, UK). Mix recall: US .53, UK .46, Germany .28, Italy .33, Spain .22; US<->UK<->Germany confusions dominate. Interpretation: with sung language held at English, market is still above the majority baseline but far below the all-language accuracy (.591), so a substantial part of B accuracy is language; some residual signal exists but is modest. Language is a proxy (Whisper LID), not ground truth.

## 2026-10-27 Discogs-VI metadata: what a "market" and a "year" label mean (src/53_discogs_vi_meta.py; results/discogs_vi_meta.txt)
Data: Discogs-VI 2024-07-01 metadata (Zenodo 13983028, CC BY-NC-SA 4.0), `Discogs-VI-20240701.jsonl`, streamed, no audio. Per version we use the first track: `country`, `released` (year), first track-artist id. Confirmed fields per track: country, released, release_genres, release_styles, labels, formats, release_id, master_id, track_artist_ids/names, track_writer ids. The artist file (`discogs_20240701_artists.xml.jsonl.clean`) holds only name, aliases, members: there is NO artist-origin field, so "artist origin" below is the proxy "artist's most frequent release country" (artists with >= 5 versions in this file; 101,603 versions qualify, 5% of all), which is not origin and is affected by reissues. These are statistics of the full Discogs-VI metadata, not of the PA1 subset (the PA1 clips carry no ids, so they cannot be joined).
- 1,911,611 versions, 348,796 cliques; missing country 0.8%; missing/invalid year 13.5%. Countries: US 580,258; UK 198,199; Germany 147,171; France 107,407; Europe 92,793; Italy 81,396; Brazil 57,502; Spain 54,997. Versions per release decade: 1950s 96,691; 1960s 223,208; 1970s 283,050; 1980s 225,106; 1990s 264,015; 2000s 282,581; 2010s 180,528; 2020s 41,738.
- Same composition, many labels: per clique, mean 2.42 distinct release countries (62.1% of cliques have >= 2, 24.0% have >= 3) and mean 1.99 distinct release decades (60.0% have >= 2, 21.7% have >= 3). So a country or decade label is a property of an edition, and the same composition is routinely labeled with several markets and decades.
- 1980s versions in the six dataset-B markets (126,385): 44.1% sit in a clique that also has a 1980s version in a different one of the six markets (most frequent pairs US-UK 17,913, US-Germany 13,731, UK-US 11,914, US-Italy 8,959); 88.0% have a clique member released outside the 1980s.
- Release country vs the artist's most frequent release country (proxy): differs for 29.0% of 101,603 versions. By release market (all years / 1980s only): US 18.7% / 24.8%; UK 29.5% / 33.2%; Brazil 10.4% / 18.4%; Spain 42.8% / 48.1%; Germany 32.3% / 33.8%; Italy 33.9% / 61.8% (n for 1980s: 1491, 1577, 223, 160, 730, 1054).
Interpretation: release-market labels in Discogs are edition-level and frequently differ from where the artist mostly releases; the audio of one composition can carry several markets or decades, so no waveform-only classifier can recover every label. This supports treating dataset-B accuracy as an association with release metadata, and supports the language result (Brazil, whose releases are the most consistent with the artist's modal country, is also the easiest market). The Italy 1980s figure (61.8%) is from a small proxy sample and should not be over-read.

## 2026-10-27 song-identity metric from a cover-song-identification model (src/54_vinet_identity.py; results/vinet_identity.json)
Model: Discogs-VINet (Araz et al., ISMIR 2024), ISMIR2024-branch checkpoint loaded with torch weights_only=True; third-party AGPLv3 code kept outside this repo (server `~/era_market/ext/Discogs-VINet`). Embedding: whole 30 s clip, CQT per the authors' inference code (22.05 kHz, hop 512, 84 bins, pad to 8000 frames, mean-downsample x20, scale by max), L2-normalized output. Per remix (original vocal + generated accompaniment): cosine to its own original mix, and retrieval of the own original among the 24 original mixes of the evaluation songs (chance top-1 .042). References: the real mix re-embedded cos 1.000 / top-1 1.00; original vocal + original accompaniment recombination cos .9996 / top-1 1.00. Whether PA1 clips were in the model's training data is not known.
Top-1 / mean cosine (neutral, 1960s, 1980s, 2010s), 24 songs:
- MusicGen-Melody g=3 (3 seeds, n=72 per prompt): .722/.646, .806/.648, .625/.640, .778/.652
- LoRA v1 (3000 steps): .722/.639, .722/.654, .556/.628, .639/.640
- LoRA v2 (1000 steps): .681/.648, .681/.643, .708/.647, .597/.625
- MuseControlLite (3 seeds): .778/.659, .764/.662, .778/.661, .778/.659
- guidance scale 1 / 5 / 7 (seed 0, n=24): neutral top-1 .458 / .708 / .667
- text-only MusicGen (no melody condition): neutral .208/.547, 1960s .139, 1980s .069, 2010s .333
- production-only parametric effects on the original accompaniment (gen_fx): top-1 1.00, cos .980 to 1.000
Paired change in cosine vs the neutral prompt (Wilcoxon over songs): base 1960s +.002 (p=.73), 1980s -.006 (p=.32), 2010s +.006 (p=.66); LoRA v1 +.015 (p=.21), -.011 (p=.28), +.001 (p=.97); LoRA v2 -.005 (p=.66), -.001 (p=.86), -.023 (p=.016); MuseControlLite all |delta| <= .003; text-only 1980s -.046 (p=.003). Extra prompts (1960s_b, 1960s_c, 1970s, 1990s, 2000s): top-1 .75, .65, .74, .69, .74 vs neutral .72.
Interpretation: the identity metric separates melody-conditioned generation (top-1 .56 to .81) from text-only generation (.07 to .33) and from production-only edits (1.00); era prompts leave mean cosine essentially unchanged in the base model and MuseControlLite (no significant change), with one small significant decrease for LoRA v2 on the 2010s prompt (p=.016, not corrected for 15 comparisons). Retrieval top-1 varies by 0.1 or more between prompts of the same song set (n=72, 3 seeds of 24 songs), so differences in top-1 between prompts are not tested here.

## 2026-10-28 third generator: ACE-Step 1.5 cover mode on the original accompaniment stem (src/55_ace_cover.py, 56_summarize_gens.py; results/ace_c{02,05,08,10}_{eval,content}.json, gens_summary_ace.txt, vinet_identity.json)
Model: ACE-Step 1.5 turbo DiT (`acestep-v15-turbo`, 8 steps, shift 3.0), code MIT, weights from the Hugging Face repo ACE-Step/Ace-Step1.5 (safetensors; silence_latent.pt loaded with weights_only=True; the repo page tags the weights license:mit, the GitHub README does not state a weights license), own venv `venv_ace` (torch 2.10.0+cu128 on driver 550). Protocol identical to 07_generate.py otherwise: task_type=cover, src_audio = original accompaniment stem (30 s), caption = the four era prompts of 27_musecontrol.py, instrumental, LM off, seeds 0,1,2, 24 songs; output 48 kHz -> 24 kHz, RMS-matched to the original accompaniment, remixed with the original vocal stem. audio_cover_strength 0.2 / 0.5 / 0.8 / 1.0 (the docs call 0.2 suggested for style transfer, 1.0 default).
Era effect (Delta P(target decade) vs neutral, codec-matched MERT / MuQ, 24 songs x 3 seeds, [songs>0], Wilcoxon):
- strength 0.2: 1960s +.049 [24/24] / +.107 [24/24]; 1980s +.128 / +.147; 2010s +.050 (p=.11) / -.031 (p=.36)
- 0.5: 1960s +.078 / +.106; 1980s +.215 / +.282; 2010s +.044 (p=.19) / -.030 (p=.20)
- 0.8: 1960s +.075 / +.117; 1980s +.218 / +.242; 2010s +.090 (p=.007) / +.031 (p=.28)
- 1.0: 1960s +.054 / +.079; 1980s +.170 / +.208; 2010s +.074 (p=.003) / -.001 (p=.83)
Content preservation (remix vs original, n=288 per strength): chroma song-id top-1 .267 / .323 / .347 / .337 for strength .2 / .5 / .8 / 1.0 (MusicGen-Melody seed 0: .906), chord agreement .230 / .228 / .227 / .229 (MusicGen-Melody .335).
Discogs-VINet identity (top-1 among 24 originals; chance .042): strength .2 neutral .10, 1960s .15, 1980s .08, 2010s .22; .5: .19/.18/.11/.15; .8: .21/.14/.15/.25; 1.0: .18/.18/.18/.19; mean cosine .48 to .54 (MusicGen-Melody .63 to .65). Era prompts change cosine by at most .02 with no significant effect.
Interpretation: ACE-Step in cover mode moves the era probes in the intended direction for 1960s and 1980s (1960s significant, which base MusicGen-Melody does not achieve), but the generated accompaniment keeps much less of the source song than chroma-conditioned MusicGen-Melody under both identity metrics, at every strength tested including 1.0. So for the criterion "same song, different era" this route trades identity for effect size; we did not test other ACE-Step tasks (repaint, lego) or the base/sft checkpoints. The codec-matched probes were trained on EnCodec-resynthesized real accompaniment, not on ACE-Step's VAE output, so a codec shift in the probe scores is possible (identity-codec control not run for this generator).
Tooling note: 54_vinet_identity.py now merges new directories into results/vinet_identity.json instead of overwriting it.

## 2026-10-28 LoRA rank check v3 (r=4, 3000 steps, bs 8, lr 1e-4; results/lora_v3_{eval,content}.json, gens_summary_lora.txt)
Same data, recipe and scoring as v1 (r=16) and v2 (r=16, 1000 steps); 24 songs x 3 seeds. Trainable parameters at r=4 are a quarter of r=16 (v1: 9.4M). Delta P(target decade) vs neutral, MERT / MuQ, [songs>0]:
- v1 r16/3000: 1960s +.396 / +.296; 1980s +.246 / +.220; 2010s +.171 / +.102
- v2 r16/1000: 1960s +.376 / +.259; 1980s +.217 / +.195; 2010s +.090 / +.049 (MuQ p=.065)
- v3 r4/3000: 1960s +.277 [21/24] / +.134 [19/24]; 1980s +.271 [23/24] / +.240 [21/24]; 2010s +.105 [17/24] p=.005 / +.029 [17/24] p=.17 (not significant)
Content (n=288): song-id top-1 .878 (v1), .882 (v2), .889 (v3); chord agreement .355 / .355 / .366 (base seed 0: .906 / .335). Discogs-VINet top-1 (neutral, 1960s, 1980s, 2010s): v3 .69, .78, .58, .71; cosine change vs neutral 1960s .000 (p=.71), 1980s -.016 (p=.16), 2010s -.015 (p=.039, 15 comparisons uncorrected).
Interpretation: lower rank reduces the 1960s effect (MERT +.277 vs +.396) and the MuQ 2010s effect becomes non-significant, while 1980s is about unchanged; identity metrics are unchanged within the resolution of this test. Single run per setting, so the rank and step-count differences are not separated from run-to-run variation (no repeated training seeds).

## 2026-10-28 genre/style confound: LEACE erasure of Discogs-EffNet style information (src/49a_tags_real.py, 58_style_erasure.py; results/style_erasure_{A,B}.json)
Style concept Z = top-k principal components (fitted on TRAIN) of the 400 Discogs-EffNet style-tag activations of the original mix: a PROXY for genre/style (the manifest has no genre column). LEACE eraser fitted on TRAIN, MERT-v2 layer/C as probe_<ds>.json, validation accuracy (A n=132, B n=102, wide CIs); random same-rank projections (30 draws) as control. Style-component predictability from the embedding (ridge R^2, validation) falls to about 0 after erasure in every row.
Dataset A decade, val acc (before -> after style erasure k=5 / 10 / 20 / 50; random same-rank mean .49 for mix at every k): mix .477 -> .485 / .409 / .326 / .280; vocals .394 -> .386 / .386 / .326 / .242 (random .39); accompaniment .485 -> .455 / .409 / .379 / .288 (random .49).
Dataset B market: mix .588 -> .520 / .500 / .529 / .510 (random .59); vocals .608 -> .598 / .588 / .500 / .559 (random .60-.61); accompaniment .422 -> .294 / .294 / .314 / .324 (random .41). Joint erasure of style (k=10) and sung-language groups: mix .343, vocals .382, accompaniment .294 (chance .167).
Caveats: before erasure the style PCs are decodable from the embedding only for the first ~10 components (R^2 .26 to .44 at k=5..10 for mix; near 0 or negative beyond), so k >= 20 mostly erases directions that are not predictable even before erasure and are fitted on 1026 (A) / 798 (B) training clips, where an over-fitted eraser can remove task information; the random-projection control does not reproduce that. Read k=5 and k=10 as the interpretable rows.
Interpretation: erasing the main style directions lowers decade accuracy more than erasing random directions (A mix k=10: .409 vs .494), i.e. part of the decade signal that the probe uses is linearly entangled with Discogs-style information, but accuracy stays well above chance (.167) after erasure, so decade information is not fully explained by this genre/style proxy. On B the accompaniment market signal drops most (.422 -> .294), consistent with regional genres (samba, flamenco, ...) carrying market information; the joint style plus language erasure leaves mix at .343. These are upper bounds on proxy reliance, not proof that the remainder is musical (correlated information is removed together with the concept).

## 2026-10-28 cue ablation with the fixed probe (src/57_cue_ablation.py; results/cue_ablation_{A,B}.json)
Interventions on the real HTDemucs validation stems (accompaniment, vocals), loudness-normalized to -23 LUFS, MERT-v2 embeddings, probe trained on UNMODIFIED train stems (same layer/C as probe_<ds>.json). Val n: A 132, B 102. Paired comparison with the unmodified row by exact McNemar (binomial) test, 15 interventions x 4 stem/dataset combinations, no multiplicity correction. This is a sensitivity test of the trained probe (a distribution shift for every intervention), NOT a measure of information left; the retrained-probe version (57b, below) addresses that.
Dataset A accompaniment (unmodified .485): low-pass 4k .379 (p=.024), 2k .212, 1k .182; high-pass 200 .288, 500 .189, 1k .189; band-pass 300-3000 .167 (chance .167); tanh compression .447 (p=.36); noise -30 dB .439 (p=.21); time-shuffle 2 s .379, 0.5 s .288; time reverse .189 with mean predicted decade moving from 2.56 to 4.14 (true mean 2.5); pitch +2 .394, -2 .341.
Dataset A vocals (unmodified .394): low-pass 4k .220, 1k .144; high-pass 200 .333 (p=.15); compress .356 (p=.42); time-shuffle 2 s .333 (p=.15), 0.5 s .273; time reverse .280 (p=.053); pitch +-2 .265/.280.
Dataset B vocals (unmodified .608): unchanged (acc .608 or .588, McNemar p >= .79) under low-pass 4k and 2k, high-pass 200, band-pass 300-3000, compression, pitch shift +-2; lower under low-pass 1k .412 (p=.003), high-pass 1k .480 (p=.015), time-shuffle 2 s .480 (p=.011), 0.5 s .422 (p=.003), time reverse .382 (p=.0003).
Dataset B accompaniment (unmodified .422): low-pass 4k .402 (p=.79), compression .422, noise .392; high-pass 200 .294 (p=.004), high-pass 1k .225 (p=.004), time-shuffle 0.5 s .275 (p=.036), band-pass .304 (p=.029).
Interpretation (fixed-probe sensitivity only): the decade probe on the accompaniment needs broadband content (removing either the low or the high part, or reversing time, takes it to about chance) and uses temporal structure at the sub-second scale (shuffling 0.5 s segments costs .197), but is insensitive to compression and low-level noise; the market probe on vocals is insensitive to band-limiting down to 2 kHz, to compression and to a +-2 semitone shift but is sensitive to temporal order (reverse and shuffle), the pattern expected from linguistic content rather than spectral coloration. These are statements about what the probe reacts to, not about what information is present.

## 2026-10-28 ACE-Step VAE identity control (src/59_ace_vae_identity.py; results/ace_vae_id_{eval,content}.json, vinet_identity.json)
The original accompaniment stem is passed through ACE-Step's VAE (encode, decode; no diffusion), RMS-matched and remixed with the vocal (24 songs, seed 0, copied for all four prompts). Result: mean expected decade (codec-matched probes, MERT / MuQ) 2.51 / 2.02 after the VAE round trip versus 2.63 / 2.45 for the EnCodec round trip of the original (identity row); ACE-Step generations with the neutral prompt: 4.20 / 3.71 (strength 0.5) and 4.11 / 3.43 (strength 1.0); MusicGen-Melody neutral 3.34 / 3.37. Identity of the VAE round trip: chroma song-id top-1 1.00, chord agreement .758, Discogs-VINet cosine .970 and top-1 1.00 (24 songs). Interpretation: the ACE-Step VAE alone neither moves the era probes toward later decades nor loses the song, so the late-decade bias of ACE-Step generations and their loss of identity come from the diffusion regeneration, not from the autoencoder.

## 2026-10-28 cue ablation with RETRAINED probes: information left after each intervention (src/57b_cue_retrain.py; results/cue_retrain_{A,B}.json; MERT embeddings cached server-side in emb_cue/)
Design: every labeled stem (HTDemucs accompaniment or vocals) is modified, loudness-normalized (-23 LUFS) and embedded with MERT-v2; a NEW probe (same layer and C as the unmodified probe, StandardScaler + logistic regression) is fitted on the modified TRAIN clips and scored on the modified validation clips (A n_val 132, B n_val 102; bootstrap 95% CI; exact McNemar against the unmodified row; 8 interventions x 2 stems x 2 datasets, no multiplicity correction). Interventions: low-pass 2 kHz, high-pass 500 Hz, band-pass 300-3000 Hz, tanh compression (drive 4), time-shuffle of 2 s and 0.5 s segments, time reversal, white noise at -30 dB re stem RMS. Pitch shift was left out (cost). Chance .167.
Dataset A accompaniment (unmodified .485 [.402, .568]): low-pass 2k .417 (p=.11); high-pass 500 .364 (p=.007); band-pass .386 (p=.024); compression .492; shuffle 2 s .432 (p=.09), 0.5 s .424 (p=.17); reverse .402 (p=.10); noise .462. Vocals (unmodified .394): every intervention .333 to .409 (all p >= .115): low-pass .409, high-pass .371, band-pass .371, compression .356, shuffle 2 s .394, 0.5 s .333, reverse .386, noise .364.
Dataset B vocals (unmodified .608 [.510, .696]): low-pass 2k .618; high-pass 500 .598; band-pass .569; compression .598; shuffle 2 s .588, 0.5 s .569; noise .647 (all p >= .52); time reversal .480 (p=.029). Accompaniment (unmodified .422 [.324, .520]): low-pass .333 (p=.11); high-pass 500 .265 (p=.002); band-pass .324 (p=.041); compression .402; shuffle 2 s .343 (p=.12), 0.5 s .402; reverse .382; noise .392.
Interpretation: the fixed-probe sensitivity in the earlier entry (accuracy near chance under band-limiting and reversal) was mostly distribution shift; after retraining, decade information in the accompaniment survives every intervention at well above chance (.36 to .49) and is not tied to temporal order or to dynamics (compression and noise cost nothing measurable), while removing the part below 500 Hz is the one significant loss (-.121, p=.007) and band-limiting at 2 kHz costs a non-significant -.07; so in this setting decade information is spread over the spectrum with some dependence on low frequencies, consistent with production/bass-and-drums cues, but we cannot localize it to one band. Market information in the vocals is unchanged by band-limiting (down to 2 kHz low-pass and above 500 Hz high-pass), compression, noise and segment shuffling and is reduced only by time reversal, i.e. by destroying the order of phonetic content within segments; in the accompaniment the market information depends on content below 500 Hz (-.157, p=.002). These intervals are wide (n=102 to 132); effects smaller than about .1 are not resolved.

## 2026-10-28 probe regularization sensitivity and learning curve (A18, A19; src/63_probe_sensitivity.py; results/probe_sensitivity_{A,B}.json)
MERT-v2 embeddings at the per-condition CV layer (probe_<ds>.json), StandardScaler + logistic regression, validation accuracy (A n=132, B n=102; differences below about .05 are within the bootstrap CIs of one split).
C grid (1e-4, 1e-3, 3e-3, 1e-2, 3e-2, 1e-1, 1, 10), val accuracy: A mix .492/.500/.523/.477/.470/.470/.455/.447; A vocals .333/.394/.409/.394/.394/.386/.379/.379; A accompaniment .447/.447/.470/.485/.455/.424/.409/.379; B mix .559/.627/.608/.588/.569/.559/.578/.549; B vocals .549/.588/.618/.608/.598/.578/.559/.598; B accompaniment .392/.441/.412/.422/.412/.392/.392/.382. The reported results use C selected by CV (0.01 in these rows); between C=1e-3 and 1e-1 accuracy moves by at most .06 (A mix) and the stem ordering (A: mix >= accompaniment > vocals; B: mix >= vocals > accompaniment) holds at every C from 1e-3 to 1e-1.
Learning curve (train fraction 10/25/50/75/100% of TRAIN; mean over 10 stratified subsets, sd .02 to .05): A mix .398/.431/.453/.461/.477 (n_train 102 to 1026); A vocals .249/.299/.330/.379/.394; A accompaniment .357/.410/.447/.445/.485; B mix .501/.557/.594/.590/.588 (n_train 79 to 798); B vocals .440/.493/.564/.575/.608; B accompaniment .323/.341/.369/.398/.422.
Interpretation: accuracy is not saturated at the full training sets for A (all three conditions) and for B vocals and accompaniment, so more labeled data would probably raise absolute accuracy (not tested beyond the available data); B mix is flat from 50% of the training data on. The hyperparameter choice does not drive the conclusions.

## 2026-10-28 audio-language-model judge, MQAScore-style (E08; src/61_lalm_era.py, 64_lalm_analysis.py; results/lalm_era.json, lalm_summary.txt)
Judge: Qwen2-Audio-7B-Instruct (HF revision in repro/environment.txt), bf16, zero-shot, no PA1 training. Readouts: (mc) one multiple-choice question "Which decade ... A) 1960s ... F) 2010s", softmax over the six letter logits of the first answer token; (yn) six yes/no questions, one per decade (score = log P(yes)). Scores are prior-corrected by subtracting the per-decade mean over the 132 real validation accompaniment clips (no labels used); the yn readout is also divided by the per-decade standard deviation pooled over all generations before the softmax. Generated accompaniment only (*_acc.wav), 24 songs x 4 prompts x 3 seeds per directory.
Positive control on real validation clips (n=132; chance accuracy .167): accompaniment mc accuracy .174 (prior-corrected .205), Spearman(expected decade, true decade) .448 (.443); yes/no accuracy .295, Spearman .622; mix mc accuracy .212 (.273), Spearman .568 (.509); yes/no accuracy .326, Spearman .672. So the judge ranks decades with moderate ordinal agreement (rho .45 to .67) but its exact-decade accuracy is near chance; it is a weak evaluator.
Change in judge P(target decade), prompt vs neutral, mean over 24 songs [songs>0], Wilcoxon p (mc readout; MERT/MuQ probe values from earlier entries in brackets for comparison):
- MusicGen-Melody base: 1960s +.031 (p=.065) [probe -.034 / +.001]; 1980s +.561 [24/24] [+.615 / +.442]; 2010s +.080 (p=.002) [+.354 / +.115]
- text-only (no melody): 1960s -.039 [0/24]; 1980s +.554; 2010s +.016
- LoRA v1: 1960s +.059 (p=.006) [+.396 / +.296]; 1980s -.010 (p=.77) [+.246 / +.220]; 2010s +.009 (p=.62) [+.171 / +.102]; v2: +.088 (p<.001), -.013, +.017; v3: +.076 (p<.001), +.035 (p=.20), +.006
- MuseControlLite: 1960s +.008; 1980s +.023 (p=.11); 2010s -.042 [3/24] (p<.001)
- ACE-Step 0.5: 1960s +.058 [21/24]; 1980s +.224 [21/24]; 2010s -.061 [6/24] (p<.001); strength 1.0: +.067, +.260, -.070
The yes/no readout is unstable on generations (for example base 1980s -.023 [5/24], p=.0002, opposite to the multiple-choice readout, and several effects below .01); it is reported in `lalm_summary.txt` but not interpreted.
Interpretation: an independent zero-shot judge agrees with the probes on the strongest effect (1980s prompt on base MusicGen-Melody and on ACE-Step) and on the sign of the 1960s effect for LoRA, but it does not confirm the LoRA 1980s and 2010s effects (probe +.17 to +.27 vs judge about 0) and it disagrees on the ACE-Step 2010s prompt (probe about 0 to +.09, judge -.06 to -.07). Because the judge's own accuracy is near chance, disagreement could come from the judge as well as from probe-specific cues; neither evaluator has been validated by listeners.

## 2026-10-28 language-proxy robustness: second language-ID model (A24; src/66_lid_second.py, 51_leace.py with LIDNAME=lid3; results/leace_{A,B}_lid3.json)
The language groups behind the LEACE/INLP/strata results come from Whisper LID on the vocal stem (first run: openai/whisper-small, `emb/*_lid_voc.npy`). Second proxy: openai/whisper-large-v3 (fp16), same procedure (language-token softmax at the first decoder step on the loudness-normalized vocal stem), `emb/*_lid3_voc.npy` (not published; features only).
Agreement of the two models: argmax language identical for 86.1% of the 1002 dataset-B clips and 89.1% of the 1290 dataset-A clips; after grouping (B: en/pt/es/it/de/other, A: en/es/other) 87.6% (B) and 90.8% (A). Largest differences: label "la" (B: 51 clips with whisper-small vs 19 with large-v3), "it" 57 vs 73, "es" 85 vs 99. There is no ground-truth language for these clips, so agreement between two Whisper models is only a consistency check, not an accuracy estimate (A23 not done: a known-language subset would need labels we do not have).
LEACE market erasure repeated with the large-v3 groups (B, train groups en 422, pt 128, es 77, it 62, de 22, other 87): mix .588 -> .382 (whisper-small groups: .353); vocals .608 -> .392 (.402); accompaniment .422 -> .314 (.314); random same-rank erasure unchanged (.591 / .606 / .413); language-group probe accuracy after erasure falls to the majority share (.569). Dataset A: mix .477 -> .462, vocals .394 -> .386, accompaniment .485 -> .508 (random .482 / .397 / .483).
Interpretation: the conclusion that most linearly accessible market information lies in a subspace aligned with sung language does not depend on which of the two Whisper models defines the language groups.

## 2026-10-28 pooling ablation: does the time pooling of the MERT hidden states matter? (A17; src/62_pooling_embed.py, 62b_pooling_probe.py; results/pooling_{A,B}.json)
Re-embedding of all clips (HTDemucs stems, -23 LUFS, MERT-v2-30s, 24 hidden-state layers) with per-layer standard deviation, maximum and three 10-second segment means (same preprocessing as the main embeddings); probe StandardScaler + logistic regression C=0.01, layer chosen per variant by 3-fold CV on TRAIN, validation accuracy (A n=132, B n=102; 95% CIs about +-.09). Variants: mean (the main setting), std, max, mean+std, mean+max, three segment means concatenated (3072-d).
Val accuracy mean / std / max / mean+std / mean+max / seg3: A mix .523 / .447 / .447 / .462 / .462 / .462; A vocals .402 / .250 / .318 / .333 / .402 / .379; A accompaniment .485 / .379 / .379 / .394 / .439 / .417; B mix .618 / .559 / .569 / .618 / .637 / .549; B vocals .569 / .490 / .529 / .549 / .559 / .608; B accompaniment .431 / .265 / .314 / .314 / .402 / .382.
Interpretation: mean pooling is best or within the interval of the best variant in all six rows (largest gains over mean: B vocals +.039 with three-segment concatenation, B mix +.020 with mean+max, both far inside the CI); std-only, max-only and concatenations of more features do not help and often lower accuracy (A vocals std .250), so the choice of mean pooling is not limiting the stem conclusions. Note: the mean-pooled rows here re-select the layer on this run's CV split and differ slightly from probe_<ds>.json (for example A mix L10 .523 vs L12 .477 in the main table) because ties between neighbouring layers are broken differently; this spread (.05) is the layer-selection noise to keep in mind.

## 2026-10-28 LoRA training-seed replicates (G11b; run_lora_seed.sh; results/lora_v1s{1,2}_{eval,content}.json, gens_summary_lora_seeds.txt)
Same recipe as v1 (r=16, 3000 steps, bs 8, lr 1e-4, training seed 0/1/2 changes LoRA initialization and batch order; generation seeds 0, 1, 2 unchanged; 24 songs, codec-matched probes). Delta P(target decade) vs neutral, mean over songs, MERT 1960s / 1980s / 2010s | MuQ 1960s / 1980s / 2010s:
- training seed 0: +.396 / +.246 / +.171 | +.296 / +.220 / +.102
- training seed 1: +.414 / +.270 / +.121 | +.264 / +.210 / +.081
- training seed 2: +.366 / +.243 / +.068 | +.205 / +.191 / +.048
- mean of the three: +.392 / +.253 / +.120 | +.255 / +.207 / +.077; standard deviation across training seeds (n=3) .024 / .015 / .052 | .046 / .015 / .028; range .048 / .028 / .104 | .091 / .029 / .055
Content (song-id top-1 / chord agreement): seed 0 .878 / .355; seed 1 .882 / .364; seed 2 .858 / .362.
Comparison with the other runs, in units of the three-seed standard deviation (rough, sd from n=3): v2 (1000 steps): MERT 1960s -0.6, 1980s -2.4, 2010s -0.6; MuQ +0.1, -0.8, -1.0; so the step-count differences are mostly within seed variation (the 2010s effect alone ranges .068 to .171 across training seeds). v3 (r=4): MERT 1960s -4.7, 1980s +1.2, 2010s -0.3; MuQ -2.6, +2.2, -1.7; so the lower 1960s effect of the rank-4 adapter lies outside the seed spread in both probes, while its 1980s and 2010s values are within about 2 sd. Interpretation: across training seeds the pattern 1960s > 1980s > 2010s and every effect being positive are stable; the 2010s effect is the least reproducible (range of .104 in MERT); the rank-4 adapter has a smaller 1960s effect than any rank-16 run.

## 2026-10-28 generation ablations G08 / G02b / G05 / G07 (summary: results/gens_summary_g08_g02b_g05_g07.txt, tool src/56_summarize_gens.py)
Statistic: per-song Δ P(target decade | era prompt) − P(target decade | neutral prompt), codec-matched MERT / MuQ probes, 24 songs, Wilcoxon; [k/24] = songs with Δ>0. Content = song-id top-1 and chord agreement. Seeds: cfg3 = seed 0 only (n=96 clips); cfg1/5/7, delta, pv: seeds 0–2 (n=288); sp: seeds 0–1 (n=192). Probe values are a proxy for era perception, not human judgement.

| run | MERT 1960s / 1980s / 2010s | MuQ 1960s / 1980s / 2010s | top1 / chord |
|---|---|---|---|
| CFG 1 | +.116 / +.211 / +.035 | +.027 / +.089 / +.118 | .618 / .291 |
| CFG 3 (default, seed 0) | −.034 / +.615 / +.354 | +.001 / +.442 / +.115 | .906 / .335 |
| CFG 5 | +.049 / +.593 / +.474 | +.053 / +.400 / +.088 | .910 / .341 |
| CFG 7 | +.111 / +.562 / +.487 | +.076 / +.378 / +.153 | .896 / .334 |
| delta g=3 | +.153 / +.566 / +.131 | +.206 / +.413 / +.130 | .688 / .313 |
| delta g=5 | +.281 / +.663 / +.266 | +.253 / +.558 / +.228 | .653 / .310 |
| prompt: decade word | +.008 / +.315 / +.003 | −.017 / +.257 / −.021 | .889 / .344 |
| prompt: short | −.033 / +.087 / −.013 | −.034 / +.125 / −.044 | .899 / .344 |
| prompt: keyword tags | −.054 / +.621 / +.477 | −.036 / +.438 / +.194 | .885 / .339 |
| top_k 250 | +.064 / +.463 / +.386 | +.042 / +.317 / +.091 | .911 / .347 |
| T 0.8 | −.034 / +.609 / +.320 | −.004 / +.387 / +.028 | .880 / .329 |
| T 1.2 | −.014 / +.524 / +.351 | −.014 / +.322 / +.100 | .911 / .347 |
| top_p 0.9 (k0p09, label as run) | +.006 / +.527 / +.316 | +.033 / +.395 / +.048 | .917 / .341 |

Observations (measured, interpretation labelled):
- Delta guidance gives the largest 1960s and 2010s probe shifts of all MusicGen runs (g=5: 1960s +.281 MERT, +.253 MuQ) but identity drops (top-1 .65–.69 vs .89–.91; chord agreement .31 vs .33–.34). Interpretation: the contrast term trades melody identity for era strength; Pareto position vs CFG needs an identity-matched comparison (VINet not yet run on these dirs).
- Higher CFG (5, 7) raises the 1960s effect over CFG 3 in both probes while top-1 is unchanged (.91/.90); CFG 1 loses identity (.618). 
- Prompt elaboration: the detailed sentence and the keyword tags carry the effect; a bare decade word or short prompt gives 1980s only (+.09 to +.32), no 2010s effect.
- Sampling parameters: top_k 250 / T 1.2 / top_p 0.9 change the probe effect by less than the differences between prompt forms; no setting produces a significant 1960s effect in both probes except top_k 250 (p=.001 / .025). Seeds 0–1 only; no seed-spread estimate for these runs, so small differences are not interpreted.
- Caveat: cfg3 is seed 0 only (n=96) while the other CFG rows have 3 seeds; a CFG 3 re-run with seeds 1,2 is not in this table.

## 2026-10-28 CFG 3 with 3 seeds, LoRA learning-rate ablation (G11c, lr part), best-of-N (G09), VINet identity for the new runs
Files: results/gens_summary_cfg3s3_lora_lr.txt, results/bon_summary.txt, results/vinet_identity.json; scripts run_cfg3_s3.sh (re-scores existing gen/ with seeds 0-2), run_lora_lr.sh (r16, 3000 steps, bs 8, lr as named; one training seed each), src/67_bestofn_analysis.py. Same statistic as the entry above (Δ P(target decade) vs neutral, 24 songs, seeds 0-2).
- CFG 3, 3 seeds (n=288): MERT 1960s −.001 (p=.97), 1980s +.593, 2010s +.328; MuQ 1960s +.006 (p=.75), 1980s +.414, 2010s +.094 (p=.007); top-1 .892, chord .336. This replaces the seed-0-only CFG 3 row of the previous table (seed 0 had 1960s −.034 / +.001): the null 1960s effect at CFG 3 holds with 3 seeds.
- LoRA lr (MERT 1960s/1980s/2010s; MuQ same; top-1): 3e-5: +.320/+.192/+.068; +.195/+.165/+.071; .899 | 1e-4 (v1): +.396/+.246/+.171; +.296/+.220/+.102; .878 | 3e-4: +.420/+.207/+.215; +.318/+.192/+.126; .792. Observation: effect grows with lr for 1960s and 2010s while song-id top-1 drops at 3e-4 (.792 vs .878-.899). One training seed per lr; the seed sd for lr 1e-4 was .015-.052 (earlier entry), so the 3e-5 vs 1e-4 gap on 1960s MERT (.076) is above that spread, the 1e-4 vs 3e-4 gap (.024) is not.
- VINet identity (cos / top-1 over 24 songs; neutral reference .65 / .72): delta g=3 era rows .56-.59 / .33-.40, g=5 .55-.58 / .28-.42 (identity clearly lower than CFG at the same probe shift); CFG 1 .59-.62 / .33-.54; CFG 5 and 7 .64-.66 / .67-.79 (no identity loss vs neutral); prompt variants and sampling settings .62-.66 / .50-.75 (tags 1980s .63 / .50). Interpretation: delta guidance buys its larger era shift with lower identity (chroma-conditioned melody leaks less); CFG 5-7 raise the 1960s effect without a VINet identity loss.
- Best-of-N (8 candidates per song and era, seeds 10-17; selectors compared with a random candidate, paired over 24 songs): judge selector raises independent-probe P by MERT +.046 / MuQ +.055 for 1960s (p=.02 / .015), not significant for 1980s or 2010s; identity selector raises VINet similarity by +.013-.016 (p<.001) without changing era probes; judge+identity keeps most of the judge gain on the judge score but the MERT/MuQ gains are not significant. The MERT-oracle selector (selecting by the evaluator itself, so an upper bound, not an independent result) gives +.18-.24 on MERT. P_judge gains for the judge selector are circular (selected by the same quantity) and are not evidence. The judge was weak on real clips (earlier entry), so the 1960s gain is small and not replicated for other eras.
- Run notes: bon, cfg3_s3, lr3e5, lr3e4, vin2 finished; vin3 (VINet on the two lr dirs) and drive9 (Drive sync) still running at the time of writing.

## 2026-10-28 R03 determinism check (run_det.sh)
MusicGen-Melody, CFG 3, `torch.manual_seed(seed)`, fp16, same inputs: 6 validation songs x {neutral, 1980s} x seed 0 generated twice on GPU 2 and once on GPU 3 (all NVIDIA H200 NVL). Result: 12/12 accompaniment waveforms bit-identical across the two runs and across the two GPUs, and 12/12 bit-identical to the files in gen/ produced earlier in the project. Scope: this covers the MusicGen generation path in this environment (torch 2.5.1, transformers 5.18); it was not repeated for MuseControlLite or ACE-Step.

## 2026-10-28 design record: G06 (LLM-written era prompts), G08b (MuseControlLite guidance), G11c no-chroma LoRA (for report methods and reviewer questions)
G06. Question: do prompts written by an LLM from the decade name alone match or beat the hand-written prompts, and does selecting among LLM candidates help? Components and reasons:
- Text LLMs: Qwen3-8B and Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct, downloaded from Hugging Face (open weights, so the prompt writing is re-runnable; 7-8B parameters in bf16 fit on one GPU; two model generations from the same vendor serve as a replicate of the LLM choice). No benchmark ranking is claimed for either; same-vendor is a stated limitation. Gemma-4-31B-it (w4a16 quantized checkpoint, needs extra libraries, ~20 GB of the 66 GB free disk) was not used.
- LLM input: the decade name and one fixed instruction only (no clips, labels or results), so the prompts cannot contain dataset information; instruction asks for an instrumental backing track, instruments, rhythm and recording/mixing style typical of the decade, at most 30 words, no artists/titles/lyrics/vocals (vocals excluded because only the accompaniment is regenerated). Sampling temperature 0.8, top_p 0.95, 8 candidates per decade (seeds 0-7); Qwen3 thinking mode disabled.
- Selection: candidates generated on 24 TRAIN songs (4 per decade, manifest order), seed 0, same protocol as the main runs; the score is the mean of the MERT and MuQ probe delta P(target decade) vs neutral; the best candidate per decade is chosen (`src/71_select_prompts.py`) and tested on the 24 validation songs, seeds 0-2, never used during selection. The mean over all 8 candidates is reported as the unselected LLM-prompt level. Caveat: the era probes were trained on the real clips of the training songs, so the selection score is not fully independent of the probe; the validation test reports MERT and MuQ probes plus VINet identity and content metrics.
- Outputs: results/llm_prompts_<tag>.json (all candidates, instruction, sampling), results/g06_<tag>_selection.txt, g06_<tag>_selected.json, g06_<tag>_eval/content.json.
G08b. MuseControlLite has three guidance scales (text 7.0, condition 1.5, audio 1.0 in the repository config; defaults used so far). The sweep varies text guidance (3, 12) and condition guidance (0.75, 3.0) one at a time, 24 songs x 4 prompts x seeds 0-1, scoring identical to MusicGen runs. Reason: CFG 3 is the MusicGen default and the sweep there (1, 3, 5, 7) changed the effect, so the MuseControlLite default needs the same check before comparing generators. APG / negative-prompt variants are not run yet.
G11c no-chroma. LoRA trained with the text condition only and used with --no_melody; tests whether the LoRA's era effect depends on the melody condition (compare with v1 at identical training config). Identity is expected to be low without chroma (text-only baseline VINet top-1 .21).

## 2026-10-28 G06 additions: Ollama-packaged models (/home/jtan/ollama on the server, 12 GB, pre-existing)
Present models: qwen2.5:14b and llama3.2:1b (Ollama builds; quantization not inspected here). Used as two more prompt writers with the identical instruction and sampling settings (src/69b_llm_prompts_ollama.py, local Ollama server on port 11500, stopped afterwards): Qwen2.5-14B tests whether a larger writer from the Qwen family changes the result; Llama-3.2-1B is the only different-vendor writer available without a new download (small, so a weak writer; reported as a replicate, not as a model comparison). Tags: qwen25_14b, llama32_1b. Same selection/test pipeline as the HF models (run_g06.sh).

## 2026-10-28 MuseControlLite guidance sweep (G08b), no-chroma LoRA (G11c), LLM prompts G06 for Qwen2.5-14B and Llama-3.2-1B (results/gens_summary_g06_g08b_nomel_part1.txt)
Statistic as before (Δ P(target decade) vs neutral, 24 validation songs, MERT | MuQ; 1960s / 1980s / 2010s). MuseControlLite seeds 0-1 (repo defaults text 7.0 / condition 1.5 / audio 1.0, default row recomputed on seeds 0-1 of mcl_gen).
| run | MERT | MuQ | top-1 / chord |
|---|---|---|---|
| MCL default (7.0/1.5) | +.003 / +.029 / +.202 | +.061 / +.072 / +.073 | 1.000 / .374 |
| MCL text 3 | +.004 / +.018 / +.061 | +.021 / +.041 / +.020 | 1.000 / .374 |
| MCL text 12 | −.020 / +.030 / +.200 | +.102 / +.036 / +.064 | .995 / .369 |
| MCL condition 3.0 | −.019 / +.043 / +.031 | +.020 / +.053 / +.034 | 1.000 / .349 |
| MCL condition 0.75 | −.037 / +.090 / +.401 | +.097 / +.053 / +.134 | .875 / .352 |
| MusicGen CFG 3, hand-written prompts (3 seeds) | −.001 / +.593 / +.328 | +.006 / +.414 / +.094 | .892 / .336 |
| MusicGen + LLM prompts, Qwen2.5-14B (selected on train songs) | +.037 / +.332 / +.169 | +.086 / +.227 / +.095 | .882 / .338 |
| MusicGen + LLM prompts, Llama-3.2-1B (selected on train songs) | +.053 / +.382 / +.161 | +.071 / +.305 / −.048 | .892 / .342 |
| LoRA without chroma (text-only train and use) | +.366 / +.555 / +.295 | +.272 / +.337 / +.398 | .295 / .217 |
Observations: (1) MuseControlLite's era effect is small at every guidance setting and mostly in the 2010s; lowering condition guidance to 0.75 raises the 2010s effect (MERT +.401) while top-1 falls from 1.000 to .875 (a lower-identity, larger-effect setting); text guidance 3 weakens the effect and 12 does not strengthen it beyond the default. Two seeds, no seed-spread estimate. (2) LLM-written prompts selected on training songs give a smaller 1980s and 2010s effect than the hand-written prompts but a 1960s effect that the hand-written prompts lack (MERT +.037 / +.053, MuQ +.086 / +.071, p<.03); the selection scores (train songs, seed 0): Qwen2.5-14B 1960s +.092, 1980s +.245, 2010s +.103 vs mean over all candidates +.011 / +.186 / +.021; Llama-3.2-1B +.117 / +.347 / +.093 vs +.015 / +.254 / −.034 (selected prompts in results/g06_<tag>_selection.txt). The gap between selected and mean candidates is as large as the effect itself, so unselected LLM prompts are close to no effect for 1960s and 2010s. Llama-3.2-1B 2010s MuQ is negative (−.048, p=.15 n.s.) while MERT is +.161: the two probes disagree there. (3) The text-only LoRA keeps a strong era effect in both probes (all six shifts positive in 24/24 songs) while melody identity is gone (song-ID top-1 .295, chord agreement .217; for comparison the earlier text-only baseline without LoRA was measured with VINet, top-1 .21, a different metric), so the LoRA's era effect does not depend on the chroma condition; identity does. Qwen3-8B and Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct G06 runs are still in the validation stage and are logged separately.

## 2026-10-28 G06 complete: four LLM prompt writers (results/gens_summary_g06_all.txt, g06_<tag>_selection.txt, llm_prompts_<tag>.json, vinet_identity.json)
Protocol as in the G06 design record: decade-name-only instruction, 8 candidates per decade, best candidate per decade chosen on 24 train songs (seed 0, mean of MERT and MuQ probe delta), tested on the 24 validation songs, seeds 0-2. Run note (internal): the Qwen3-8B and Qwen2.5-7B pipelines stopped after candidate scoring because run_g06.sh was edited while running; they were resumed from the selection step (run_g06_tail.sh) with no change to the protocol.
Validation Δ P(target decade) vs neutral, MERT | MuQ, 1960s / 1980s / 2010s; selection-score columns are train-song means (selected vs mean over all 8 candidates), VINet cos / top-1 over the three era rows (neutral reference .65 / .72):
| writer | MERT | MuQ | top-1 / chord | VINet era rows |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| hand-written (CFG 3, 3 seeds) | −.001 / +.593 / +.328 | +.006 / +.414 / +.094 | .892 / .336 | .64-.65 / .62-.81 (gen/, 3 era rows) |
| Qwen3-8B | +.179 / +.579 / +.268 | +.163 / +.323 / −.002 | .882 / .343 | .64-.65 / .64-.68 |
| Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct | +.196 / +.506 / +.360 | +.096 / +.322 / +.185 | .889 / .342 | .63-.65 / .64-.69 |
| Qwen2.5-14B (Ollama) | +.037 / +.332 / +.169 | +.086 / +.227 / +.095 | .882 / .338 | .65-.66 / .65-.71 |
| Llama-3.2-1B (Ollama) | +.053 / +.382 / +.161 | +.071 / +.305 / −.048 | .892 / .342 | .64 / .67-.68 |
Selection scores (train songs; chosen / mean of 8): Qwen3-8B 1960s +.183 / +.013, 1980s +.447 / +.240, 2010s +.146 / +.031; Qwen2.5-7B 1960s +.240 / +.105, 1980s +.406 / +.276, 2010s +.249 / +.059; Qwen2.5-14B +.092 / +.011, +.245 / +.186, +.103 / +.021; Llama-3.2-1B +.117 / +.015, +.347 / +.254, +.093 / −.034.
Observations (measured; interpretation labelled): (1) Selected LLM prompts from the two 7-8B models give a significant 1960s shift in both probes (MERT +.179 / +.196, MuQ +.163 / +.096; all p<.001) that the hand-written 1960s prompt does not (−.001 / +.006); the 14B and 1B writers give smaller 1960s effects (MERT +.037 / +.053). The hand-written prompts are stronger on 1980s (MERT +.593 vs +.332 to +.579) and similar or stronger on 2010s MERT. (2) The 2010s MuQ effect is absent for Qwen3-8B (−.002, p=.99) and Llama (−.048) while MERT is positive (+.268, +.161): the two probes disagree there, so the 2010s result is probe-dependent. (3) Selection matters: the chosen candidate exceeds the mean candidate by .08-.17 on the train score, and the mean candidate is near zero for 1960s and 2010s, so an unselected LLM prompt gives little era effect for those decades. (4) Song-ID top-1 (.88-.89) and chord agreement (.34) are the same as with hand-written prompts; VINet top-1 for the era rows is .64-.71 vs .72 neutral. (5) Writer size does not order the results (14B < 7B for 1960s), consistent with prompt-to-prompt variability being larger than writer differences; one selection run per writer, no repeat of the LLM sampling, so differences between writers are not separately tested. (6) Selection used the same probe family as the evaluation (probes trained on real clips of the train songs); MERT and MuQ are both reported, and no human check was done.

## 2026-10-28 entropy-matched sampling, step 1: teacher-forced entropy of real vs generated accompaniment (src/72_entropy_match.py, results/entropy_match_run1.json; HW2 idea)
Measurement (identical for every source): MusicGen-Melody (bf16), 24 validation songs, EnCodec codes (32 kHz, 4 codebooks) of frames 250-750 (5-15 s), neutral prompt, chroma of the original mix over the same span, no CFG (conditional branch), mean over positions and codebooks of the predictive entropy H and of the cross-entropy of the observed code (nats). Generated sources are the existing neutral accompaniments (seed 0, CFG 3 at generation, re-encoded with EnCodec).
| source | entropy | cross-entropy |
|---|---|---|
| real accompaniment stem | 4.304 | 4.331 |
| T=1.0, top_k 50 (library default) | 3.701 | 3.785 |
| T=0.8, top_k 50 | 2.860 | 2.884 |
| T=1.2, top_k 50 | 4.030 | 4.139 |
| T=1.0, top_k 250 | 4.243 | 4.347 |
| T=1.0, top_p 0.9 (top_k 0) | 3.393 | 3.421 |
Observation: the default sampler produces accompaniment that is lower-entropy (0.60 nats) than real accompaniment under the same model; entropy rises with temperature and with top_k, and top_k 250 at T=1 (Meta's reference value) lands within 0.06 nats of the real value. Song-to-song sd is .41-.56 so the single-seed mean difference to the real value for k250 is well inside the spread. Interpretation (labelled): the library default is more conservative than real music; matching entropy points at top_k 250 or temperature about 1.2-1.4 with top_k 250. Step 2 generates T=1.2 and T=1.4 with top_k 250 (configs t12k250, t14k250 in 68_sampling_params.py, seeds 0-1) and re-measures; era probes and content metrics follow the usual scoring. Limits: the model measuring entropy is the model that generated (self-referential), real stems are Demucs separations, one seed.

- MERT 1960s +.236 / 1980s +.584 / 2010s +.122; MuQ +.280 / +.417 / +.125; song-ID top-1 .681, chord .317; VINet top-1 .389 / .278 / .403 (neutral .722).

Differences between the two guidance variants are of the order of the seed variation seen elsewhere (one run each; no significance test between variants run) - not claimed as better. Neither reaches the hand-written CFG-3 2010s level in MERT (+.328) in this setting; that comparison uses different guidance structure, so it is descriptive only.

KAD is computed on the same encoder family as the probe (MERT), so it is not an independent confirmation of the probe result.

## 2026-10-30 LoRA v1 + contrast guidance (results/lorav1_{neg,delta}_g3_*, gens_summary_lorav1_guid.txt, vinet_identity.json; src/run_lora_guid.sh, 60_delta_guidance.py and 60b_negprompt_guidance.py with env LORA / NEUTRAL_SRC)
Config: LoRA v1 adapter merged (PEFT, as in 07_generate.py) into MusicGen, then the same contrast guidance as the base-model runs at scale g=3 (negative-prompt: opposite-era contrast 1960s/1980s -> 2010s, 2010s -> 1960s; delta: era-vs-neutral delta guidance); neutral rows taken from the LoRA v1 alone set (gen_lora). 24 validation songs, same codec-matched MERT/MuQ probes, Wilcoxon. Why: tests whether a trained adapter and inference-time guidance add up (reviewer question: is guidance redundant with fine-tuning).
Per-song delta P(target decade), MERT 1960s/1980s/2010s (MuQ in the summary file):
| set | MERT | song-ID top-1 | VINet top-1 (1960s/1980s/2010s; neutral .722) |
|---|---|---|---|
| LoRA v1 alone | +.396 / +.246 / +.171 | .878 | .722 / .556 / .639 |
| LoRA v1 + negative-prompt g3 | +.612 / +.324 / +.346 | .674 | .319 / .417 / .431 |
| LoRA v1 + delta g3 | +.528 / +.215 / +.236 | .681 | .417 / .347 / .389 |
All shifts p<.001, 21-24 of 24 songs positive. For comparison, base model + guidance g3 (no LoRA): negative-prompt +.236/+.584/+.122, delta +.153/+.566/+.131. Observations: adding guidance on top of LoRA raises 1960s and 2010s shifts over LoRA alone, but the 1980s shift is not above base-model guidance (+.584 vs +.324 / +.215), and identity falls to the same level as guidance without LoRA (top-1 .67-.68; VINet roughly halved). So in this grid the two do not combine into a larger shift at preserved identity; the identity cost comes from the guidance. (Interpretation, single seed of adapter and generation.)

## 2026-10-30 LoRA v1 + contrast guidance, scale sweep g=1,2,3 (results/lorav1_{neg,delta}_g{1,2,3}_*, gens_summary_lorav1_{neg,delta}_sweep.txt, vinet_identity.json; src/run_lora_guid.sh, same config as the g=3 entry above)
MERT shift 1960s / 1980s / 2010s (24 songs, 3 seeds; * = p>.05), song-ID top-1, VINet top-1 (neutral .722):
| set | MERT | top-1 | VINet |
|---|---|---|---|
| LoRA v1 alone (standard CFG 3) | +.396 / +.246 / +.171 | .878 | .722 / .556 / .639 |
| neg g1 | +.221 / +.039* / +.085 | .670 | .278 / .431 / .361 |
| neg g2 | +.463 / +.119 / +.280 | .670 | .361 / .375 / .389 |
| neg g3 | +.612 / +.324 / +.346 | .674 | .319 / .417 / .431 |
| delta g1 | +.221 / +.039* / +.064* | .677 | .278 / .431 / .333 |
| delta g2 | +.396 / +.112 / +.190 | .670 | .361 / .333 / .375 |
| delta g3 | +.528 / +.215 / +.236 | .681 | .417 / .347 / .389 |
Observations: (1) the shift grows with g for both contrasts (MuQ agrees in sign; 2010s MuQ at g1 n.s.). (2) Song-ID top-1 is .67-.68 at every g including g=1, i.e. identity does NOT trade off against g here; it is lower than LoRA alone (.878) already at g=1. (3) At g=1 the patched guidance returns the unmodified path (guidance_scale<=1 → no CFG branch at all), so g=1 is LoRA + era prompt WITHOUT any classifier-free guidance; neg and delta g1 coincide (identical 1960s/1980s numbers; the 2010s values differ slightly, cause not investigated, possibly GPU nondeterminism). Interpretation (not tested): the identity drop is due to losing the standard CFG (era+chroma vs. null) term, which amplifies melody adherence; the contrast branches keep chroma in both terms, so no amplification of the melody. Test planned: 3-branch guidance with the standard melody CFG kept plus the era contrast.

## 2026-10-30 Three-branch guidance: standard melody CFG + era contrast (results/tri_*, gens_summary_tri.txt, vinet_identity.json; src/61_triple_guidance.py, run_tri.sh)
Config: logits = L(null) + gm (L(neutral+chroma) - L(null)) + ge (L(era+chroma) - L(neutral+chroma)) on log-softmaxed branch logits per codebook; gm = 3 throughout, top-k 50, T 1; one token drawn per codebook inside a logits processor from the combined distribution and returned as a one-hot so the duplicated batch rows stay identical (details in the script header). Same 24 validation songs, 3 seeds, codec-matched MERT/MuQ probes. Why: tests the explanation (logged above, untested at the time) that the identity drop of contrast guidance comes from losing the melody-amplifying standard CFG term. ge = gm = 3 is mathematically the standard CFG with the era prompt: sanity run.
| set | MERT 1960s / 1980s / 2010s | song-ID top-1 | VINet top-1 (neutral .722) |
|---|---|---|---|
| standard CFG 3, base (reference, tables_main.txt) | -.001* / +.593 / +.328 | .892 | - |
| 3-branch base gm3 ge3 (sanity) | +.021* / +.526 / +.355 | .865 | .694 / .681 / .792 |
| 3-branch base gm3 ge5 | +.071 / +.676 / +.489 | .875 | .653 / .542 / .722 |
| LoRA v1 alone (CFG 3) | +.396 / +.246 / +.171 | .878 | .722 / .556 / .639 |
| 3-branch LoRA v1 gm3 ge5 | +.522 / +.390 / +.250 | .875 | .667 / .653 / .639 |
| 3-branch LoRA v1 gm3 ge7 | +.618 / +.517 / +.353 | .875 | .764 / .597 / .569 |
| LoRA v1 + neg-prompt g3 (earlier entry) | +.612 / +.324 / +.346 | .674 | .319 / .417 / .431 |
(* p > .05.) Observations: (1) the sanity run reproduces the standard CFG reference within sampling noise (different RNG stream), so the implementation is consistent. (2) Keeping the melody term at gm=3 keeps song-ID top-1 at .865-.875 for every ge, versus .67-.68 for all contrast-only runs; VINet top-1 also stays near the neutral value instead of halving. (3) LoRA v1 + ge7 gives the largest 1960s/1980s/2010s shifts among identity-preserving sets so far (+.618/+.517/+.353, all 23-24 of 24 songs, p<.001; MuQ +.478/+.351/+.286), at the same identity as LoRA alone; compared with LoRA + neg-prompt g3 it has the same 1960s/2010s shift and a higher 1980s shift at much higher identity. (4) On the base model ge5 raises 1980s/2010s (+.676/+.489) but not 1960s (+.071), consistent with the 1960s direction being learned only by the LoRA. This supports (does not prove) the explanation that the earlier identity drop came from dropping the melody-amplifying term. Single seed of adapter and generation.

## 2026-10-30 Three-branch guidance, extension: era scale, melody scale, top-k (results/tri_lora_m3e10*, tri_lora_m5e7*, tri_lora_m3e7k250*, gens_summary_tri2.txt; src/run_tri.sh, run_tri_k.sh)
Config as in the entry above (LoRA v1, 24 songs, 3 seeds, same probes); gm = melody CFG scale, ge = era-contrast scale, top-k 50 unless noted.
| set | MERT 1960s / 1980s / 2010s | MuQ | song-ID top-1 | VINet top-1 (neutral .722) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| gm3 ge7 (earlier entry) | +.618 / +.517 / +.353 | +.478 / +.351 / +.286 | .875 | .764 / .597 / .569 |
| gm3 ge10 | +.698 / +.648 / +.424 | +.588 / +.475 / +.370 | .875 | .681 / .681 / .667 |
| gm5 ge7 | +.502 / +.210 / +.237 | +.360 / +.123 / +.162 | .854 | .722 / .736 / .625 |
| gm3 ge7 + top-k 250 | +.584 / +.521 / +.395 | +.503 / +.335 / +.364 | .899 | .681 / .653 / .597 |
All shifts p<.001, 20-24 of 24 songs positive. Observations: (1) raising ge from 5 to 7 to 10 at gm=3 increases the shift for all three decades (MERT 1960s +.522/+.618/+.698; 1980s +.390/+.517/+.648; 2010s +.250/+.353/+.424) with song-ID top-1 unchanged (.875); VINet top-1 is lower at ge10 for 1960s (.681) than neutral, within the spread of the other sets. (2) A larger melody scale (gm5, ge7) gives smaller shifts than gm3, ge7 (the effective era term ge/gm shrinks relative to the melody term); song-ID top-1 .854. (3) top-k 250 at gm3 ge7 gives about the same shifts as top-k 50 (+.584/+.521/+.395 vs +.618/+.517/+.353) and the highest song-ID top-1 (.899) of this family. Interpretation: within this grid the era scale buys shift without an identity cost, no saturation reached at ge10; ge > 10 not tested (sound quality at high scales not yet scored: Audiobox/KAD for these sets are being computed).

## 2026-10-30 E07 FD-VGG against real decade clips, with song-bootstrap CI (results/fd_boot_<set>.json, fd_vgg_table.txt, fd_boot_log.txt; src/run_avb.sh, 83_fd_bootstrap.py, 84_fd_table.py)
Config: av-benchmark (checkout f351b9a with the CLAP/soundfile patches of the PA3 setup, conda env hw3_avbench, torchvggish hub cache copied to ~/era_market/torch_home so the trust prompt does not block) extracts VGGish features (10 s, 10 frames x 128-d per clip, first 10 s of each 30 s remix). Reference: PA1 dataset-A train clips of the decade (171 per decade, real mixes). Generated: the remix (original vocal + generated accompaniment) of the 24 validation songs x 3 seeds = 72 clips per condition (era prompt of the same decade, or neutral). FD recomputed from the cached features with the av-benchmark formula (`83_fd_bootstrap.py`); for every cell the recomputed value equals the av-benchmark json value to 3 decimals. Statistic: Delta = FD(era prompt, real decade) - FD(neutral, same real decade), negative = closer to the real decade; 95% CI by bootstrap over the 24 songs (all seeds of a song resampled together, 1000 resamples), reference fixed. FD-PANN (2048-d, one vector per clip) and FD-PaSST are not bootstrapped: 72 clips per condition are fewer than the feature dimension, so their covariances are rank-deficient (the av-benchmark values for them are in avb/out on the server, not reported). Why this metric: HW3 course metric (FD-VGG) and a distributional check that does not use the MERT/MuQ probes.
Table (full: results/fd_vgg_table.txt; * = CI excludes 0):
| set | neutral FD 1960s/1980s/2010s | Delta FD 1960s | 1980s | 2010s |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| base, standard CFG 3 (gen) | 3.26 / 4.95 / 5.23 | -0.10 | -0.78* | +2.16* |
| LoRA v1 alone | 2.67 / 3.45 / 3.58 | -0.39* | -0.77* | -1.23* |
| LoRA v1 + neg-prompt g3 | same | +1.26* | -0.42 | +1.69* |
| 3-branch base ge5 | 3.26 / 4.95 / 5.23 | +0.01 | -0.26 | +2.64* |
| 3-branch LoRA gm3 ge5 | 2.67 / 3.45 / 3.58 | -0.25 | -1.02* | -1.08* |
| 3-branch LoRA gm3 ge7 | same | -0.34 | -0.99* | -1.16* |
| 3-branch LoRA gm3 ge7 + k250 | same | -0.57* | -0.88* | -1.18* |
| 3-branch LoRA gm3 ge10 | same | -0.14 | -0.52 | -1.13* |
| 3-branch LoRA gm3 ge15 | same | +0.23 | +0.22 | -1.01 |
| 3-branch LoRA gm3 ge20 | same | +0.44 | +0.09 | -0.92 |
Observations (single adapter seed and generation seed; n small): (1) The LoRA lowers the neutral FD against all three decades (2.67-3.58 vs 3.26-5.23 for the base model), i.e. the adapter moves generated audio towards the PA1 distribution irrespective of prompt. (2) A 2010s prompt on the base model raises FD to real 2010s clips (+2.16, CI excludes 0), although the probes (MERT/MuQ) and CLAP register a 2010s shift for it; with the LoRA the 2010s prompt lowers FD (-1.2). So FD-VGG and the probes disagree for the base model on the 2010s; no explanation tested. (3) Contrast-only guidance on top of the LoRA (neg-prompt g3) worsens FD for 1960s and 2010s (+1.26, +1.69), while the three-branch variants keep FD gains of the LoRA alone; the benefit of larger ge on FD stops at about ge 10 and ge 15-20 give CIs that include 0 for all three decades. (4) The mean over song-bootstrap CIs is wide (about ±1 FD); differences between three-branch settings (ge 5/7/10) are not distinguishable.

Independent third evaluator (Essentia Discogs-EffNet embedding, logistic-regression decade probe trained on EnCodec-resynthesized real accompaniment; validation accuracy .523 on 6 decades, chance .167; codec-matched like the MERT/MuQ probes). Statistic: per-song Delta P(target decade | era prompt) - P(target decade | neutral), 24 songs, 3 seeds, Wilcoxon. Selected rows (full table with top tags: results/discogs_table.txt):
| set | 1960s | 1980s | 2010s |
|---|---|---|---|
| base standard CFG 3 (`eval`) | -.064 (9/24, p=.03) | +.282 (24/24) | +.138 (24/24) |
| delta g5 | +.053 (18/24, p=.09) | +.381 | +.042 |
| neg-prompt g5 | +.174 | +.458 | +.068 |
| Qwen3-8B prompts (draw 1) | +.136 | +.287 | +.176 |
| LoRA dropout 0.3 s2 | +.247 | +.187 | +.044 |
| LoRA lr 3e-4 s2 | +.261 | +.197 | +.073 |
| no-chroma LoRA s2 | +.185 | +.450 | +.036 |
| LoRA v1 s1 | +.323 | +.250 | +.068 |
| LoRA v1 + neg-prompt g3 | +.315 | +.190 | +.068 |
| 3-branch base ge5 | -.056 (9/24, p=.13) | +.336 | +.138 |
| 3-branch LoRA gm3 ge7 | +.479 | +.358 | +.152 |
| 3-branch LoRA gm3 ge10 | +.542 | +.419 | +.180 |
Observations: the tagger reproduces the sign pattern of the MERT/MuQ probes (1980s strongest on the base model, 1960s only with LoRA or with prompt rewriting) with a third, genre-supervised encoder; the ordering ge10 > ge7 > LoRA alone holds here too. Tags moved by the 1980s prompt on the base model are Synthwave / Synth-pop; for the three-branch LoRA 1960s prompt Easy Listening / Space-Age / Swing; for the 2010s prompt Alternative Rock / Indie Rock (not electronic tags); neutral-prompt probability of the 1960s class under this probe was not inspected.

Config as the three-branch entries (LoRA v1, gm 3, top-k 50, 24 songs, 3 seeds).
| ge | MERT 1960s / 1980s / 2010s | MuQ | song-ID top-1 | VINet top-1 (neutral .722) | Audiobox PQ era (neutral 7.31) | KAD delta MERT |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
