| v1 (seed 0) | LoRA alone | +.396 / +.246 / +.171 | +.296 / +.220 / +.102 | .878 | .722 / .556 / .639 (neutral .722) |
| v1 | gm3 ge7 | +.618 / +.517 / +.353 | +.478 / +.351 / +.286 | .875 | .764 / .597 / .569 |
| v1 | gm3 ge10 | +.698 / +.648 / +.424 | +.588 / +.475 / +.370 | .875 | .681 / .681 / .667 |
| v1s1 | LoRA alone | +.414 / +.270 / +.121 | +.264 / +.210 / +.081 | .882 | - |
| v1s1 | gm3 ge7 | +.658 / +.635 / +.286 | +.534 / +.458 / +.203 | .847 | .681 / .667 / .681 |
| v1s1 | gm3 ge10 | +.709 / +.708 / +.378 | +.676 / +.480 / +.320 | .809 | .583 / .694 / .611 |
| v1s2 | LoRA alone | +.366 / +.243 / +.068 | +.205 / +.191 / +.048 | .858 | - |
| v1s2 | gm3 ge7 | +.601 / +.614 / +.262 | +.482 / +.464 / +.248 | .868 | .708 / .667 / .597 |
| v1s2 | gm3 ge10 | +.661 / +.746 / +.387 | +.552 / +.510 / +.386 | .851 | .611 / .625 / .681 |
All three-branch rows: 22-24 of 24 songs positive, p<.001. Observations: (1) The three-branch gain over LoRA alone replicates in all three adapter seeds for all three decades and both probes (gain over the same adapter alone, MERT 1960s: +.22 to +.24 at ge7 and +.29 to +.30 at ge10; 1980s: +.27 to +.37 at ge7 and +.40 to +.50 at ge10). (2) Raising ge from 7 to 10 raises the MERT shift in every seed and decade. (3) Song-ID top-1 stays .81-.88; the seed-1 adapter loses more at ge10 (.809) than seeds 0 and 2 (.875, .851), so the identity cost at ge10 is seed-dependent. (4) VINet top-1 for the guided sets is at or somewhat below the same adapter's neutral value in these seeds. FD-VGG for the two new seeds not computed. Interpretation: the effect is not a single-adapter artefact; the identity-cost spread at ge10 indicates ge 7 is the safer reporting setting.

## 2026-10-31 FD-VGG for the three-branch replicates on LoRA seeds 1 and 2 (results/fd_boot_tri_lora_m3e{7,10}v1s{1,2}.json, fd_vgg_table.txt; protocol as the 2026-10-30 FD-VGG entry)
Delta FD-VGG = FD(era prompt) - FD(neutral of the same adapter) against real PA1 train clips of the decade; 95% CI by song bootstrap (* excludes 0). Neutral FD for these adapters, 1960s / 1980s / 2010s: v1s1 2.64 / 4.24 / 4.28, v1s2 2.57 / 4.29 / 4.44 (adapter seed 0: 2.67 / 3.45 / 3.58).
| set | 1960s | 1980s | 2010s |
|---|---|---|---|
| v1s1 gm3 ge7 | -0.43 | -1.48* | -1.98* |
| v1s1 gm3 ge10 | +0.02 | -0.83 | -1.88* |
| v1s2 gm3 ge7 | +0.06 | -1.64* | -1.91* |
| v1s2 gm3 ge10 | +0.25 | -0.90* | -2.05* |
Observations: the 1980s and 2010s FD gains replicate in both further seeds (CI excludes 0 in 7 of 8 cells), the 1960s FD does not improve in any of these four (CI includes 0), unlike the probes, which show the largest 1960s shift. As for seed 0, ge10 is not better than ge7 on FD (1980s -0.83/-0.90 vs -1.48/-1.64). Interpretation: FD-VGG favours ge 7 over 10, in agreement with the identity cost at ge 10 found in the same seeds.

## 2026-10-31 E07 paired HW3 metrics: KL-PaSST to the original clip and LAION-CLAP text score (results/pair_<set>.json, pair_table.txt, pair_log.txt; src/run_avb_pair.sh, 85_pair_metrics.py)
Config: av-benchmark (same install and patches as the FD-VGG entry; CLAP checkpoint music_audioset_epoch_15_esc_90.14). Pairing: each generated remix (24 validation songs x 3 seeds, first 10 s) is paired with ITS OWN original PA1 clip (the real mix, same song id), so KL-PaSST measures how differently PaSST classifies the generated clip and the original (KL(original || generated), lower = more similar); this is a content-preservation measure, not the HW3 retrieval-based protocol. CLAP text score: cosine of the CLAP audio embedding of the generated clip and the text embedding of the prompt it was generated from. Per-clip values were recomputed from the cached features (`85_pair_metrics.py`); the means equal the av-benchmark outputs in all 40 cells to 4 decimals. Statistics per set and era: Delta KL = KL(era prompt) - KL(neutral); Delta CLAP = cos(audio(era prompt), era text) - cos(audio(neutral), same era text); per song = mean of its 3 seeds, 95% CI by bootstrap over the 24 songs.
Full table with CIs: results/pair_table.txt. Selected rows (Delta CLAP / Delta KL for 1960s, 1980s, 2010s):
| set | Delta CLAP | Delta KL |
|---|---|---|
| base standard CFG 3 | +.130 / +.228 / +.224 | -.158* / -.135 / +.025 |
| base 3-branch ge5 | +.160 / +.235 / +.226 | -.289* / -.170 / +.031 |
| LoRA v1 alone | +.043 / +.070 / +.037 | +.054 / -.106 / -.073 |
| LoRA v1 3-branch ge7 | +.063 / +.164 / +.071 | -.034 / -.210* / -.203* |
| LoRA v1 3-branch ge10 | +.074 / +.210 / +.070 | -.018 / -.202* / -.191* |
| LoRA v1 3-branch ge20 | +.077 / +.229 / +.105 | -.121 / -.135 / -.234* |
| LoRA v1 + neg-prompt g3 | +.077 / +.155 / +.155 | -.022 / -.014 / +.030 |
(CLAP CIs exclude 0 in every row; * = KL CI excludes 0.)
Observations: (1) CLAP says the era-prompted audio matches the era text better than neutral audio does in every set and decade; the LoRA sets show smaller CLAP gains than the base model (+.04 to +.23 vs +.13 to +.23), and the gain grows with ge for the 1980s (+.164, +.210, +.229) and 2010s (+.071, +.070, +.105). (2) KL to the original clip: the three-branch LoRA ge7 and ge10 rows (and the 2010s of ge20) have a lower KL than neutral for the 1980s and 2010s (CI excludes 0; ge20 1980s CI includes 0), i.e. the era-prompted clip is classified more like the original than the neutral LoRA clip is; for the base model and contrast-only guidance the KL change is not distinguishable from 0 for 2010s. No explanation tested; the original clips have vocals and real production while the neutral LoRA outputs may differ in genre distribution. (3) The seed-1/2 adapters' neutral KL is higher (.90, .93) than seed 0 (.77), so their larger Delta KL partly reflects a worse neutral baseline.

## 2026-10-31 probe training-set robustness (`src/73_probe_augment.py` -> `results/probe_augment.{json,txt}`; CPU, stored embeddings)
Why: the Delta metric depends on a probe trained on real stems; the main protocol trains on EnCodec-resynthesized stems (codec-matched). This asks whether the conclusions depend on that choice. Config: logistic regression C=0.01 on dataset-A TRAIN accompaniment stems at the codec-probe layers (MERT L18, MuQ L9); three training sets: codec (main), raw (original stems), union (original + codec-resynthesized, every clip twice). Stored embeddings of generated sets (24 songs, 3 seeds) are rescored; Delta = per-song P(target decade | era prompt) - P(. | neutral), mean with 95% song-bootstrap CI (2000 resamples). The codec rows reproduce the logged values (for example base g3 MERT +.593 1980s, +.328 2010s).
Validation accuracy (original / codec-resynthesized validation stems): MERT codec .485/.462, raw .492/.364, union .523/.462; MuQ codec .371/.432, raw .424/.348, union .394/.424.
Delta, MERT (1960s / 1980s / 2010s), codec | raw | union:
| set | codec | raw | union |
|---|---|---|---|
| base g3 | -.001 / +.593 / +.328 | -.032 / +.042 / +.394 | +.049 / +.555 / +.249 |
| base g5 | +.049 / +.593 / +.474 | +.016 / +.011 / +.509 | +.142 / +.506 / +.416 |
| LoRA alone | +.396 / +.246 / +.171 | +.356 / +.024 / +.227 | +.404 / +.229 / +.162 |
| three-branch LoRA ge7 | +.618 / +.517 / +.353 | +.508 / +.056 / +.426 | +.585 / +.466 / +.356 |
| three-branch LoRA ge10 | +.698 / +.648 / +.424 | +.561 / +.114 / +.497 | +.648 / +.613 / +.448 |
| three-branch LoRA ge20 | +.759 / +.826 / +.649 | +.585 / +.230 / +.637 | +.681 / +.815 / +.692 |
MuQ shows the same pattern (for example ge10: codec +.588 / +.475 / +.370, raw +.519 / +.339 / +.458, union +.623 / +.473 / +.420); all sets, both encoders, and CIs are in `results/probe_augment.txt`.
Observations: (1) the union probe gives the same sign and similar size as the codec probe in all 10 sets checked and both encoders (1980s and 2010s CIs exclude 0 in every row); the ordering ge7 < ge10 < ge20 holds under all three training sets in both encoders. (2) The raw-trained probe barely registers the 1980s shift of generated audio with MERT (+.01 to +.23 where the codec probe gives +.25 to +.83), while it still sees 1960s and 2010s; the 1960s effect of the LoRA does not depend on the training set. (3) Interpretation (not tested further): a probe trained on original stems misses the generated 1980s signal because generated audio carries codec artifacts the probe has not seen; this is the reason the main protocol is codec-matched. Double-codec or generated-audio augmentation were not run.

## 2026-10-31 DSP baseline components (`src/74_dsp_components.py` -> `results/dsp_components.{json,txt}`; GPU 2)
Why: earlier logs show the crude "old" filter lowers the decade-probe output (older-sounding) and the "new" filter does not; the open question was which part of the filter chain moves which encoder. Config: 24 validation songs of dataset A, original accompaniment stems; components applied alone and in the two chains (old = band-limit 60 Hz-7 kHz -> tanh clip drive 1.8 -> hiss -50 dB; new = peak-normalize -> tanh limiter drive 3 -> + 0.6 x 3 kHz high-passed copy); codec-matched probes (MERT L18, MuQ L9), loudness-normalized input; statistic = per-song change of expected decade index versus the unprocessed stem, Wilcoxon over songs (no multiplicity correction).
| component | MERT d(expected decade) [songs > 0] p | MuQ d(expected decade) [songs > 0] p |
|---|---|---|
| band-limit | -0.296 [5/24] 0.0008 | -0.614 [0/24] <1e-4 |
| clip | -0.008 [12/24] 0.90 | +0.026 [15/24] 0.34 |
| hiss | -0.232 [5/24] 0.0059 | -0.141 [1/24] <1e-4 |
| limiter | -0.051 [14/24] 0.83 | +0.045 [15/24] 0.24 |
| shelf | -0.010 [7/24] 0.49 | +0.010 [14/24] 0.73 |
| old chain | -0.334 [7/24] 0.014 | -0.606 [0/24] <1e-4 |
| new chain | -0.071 [14/24] 0.75 | +0.044 [15/24] 0.26 |
Observations: both encoders move toward earlier decades only for the band-limit and the added hiss; soft clipping, the limiter and the high shelf do not move either probe. The band-limit alone accounts for about the whole MuQ response to the old chain (-0.61 of -0.61), and the band-limit plus hiss for the MERT response (-0.30, -0.23 vs -0.33). The earlier "old vs new" difference is therefore a bandwidth/noise-floor effect. Interpretation (not tested): the probes use bandwidth and noise floor as an era cue, which the real-audio probes could learn from decade-correlated recording bandwidth. Measured side note: the band-limit also raises MuQ P(1980s) by +0.144 and P(1960s) by +0.112 per song, so a bandwidth change alone produces a 1980s-direction shift in the MuQ probe of a size comparable to some era-prompt Deltas; MuQ 1980s Deltas are therefore not separable from bandwidth changes without a bandwidth check on the generated audio (not run).

## 2026-10-31 bandwidth / noise-floor check of generated audio (`src/75_bandwidth_check.py` -> `results/bandwidth_check.{json,txt}`; CPU)
Why: the DSP-component ablation (above) shows band-limit and hiss move both probes, so the generated-audio Deltas need a bandwidth check. Config: generated accompaniment of 24 songs x 3 seeds (seed mean per song), loudness-normalized -23 LUFS; features = 95% spectral rolloff (Hz) and energy fraction above 7 kHz (dB); per-song change versus the neutral prompt (Wilcoxon over songs); Spearman across song x era (n = 72) between the change in a feature and the change in P(target decade) from the codec-matched probes of the same set.
| set | era - neutral rolloff95 (Hz) | era - neutral HF > 7 kHz (dB) |
|---|---|---|
| base g3 | 1960s +416*, 1980s +371*, 2010s -48 | +8.6*, +11.8*, +12.8* |
| LoRA v1 alone | -73, +222*, -421* | -1.0, +4.0*, -2.7* |
| three-branch LoRA ge10 | +98, +840*, -627* | +1.2, +8.1*, -2.6* |
(* Wilcoxon p < 0.05, no multiplicity correction; neutral rolloff 970 Hz base / 1505 Hz LoRA.) Spearman between change in P(target) and change in rolloff: MERT -0.14 / +0.26 / +0.44, MuQ +0.06 / +0.22 / +0.20 for base / LoRA / three-branch ge10 (significant: MERT LoRA p=0.029, MERT three-branch p=0.0001); with HF energy: MERT +0.23 / +0.17 / +0.37, MuQ +0.11 / +0.23 / +0.14 (MERT three-branch p=0.0013).
Observations: (1) the era prompt changes high-frequency content in every set: the 1980s prompt adds high-frequency energy (+4 to +12 dB above 7 kHz), the LoRA 2010s prompt removes it; for the base model the 2010s prompt adds +12.8 dB without a rolloff change. (2) The correlation between probe change and bandwidth change is weak to moderate (|rho| up to 0.44), not near 1, and is absent for the base model, so bandwidth does not explain the probe change alone; but for the three-branch LoRA a part of the MERT change covaries with bandwidth, and the bandwidth was not held fixed. (3) The directions are not those of the DSP ablation (there band-limiting moved the probes toward earlier decades): the prompt that adds bandwidth (1980s) also gives the largest positive Delta. No bandwidth-matched control generation was run.

## 2026-10-31 long-term-spectrum-matched control (`src/76_spectrum_match.py` -> `results/spectrum_match.{json,txt}`; GPU 2)
Why: the bandwidth check (above) shows era prompts change spectral content; this removes the long-term spectral difference from the era clips and rescoring shows how much of the Delta remains. Config: every era clip is equalized to the long-term average spectrum of the neutral clip of the same song and seed (STFT 2048/512, power averaged over time, smoothed over +-8 bins, gain limited to +-24 dB); neutral kept; 24 songs x 3 seeds; codec-matched probes; Delta with 95% song-bootstrap CI.
Delta (1960s / 1980s / 2010s), unmatched -> spectrum-matched:
| set | MERT | MuQ |
|---|---|---|
| base g3 | -.001 / +.593 / +.328 -> -.014 / +.426 / +.312 | +.006 / +.414 / +.094 -> +.078 / +.333 / -.044 |
| LoRA alone | +.396 / +.246 / +.171 -> +.382 / +.184 / +.169 | +.296 / +.220 / +.102 -> +.314 / +.127 / +.040 |
| three-branch LoRA ge10 | +.698 / +.648 / +.424 -> +.665 / +.553 / +.406 | +.588 / +.475 / +.370 -> +.552 / +.348 / +.187 |
Observations: (1) with MERT every 1980s and 2010s Delta that was positive stays positive with the CI excluding 0 after matching (1980s loses 0.06 to 0.17, 2010s loses 0.00 to 0.02). (2) With MuQ the effect shrinks more: base 2010s goes from +.094 [+.033,+.156] to -.044 [-.097,+.009] (CI includes 0), LoRA 2010s to +.040 (CI excludes 0), three-branch ge10 2010s halves (+.370 to +.187), 1980s loses 0.09 to 0.13 but stays positive. (3) The 1960s LoRA/three-branch Delta is unchanged by matching in both encoders. Interpretation (not tested further): a part of the MuQ 2010s and of the 1980s response is long-term spectral balance; the rest survives equalization, so it is not explained by the average spectrum alone. This matching removes only the time-averaged spectrum, not dynamics, reverb, or stereo/phase cues.

## 2026-10-31 double-codec probe augmentation (`src/77_probe_doublecodec.py` -> `results/probe_doublecodec.{json,txt}`; GPU 2)
Why: closes the "double-codec augmentation not run" gap of the probe-robustness entry. Config: all 1290 dataset-A accompaniment stems passed through the MusicGen EnCodec (2.2 kbps) a second time (on top of the codec-resynthesized stems), loudness-normalized, embedded at the codec-probe layers (MERT L18, MuQ L9); probes trained on codec (main), double-codec only, or codec+double (TRAIN clips of both, 2x); same statistic and 6 stored generated sets (24 songs, 3 seeds) as `73_probe_augment.py`.
Validation accuracy (codec / double-codec val stems): MERT codec .462/.417, double .432/.432, codec+double .455/.424; MuQ codec .432/.402, double .424/.371, codec+double .439/.417.
Delta MERT (1960s / 1980s / 2010s), codec -> codec+double: base g3 -.001 / +.593 / +.328 -> -.013 / +.550 / +.388; LoRA alone +.396 / +.246 / +.171 -> +.432 / +.260 / +.204; three-branch ge10 +.698 / +.648 / +.424 -> +.722 / +.689 / +.493; ge20 +.759 / +.826 / +.649 -> +.769 / +.856 / +.706. MuQ and the double-only probe are in `results/probe_doublecodec.txt`.
Observations: in all 6 sets and both encoders, the 1980s and 2010s Deltas keep sign and CI excluding 0 under the double-codec and codec+double probes, differences from the codec probe are at most 0.09 (largest: MuQ base 2010s +.094 -> +.174, tri_base ge5 MuQ 2010s +.189 -> +.266); the 1960s base-model Delta stays at about 0; the ge7 < ge10 < ge20 order holds in every probe/encoder. Interpretation: the conclusions do not depend on a single codec pass in the probe training data. A probe trained on generated audio was not run (needs labels the generated audio does not have).

## 2026-10-31 six-decade controllability with three-branch guidance (`src/61_triple_guidance.py --eras`, `src/run_tri_x.sh`, `src/run_tri.sh`, `src/78_six_decades.py` -> `results/six_decades.{json,txt}`, `results/trix_*_eval.json`, `results/tri_base_m3e7_eval.json`; GPUs 0-3)
Why: all earlier guidance results use the 1960s / 1980s / 2010s prompts; this tests whether the same guidance moves the codec-matched probes along the whole decade axis. Config: three-branch guidance (gm=3, era weight ge as stated), prompts for 1970s / 1990s / 2000s from `prompts_era.py` (same prompts as the extra-prompt run), base model ge7, LoRA v1 ge7 and ge10 (the adapter was trained on clips of all six decades); 24 validation songs x 3 seeds; neutral clips of the same model reused (base: `gen`, LoRA: `gen_lora`); decade axis index 0 = 1960s .. 5 = 2010s. Statistic: per-song P(target decade | era prompt) - P(. | neutral), 95% song-bootstrap CI; E = mean expected decade index of the era prompt's clips; top1 = share of clips whose most probable decade is the target.
MERT, Delta [CI] / E / top1:
| prompt | base ge7 | LoRA ge7 | LoRA ge10 |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1960s | +.151 [+.105,+.201] / 2.80 / .29 | +.618 / 0.51 / .97 | +.698 / 0.26 / .99 |
| 1970s | -.036 [-.053,-.021] / 4.46 / .00 | +.470 / 1.39 / .64 | +.595 / 1.15 / .82 |
| 1980s | +.729 [+.686,+.770] / 2.47 / .99 | +.517 / 2.42 / .74 | +.648 / 2.25 / .88 |
| 1990s | +.035 [-.009,+.080] / 3.50 / .12 | +.172 / 3.26 / .33 | +.256 / 3.31 / .46 |
| 2000s | -.130 [-.206,-.053] / 4.12 / .29 | +.229 / 4.04 / .83 | +.257 / 4.05 / .82 |
| 2010s | +.547 [+.502,+.593] / 4.62 / 1.00 | +.353 / 4.36 / .65 | +.424 / 4.49 / .75 |
MuQ values (CIs, E, top1) are in `results/six_decades.txt`; same pattern (LoRA ge10: 1960s +.588, 1970s +.562, 1980s +.475, 1990s +.177, 2000s +.318, 2010s +.370, all CIs exclude 0; E 0.65, 1.43, 2.38, 3.08, 3.91, 4.30).
Observations: (1) with the LoRA the six prompts give expected decades that increase monotonically with the prompted decade in both encoders (MERT ge10: 0.26, 1.15, 2.25, 3.31, 4.05, 4.49), and every Delta CI excludes 0; the 1990s prompt is the weakest (top1 .33-.46 MERT, .36-.43 MuQ). (2) The base model moves only 1980s and 2010s clearly: its 1970s prompt gives a negative Delta (CI excludes 0) and an expected decade of 4.46 (MERT), the 2000s Delta is negative in MERT and about 0 in MuQ, and 1990s about 0. (3) Going from ge7 to ge10 raises the Delta for 1960s, 1970s, 1980s, 1990s and 2010s in MERT and MuQ (2000s +.03 MERT, +.06 MuQ). Interpretation (not tested further): the LoRA, trained with decade-labeled stems for all six decades, is what makes the extra decades promptable; guidance alone on the base model does not. These are probe-based scores; no listening test.

## 2026-10-31 six-decade guidance sweep: ge15/ge20, LoRA training seeds, content (`src/run_tri_x2.sh`, `src/78_six_decades.py`, `src/79_extra_content_table.py` -> `results/six_decades.{json,txt}` (7 configurations), `results/extra_content.txt`, `results/trix_*_{eval,content}.json`; GPUs 0-3)
Why: follow-up of the six-decade entry above: does a larger ge help the weakest prompts (1990s, 2000s), do the extra decades replicate on the LoRA training seeds, and what does the extra-decade guidance cost in melody preservation. Config as above (24 songs x 3 seeds, gm=3, three-branch guidance, adapters trained on all six decades; seed adapters v1s1 / v1s2 at ge10 with their own neutral clips); statistic as above.
MERT, Delta / expected decade E / top1 (target) for the 1990s and 2000s prompts and the span of E:
| config | 1990s | 2000s | E for 1960s ... 2010s |
|---|---|---|---|
| LoRA ge7 | +.172 / 3.26 / .33 | +.229 / 4.04 / .83 | 0.51, 1.39, 2.42, 3.26, 4.04, 4.36 |
| LoRA ge10 | +.256 / 3.31 / .46 | +.257 / 4.05 / .82 | 0.26, 1.15, 2.25, 3.31, 4.05, 4.49 |
| LoRA ge15 | +.338 / 3.20 / .58 | +.202 / 4.16 / .76 | 0.12, 1.06, 2.11, 3.20, 4.16, 4.71 |
| LoRA ge20 | +.373 / 3.22 / .58 | +.181 / 4.19 / .65 | 0.11, 1.01, 2.06, 3.22, 4.19, 4.81 |
| seed 1 ge10 | +.299 / 3.39 / .46 | +.335 / 4.08 / .86 | 0.16, 1.10, 2.18, 3.39, 4.08, 4.54 |
| seed 2 ge10 | +.331 / 3.39 / .40 | +.362 / 4.03 / .92 | 0.28, 1.08, 2.23, 3.39, 4.03, 4.51 |
MuQ in `results/six_decades.txt` (e.g. ge20: 1990s +.259, 2000s +.282, E 0.28 ... 4.70). Content, song-ID top-1 / chord agreement vs the original mix (`results/extra_content.txt`; neutral clips top1 .944 / .352 for the main adapter): LoRA ge7 1970s .792 / .342, 1990s .861 / .360, 2000s .889 / .360; ge10 .833 / .337, .875 / .348, .903 / .367; ge15 .778 / .328, .917 / .348, .847 / .351; ge20 .764 / .317, .889 / .332, .792 / .356; base ge7 1970s .958 / .314, 1990s .847 / .306, 2000s .931 / .322 (neutral .847 / .344).
Observations: (1) every Delta CI excludes 0 for all six prompts in all six LoRA configurations and both encoders (CIs in the txt). (2) The 1990s Delta keeps rising with ge (+.172 to +.373 MERT), but its expected decade stays at 3.2-3.4, i.e. near the 1990s, and top1 saturates at .58; the 2000s Delta peaks at ge10 (MERT +.257) and falls at ge15 and ge20 (+.202, +.181) while E for the 2000s prompt moves toward the 2010s (4.04 to 4.19) — larger ge pulls the 2000s prompt toward the 2010s in the probe. (3) The ordering of E across the six prompts is monotone in all configurations including both seed adapters, and the 1990s/2000s Deltas at ge10 are higher for the seed adapters (+.30/+.33 and +.34/+.36) than for the main adapter (+.256, +.257). (4) Melody preservation: song-ID top-1 of the extra-decade remixes falls with ge for the 1970s (.833 at ge10, .764 at ge20 vs neutral .944) and the 2000s (.903 to .792); chord agreement drops from .352 to .317-.337 for 1970s. Interpretation: ge 10 is a compromise; no listening test, so a quality statement is not made.

| base ge7 | -.028 [13/24], +.007 [12/24], +.343 [24/24], +.162 [23/24], -.028 [10/24], +.128 [24/24] | 2.19, 3.08, 2.99, 3.31, 3.35, 3.49 |
| LoRA ge7 | +.479, +.235, +.358, +.195, +.120, +.152 (22-24/24 positive) | 0.96, 1.94, 2.49, 2.85, 3.41, 3.60 |
| LoRA ge10 | +.542, +.279, +.419, +.259, +.092, +.180 (19-24/24 positive) | 0.74, 1.79, 2.46, 2.90, 3.33, 3.73 |
Audiobox PQ (production quality) change vs the neutral prompt of the same set, 1970s / 1990s / 2000s [songs > 0]: base ge7 +0.11 [17/24] (p=.13) / +0.58 [22/24] / +0.58 [22/24] (neutral 7.07); LoRA ge7 +0.25 / +0.35 / +0.08 (n.s.); LoRA ge10 +0.30 / +0.46 / -0.03 (n.s.); LoRA ge15 +0.33 / +0.56 / -0.16 (n.s.); LoRA ge20 +0.38 / +0.56 / -0.27 [7/24] (p=.005) (neutral 7.31). CE (enjoyment): LoRA ge20 2000s -0.32 (p=.010); 1970s and 1990s CE +0.36 to +0.57 for LoRA.
Observations: (1) the independent encoder agrees with the MERT/MuQ result that the LoRA with guidance orders the six prompts monotonically in expected decade (E 0.74 to 3.73 at ge10) and that all six Deltas are positive and significant for the LoRA; the compression of E at the late end (3.33 for 2000s, 3.73 for 2010s) is stronger than in MERT (4.05 / 4.49). (2) The base model under this encoder shows positive Deltas only for the 1980s, 1990s and 2010s, and none for 1960s, 1970s, 2000s, i.e. the same failure on the early and 2000s prompts as in MERT/MuQ. (3) Quality: no quality loss for 1970s and 1990s prompts at any ge (PQ up); the 2000s prompt loses PQ and CE only at ge20 (-0.27 PQ, -0.32 CE), consistent with the drift of the 2000s prompt toward the 2010s seen in the probes at high ge (previous entry). No listening test.

## 2026-10-31 FD-VGG for the extra decades (`src/run_avb.sh` with `EL`, `src/83_fd_bootstrap.py` with `DEC` -> `results/fd_boot_trix_*.json`; GPUs 0-3)
Why: the distribution-level check of the six-decade result, as in the main FD-VGG entry. Config: av-benchmark FD-VGG (10 s remix clips) of each era set against real PA1 train clips (dataset A, 171 per decade) of the prompted decade; Delta FD = FD(era prompt vs real decade d) - FD(neutral prompt vs the same clips); song bootstrap (1000 resamples over 24 validation songs); lower FD = closer; recomputed FD equals av-benchmark to 3 decimals in all 12 cells.
| set | 1970s Delta [CI] | 1990s Delta [CI] | 2000s Delta [CI] |
|---|---|---|---|
| base ge7 | -0.81 [-1.91, -0.28] | +2.99 [+1.75, +3.82] | +1.93 [+0.81, +2.72] |
| LoRA ge7 | -1.08 [-2.50, -0.38] | -0.61 [-1.90, +0.25] | -1.00 [-2.60, -0.05] |
| LoRA ge10 | -0.72 [-2.07, -0.18] | -0.57 [-2.07, +0.41] | -0.92 [-2.49, -0.05] |
| LoRA ge20 (v1) | -0.03 [-1.34, +0.56] | +0.23 [-1.40, +0.91] | -0.80 [-2.38, +0.13] |
(neutral FD base 4.19 / 4.52 / 5.00; LoRA 2.90 / 3.06 / 3.38 for 1970s / 1990s / 2000s references.)
Observations: (1) the base model's 1990s and 2000s prompts move the generated distribution away from the real clips of that decade (CIs exclude 0, positive), matching its failure in the probes; its 1970s prompt lowers FD although the probes show a negative Delta for the 1970s prompt (disagreement between the FD and the probes for the base 1970s, not explained). (2) With the LoRA at ge7 and ge10 FD falls for the 1970s and 2000s (CIs exclude 0) and the 1990s Delta is negative with a CI that includes 0. (3) At ge20 no CI excludes 0 for these decades. Interpretation: as for the main decades, the guidance helps up to a moderate ge; 72 clips per condition keep the intervals wide.

## 2026-10-31 1990s prompt-wording variants with the LoRA (`src/61_triple_guidance.py` with `EXTRA_PROMPTS`, `src/prompts_1990s_variants.json`, `src/run_tri_p90.sh`, `src/78b_prompt_variants_decade.py` -> `results/prompt_variants_decade.txt`, `results/trp90_lora_m3e{10,15}_{eval,content}.json`; GPUs 0-1)
Why: the 1990s is the weakest prompt in the six-decade result (MERT top1 .46 at ge10, .58 at ge15); this asks whether the wording matters. Config: LoRA v1, three-branch guidance gm=3, ge10 and ge15, 24 songs x 3 seeds, four wordings (texts in `src/prompts_1990s_variants.json`): a = alternative rock / grunge, b = eurodance / house pop, c = r&b / new jack swing, d = "a recording from the nineties"; the default prompt (sampled breakbeat, early digital synths) is the reference from the six-decade entry. Statistic: Delta P(1990s) vs the neutral clips of the same adapter, 95% song-bootstrap CI, E = expected decade index, top1 = share of clips with the 1990s as most probable decade.
MERT (Delta [CI] / E / top1), default prompt -> variants:
| ge | default | a rock | b eurodance | c r&b | d plain |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 10 | +.256 / 3.31 / .46 | +.098 [+.043,+.163] / 3.86 / .19 | +.286 [+.233,+.343] / 3.50 / .51 | +.208 / 3.46 / .35 | +.184 / 3.29 / .39 |
| 15 | +.338 / 3.20 / .58 | +.108 / 3.86 / .14 | +.413 [+.344,+.476] / 3.41 / .71 | +.331 / 3.38 / .53 | +.175 / 3.33 / .38 |
MuQ (Delta, top1): default ge10 +.177 / .43, ge15 +.236 / .65; variants ge10: a -.023 (CI includes 0) / .10, b +.035 (CI includes 0) / .18, c +.137 / .42, d +.105 / .36; ge15: a +.016 (CI includes 0) / .17, b +.149 / .42, c +.173 / .46, d +.180 / .54.
Content (song-ID top-1 / chord agreement; neutral .944 / .352): ge10 a .806 / .349, b .833 / .358, c .847 / .349, d .750 / .334; ge15 a .833 / .348, b .875 / .344, c .847 / .345, d .694 / .316.
Observations: (1) wording changes the 1990s response a lot: the rock/grunge wording gives the weakest Delta in both encoders and an expected decade of 3.86 (the 2000s side), the eurodance wording the strongest MERT Delta (+.413, top1 .71 at ge15) but a MuQ Delta below the default prompt (+.149 vs +.236); no variant beats the default prompt in both encoders. (2) The plain "recording from the nineties" wording gives a lower melody retention at ge15 (song-ID .694) than the others (.833-.875). Interpretation: the 1990s probe response mixes era with genre in the wording (grunge pushes toward the 2000s in the probe); the decade is not a wording-independent control axis, and the two encoders disagree on which wording is best. Not tested: other genres, a listening test.

## 2026-10-31 paired metrics for the extra decades (`src/run_avb_pair.sh` with `EL`, `src/85_pair_metrics.py` with `EL` -> `results/pair_trix_*.json`; GPUs 0-3)
Why: the paired av-benchmark metrics (KL-PaSST to the clip's own original, LAION-CLAP text score of the generated audio against the prompt it was generated from) for the 1970s / 1990s / 2000s prompts, as in the main-decade entry. Config: 24 songs x 3 seeds, seed mean per song, 95% song-bootstrap CI (1000 resamples); captions from `prompts_era.py` (checked identical to the `07_generate.py` texts for the four main prompts); Delta = era prompt minus neutral of the same set.
| set | Delta KL 1970s | Delta KL 1990s | Delta KL 2000s | Delta CLAP 1970s | Delta CLAP 1990s | Delta CLAP 2000s |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| base ge7 | -.305* | -.082 | -.082 | +.154* | +.234* | +.135* |
| LoRA ge7 | -.129* | -.159* | -.164* | +.012 | +.016 | +.054* |
| LoRA ge10 | -.108 | -.210* | -.174* | +.015 | +.023* | +.075* |
| LoRA ge20 (v1) | -.075 | -.220* | -.199* | +.030* | +.057* | +.092* |
(* CI excludes 0; neutral KL base .955, LoRA .771; CLAP of the neutral clips against the era text: base .079 / .042 / .083, LoRA .099 / .017 / .046.)
Observations: (1) the CLAP text score rises for the base model in all three decades (+.14 to +.23, CIs exclude 0) although the probes show it failing on the 1970s and 2000s prompts, so the text-audio score and the decade probes disagree for the base model (not explained). For the LoRA the CLAP gains are small (+.01 to +.09) and for the 1970s and 1990s at ge7 the CI includes 0. (2) KL to the original clip falls for the LoRA in all three decades at ge7 (CIs exclude 0) and for the 1990s and 2000s at ge10 and ge20 (the 1970s CI includes 0 at ge10 and ge20); for the base model only the 1970s changes (-.305). The original-clip KL is lower for era-prompted clips than neutral clips, as for the main decades; no cause tested.

Config: LoRA seed adapters v1s1/v1s2, three-branch guidance, ge15 and ge20, same 24 songs x 3 seeds per decade as the other rows; main decades `tri_lora_m3e{15,20}v1s{1,2}`, extra decades `trix_lora_m3e{15,20}v1s{1,2}`. | ge | seed1 | seed2 |
|---|---|---|
| 7 | -0.43 / -1.48* / -1.98* | +0.06 / -1.64* / -1.91* |
| 10 | +0.02 / -0.83 / -1.88* | +0.25 / -0.90* / -2.05* |

## 2026-11-01 — 2000s prompt-wording variants (primary adapter, ge10 / ge15; `src/prompts_2000s_variants.json`, `run_tri_p00.sh`, `results/trp00_*`, `results/prompt_variants_2000s.txt`)
Config: LoRA v1, three-branch gm 3, 24 songs x 3 seeds; variants a hip-hop/r&b, b pop-punk/emo rock, c dance-pop/electro, d "a recording from the two-thousands"; delta = P(2000s | variant) - P(2000s | neutral).
MERT delta [CI] / E / top1 at ge10 and ge15: a +0.133 / 4.00 / .64 and +0.100 / 4.11 / .61; b +0.240 / 4.03 / .83 and +0.240 / 4.09 / .83; c -0.178 / 3.74 / .14 and -0.155 / 3.83 / .18; d -0.072 / 3.50 / .35 and -0.155 / 3.79 / .19. MuQ delta: a +0.155, +0.104; b +0.361, +0.313; c -0.046, -0.023; d +0.031, +0.006. For comparison the default wording (hand-written 2000s prompt) has MERT +0.257 and +0.202 (E 4.05, 4.16; top1 .82, .76) and MuQ +0.318 and +0.263.
Observation: the pop-punk/emo wording does not lose its effect from ge10 to ge15 (MERT +0.240 both; top1 .83 both) while the default wording does (+0.257 -> +0.202); its MuQ delta is higher than the default at both ge. Dance-pop and the bare-decade wording lower P(2000s) in MERT. Song-ID top1 (neutral .944): a .847/.806, b .833/.819, c .708/.708, d .875/.875.
Interpretation: the primary adapter's 2000s decline at high ge is wording-dependent; "2000s" in this probe's training data is separable by guitar-based wording but not by electronic wording. One decade, one adapter, one seed set.

## 2026-11-01 — Era-free bandwidth wording (`src/prompts_bandwidth.json`, `run_tri_pbw.sh`, `78c_bandwidth_prompts.py`, `results/trpbw_*`, `results/bandwidth_prompts.txt`)
Config: LoRA v1, three-branch gm 3 at ge10 and ge20, 24 songs x 3 seeds; prompts contain no decade: bw_hi "modern studio recording with extended high-frequency content, bright airy full-bandwidth sound", bw_lo "dull band-limited recording with rolled-off highs and tape hiss, narrow bandwidth sound". Change vs neutral of the probe's expected decade index E (0 = 1960s), song-bootstrap 95% CI:
| | MERT ge10 | MERT ge20 | MuQ ge10 | MuQ ge20 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| bw_hi dE | +0.42 [+0.20,+0.64] | +0.95 [+0.66,+1.24] | +0.97 [+0.76,+1.19] | +1.24 [+0.99,+1.48] |
| bw_lo dE | -1.34 [-1.65,-1.02] | -1.75 [-2.03,-1.44] | -0.98 [-1.26,-0.68] | -1.51 [-1.80,-1.20] |
Reference at ge10, same adapter: 1960s prompt dE = -2.79 (MERT, 0.26 vs neutral 3.05) and -2.29 (MuQ, 0.65 vs 2.94); 2010s prompt +1.44 / +1.36.
Observation: wording about bandwidth alone, with no era named, moves the probes by about half of the 1960s prompt effect (bw_lo, MERT ge10: P(1960s) +0.34, P(2000s) -0.27; bw_hi: P(2010s) +0.10 MERT, +0.28 MuQ at ge10, +0.35/+0.45 at ge20). Interpretation: the era probes respond to bandwidth/brightness cues; this is consistent with the post-hoc band-limit result (2026-10-31) and means part of the era-prompt effect can be carried by bandwidth. It does not show that all of it is: the spectrum-matched control (2026-10-31) kept the MERT 1980s/2010s deltas positive with CI excluding 0 after matching long-term spectra. A generation-time hard bandwidth constraint (decoder-level) was not run; this entry is the prompt-level version.

## 2026-11-01 — Review follow-ups: steering after nuisance removal; language-held-fixed market; genre-held-out decade (`src/94_nuisance_steering.py`, `src/95_controlled_tasks.py` -> `results/nuisance_steering.{json,txt}`, `results/controlled_tasks.{json,txt}`; CPU, stored embeddings)
Why: an external review (`deep-research-paper-review.md`) asked whether the steering effect survives removal of genre and bandwidth information, whether market is more than language, and whether decade generalizes across genre. Not done (blocked): recovery of Discogs ids / clique-level splits (the PA1 clips carry no ids), a listening test (needs participants), larger or held-out generation set, a nuisance-disentangled embedding used as the steering signal (method work, not started).
**Steering after nuisance removal.** Concept Z fitted on dataset-A TRAIN: style = top-10 PCs of Discogs-EffNet tag activations of the original mix; bw = 9 signal statistics of the original accompaniment stem; both = concatenation (rank 19). LEACE eraser fitted on codec-resynthesized TRAIN accompaniment embeddings (MERT L22, MuQ L9), era probe retrained on erased features, same eraser applied to stored embeddings of generated sets; Delta as before; random orthogonal projection of the same rank as control (20 draws): reproduces the unerased Delta within .004 in all sets (rows `*_random`). Real-audio validation accuracy after erasure (n=132): MERT none .462, style .417, bw .477, both .417 (Spearman of expected vs true decade .72 / .54 / .66 / .49); MuQ .432 / .333 / .379 / .295 (.69 / .54 / .61 / .40).
Delta (1960s / 1980s / 2010s), MERT, unerased -> erased with both: base standard CFG 3 -.001 / +.593 / +.328 -> +.069 / +.608 / -.020 [-.075,+.036]; adapter alone +.396 / +.246 / +.171 -> +.179 / +.165 / +.050 [+.004,+.099]; three-branch ge7 +.618 / +.517 / +.353 -> +.379 / +.382 / +.162; ge10 +.698 / +.648 / +.424 -> +.489 / +.503 / +.249; ge20 +.759 / +.826 / +.649 -> +.613 / +.747 / +.476; seed adapters ge10: seed1 +.709 / +.708 / +.378 -> +.558 / +.538 / +.218, seed2 +.661 / +.746 / +.387 -> +.494 / +.586 / +.175. MuQ ge10 +.588 / +.475 / +.370 -> +.316 / +.397 / +.178; base MuQ 2010s +.094 -> +.010 [-.040,+.062].
Observations: (1) every adapter three-branch Delta stays positive with the CI excluding 0 after erasing both nuisance sets, in both encoders, at ge7, 10, 20 and in both seed adapters; sizes shrink by 0.07 to 0.27 (MERT ge10: 1960s -.21, 1980s -.15, 2010s -.18). (2) The base model's 2010s Delta disappears (MERT +.328 -> -.020; MuQ +.094 -> +.010) while its 1980s Delta is unchanged (+.593 -> +.608): the base 2010s effect is carried by the nuisance directions, the base 1980s effect is not. (3) Erasing bandwidth statistics alone leaves the 1960s and 1980s three-branch effects nearly unchanged and removes about a third of the 2010s effect (MERT ge10 +.424 -> +.270); erasing style alone mostly reduces the 1960s (+.698 -> +.519 MERT, +.588 -> +.303 MuQ). (4) The erased probes are worse on real audio (MuQ both: .295 accuracy), so shrinkage may also reflect a weaker probe; erasure also moves the neutral baseline (base 1960s Delta becomes +.069 [+.035,+.106]). Interpretation: the adapter-plus-guidance effect is not explained by the linear style/bandwidth information measured here; part of it is shared with it.
**Market with sung language fixed (English-sung B clips, n=489, pooled train+val, not artist-grouped, 5-fold OOF x 3 seeds, MERT).** US vs UK (n=266, majority .504): mix acc .713 [.665,.762], vocals .642, accompaniment .673 (balanced accuracy and macro-F1 equal to accuracy within .002). Five-way without Brazil (n=477, majority .281): mix .398 [.360,.437] (balanced .349, macro-F1 .351), vocals .330, accompaniment .377. Reference, all languages six-way (n=900): mix .587 (balanced .587, macro-F1 .593). Observation: with language held at English the market signal is far smaller for the five-way task and, for US vs UK, still above chance (.713 vs .500); the accompaniment carries about as much as the vocals once language is fixed. Caveat: no artist ids, so the pooled OOF numbers can include artist leakage; the value for US vs UK in particular may be optimistic.
**Decade with a genre cluster held out (A pooled n=1158, 6 k-means clusters of the Discogs-EffNet embedding, sizes 153/220/125/165/460/35).** Leave-one-cluster-out accuracy, MERT mix .430 [.402,.459] (MAE .90 decades) vs stratified OOF .472 (MAE .80); accompaniment .436 [.408,.465] (MAE .94) vs .467 (.86). Per held-out cluster (acc / majority share) mix: .45/.60, .38/.20, .28/.35, .45/.25, .47/.20, .54/.29. Observation: holding out a whole genre cluster costs about .03 to .04 accuracy; accuracy stays above chance (.167) and above the majority share in 4 of 6 clusters. Interpretation: decade information is not only within-genre style, but clusters are a proxy for genre and the split is not artist-grouped.

## 2026-11-01 — Split audit without ids (`src/96_clique_proxy.py` -> `results/clique_proxy.{json,txt}`; GPU 1 for the embeddings)
Fact: the public manifests and READMEs of dataset A and B state that file names and manifest do not expose artist, title, album, Discogs ID or YouTube ID; the clips cannot be joined to Discogs-VI. Proxy: Discogs-VINet embedding (whole 30 s mix, as in `54_vinet_identity.py`) of every clip of A (1290) and B (1002), cosine graph, connected components at cosine >= T, group-aware 5-fold out-of-fold MERT mix probe vs ordinary stratified 5-fold.
Measured: nearest-neighbour cosine, median .716 (A) / .707 (B), 99th percentile .828 / .818; edges at T=.8: 40 (A, 16 across splits) and 20 (B, 6 across splits), 58 and 33 clips in groups of size >= 2; at T=.7 one giant component (759 of 1290, 435 of 1002 clips) and at T=.9 no edges. Group-aware OOF accuracy at T=.8: A .490 (stratified .472), B .582 (stratified .587); at T=.9 .474 / .596.
Observation: group-aware evaluation does not lower accuracy. Limit: VINet cosines are high for unrelated clips (our own generated covers of a song score about .65 against their original, below the nearest-neighbour cosine of a random clip), so no absolute threshold identifies same-composition pairs; only the retrieval rank is meaningful. The audit shows that a small number of high-similarity pairs exist (<= 4.5% of clips at T=.8) and that excluding them does not change the accuracies; it does not prove composition-disjointness.

## 2026-11-01 — M-1 nuisance-controlled ordinal era encoder (`src/97_era_encoder.py` -> `results/era_encoder_{mert,muq}_{adv,leace}.{json,txt}`, `emb/A_eraz_<enc>_<mode><lam>_s<seed>.npy` on the server; CPU)
Why: the review asked for an era representation learned with genre and bandwidth removed, to be used for steering. Design: frozen MERT layer-22 (MuQ layer-9) mean-pooled embedding of the codec-resynthesized accompaniment (dataset-A TRAIN 1026 clips, validation 132); MLP encoder to z (8-d); CORAL ordinal head for the decade; nuisance vector N = 10 style PCs (Discogs-EffNet tags of the original mix) + 9 signal statistics of the original accompaniment (standardized on TRAIN). Modes: `adv` = gradient-reversal adversary heads (lambda 0 / 0.1 / 0.3); `leace` = LEACE-erase N (rank 19) from the input features, then lambda 0. 300 epochs, batch 128, AdamW lr 1e-3, wd 1e-2, dropout .2, 3 training seeds, fixed before looking at validation. Leakage = held-out R2 of a fresh ridge / MLP from z to N (validation); lower = better hidden.
Measured (validation, mean of 3 seeds), MERT: adv lam 0: ordinal accuracy .303, MAE 0.97 decades, Spearman(expected decade, true) .705, leakage R2 ridge .102 / MLP .124; lam .1: .290, 0.98, .698, .084 / .097; lam .3: .288, 0.97, .700, .081 / .088; leace: .265, 1.31, .486, .018 / -.002. MuQ: adv lam 0 .293, 1.16, .649, .076 / .095; lam .1 .290, 1.15, .663, .118 / .128; lam .3 .280, 1.16, .629, .060 / .070; leace .273, 1.39, .479, .025 / .015. (An earlier run of the same script with lam 1 and 3, before the result files were split by mode and not kept, gave MERT Spearman .624 / .477 and leakage R2 .25 / .27 (ridge), MuQ Spearman .491 / .166 and leakage .25 / .26: leakage was higher than at lam 0, i.e. adversarial training was unstable at those weights; lam 1 and 3 were dropped from the rerun.)
Observation: gradient reversal at small weights barely reduces leakage (R2 .102 -> .081); LEACE on the input removes it (R2 ~ 0) but the era coordinate then predicts the decade clearly worse (Spearman .705 -> .486 MERT, .649 -> .479 MuQ). So about 30% of the rank correlation with the decade (in this feature space) depends on information linearly shared with style and bandwidth. The ordinal accuracy of the scalar CORAL head (.27-.30) is below the six-way logistic probe (.46) because the readout is one-dimensional.
Next: M-2 trains two era-axis conditioned adapters, `ax_free` (z from adv lam 0, seed 0: uncontrolled) and `ax_ctrl` (z from leace, seed 0: nuisance-controlled), and generates with decade prototypes (src/98_era_axis_lora.py, 99b_era_axis_gen.py, run_ax.sh; GPUs 0-3).

## 2026-11-01 — M-2 first results: era-axis conditioned adapters (`src/98_era_axis_lora.py`, `src/99b_era_axis_gen.py`, `src/run_ax.sh`; `results/ax_{ctrl,free}_ge{7,10}_{eval,content}.json`, `results/six_decades_axis.{json,txt}`, `results/nuisance_steering_axis.{json,txt}`)
Config: MusicGen-Melody + LoRA r16 (3000 steps, bs 8, lr 1e-4, seed 0) with the text prompt always neutral and the era entering only through z (8-d, standardized on TRAIN) added as a bias to the projected text states (A: Linear(8, hidden), jointly trained, zero init); z dropped to 0 with p .1; chroma of the original mix as before. `ax_ctrl`: z from the LEACE-input-erased encoder (nuisance-controlled, leakage R2 ~ 0), `ax_free`: z from the unconstrained encoder (lam 0). Generation: three-branch guidance, gm 3, ge 7 or 10 on z (era branch z = decade prototype = mean z of TRAIN clips of the decade; neutral branch z = 0), 24 songs x 7 prompts x 3 seeds. Both ordinal-only z (CORAL head).
MERT Delta P(target) (1960s / 1970s / 1980s / 1990s / 2000s / 2010s) and expected decade E: ax_ctrl ge10 +.783 / +.257 / -.001 / -.031 / +.161 / +.447, E .29 .93 2.28 3.53 3.93 4.36; ax_ctrl ge7 +.711 / +.170 / +.007 / -.006 / +.152 / +.357, E .50 1.39 2.60 3.39 3.92 4.19; ax_free ge7 +.654 / +.069 / +.053 / +.058 / +.159 / +.347, E .48 1.49 2.82 3.43 3.86 4.33. MuQ ax_ctrl ge10 +.694 / +.246 / -.001 / -.049 / +.250 / +.398, E .58 1.31 2.33 3.33 3.82 4.27. Text-prompt LoRA + three-branch for reference (ge10, MERT): +.698 / +.595 / +.648 / +.256 / +.257 / +.424, E .26 1.15 2.25 3.31 4.05 4.49.
Content: song-ID top-1 .883 / .857 (ctrl ge7 / ge10), .877 (free ge7); chord agreement .356 / .351 / .359.
After erasing style and bandwidth directions from the probe (MERT, `nuisance_steering_axis.txt`): ax_ctrl ge10 1960s +.783 -> +.505, 2010s +.447 -> +.227, 1980s +.001 (unchanged); ax_ctrl ge7 +.711 -> +.433, +.357 -> +.201; ax_free ge7 +.654 -> +.421, +.347 -> +.164; text LoRA + three-branch ge10 +.698 -> +.489, +.424 -> +.249 (ge7 +.618 -> +.379, +.353 -> +.162). MuQ ax_ctrl ge10 +.694 -> +.445, +.398 -> +.213.
Observations: (1) the nuisance-controlled coordinate alone (no decade word) moves the generator along the decade axis: E increases monotonically with the prototype decade (0.29 ... 4.36 in MERT, ge10), the extremes shift as much as with the text prompt (1960s Delta +.783 vs +.698; 2010s +.447 vs +.424), and the shift survives nuisance erasure at the same size as the text-prompt system (1960s +.505 vs +.489; 2010s +.227 vs +.249). (2) Delta P(target) for the middle decades is about 0 (1980s -.001 to +.053, 1990s -.031 to +.058) even though E moves monotonically: z-prototypes of the middle decades lie close to the neutral z = 0 (the train mean), so the generator output is close to neutral; the middle-decade signature of the text-prompt system (1980s +.648) is not reproduced, with either z. (3) The unconstrained z (ax_free) does not recover the 1980s either. Interpretation: the 1-D ordinal readout does not encode decade identity for the middle decades; a six-way head on z is added next (variants `c`). Run-to-run variation of the adapter is not yet measured (seed replicates running).
Running (launched 15:10-15:13 JST): `ax_dose` (dose-response: interpolation alpha 0, .25, .5, .75, 1 between the 1960s and 2010s prototypes, ax_ctrl ge10, `SUF=_dose`), `ax_ctrl_s1`, `ax_ctrl_s2` (adapter and z training seeds 1, 2, ge10), `ax_ctrlc` and `ax_freec` (z with the six-way head: leacec / advc, ge10; val six-way accuracy of the head on z: advc .424, leacec .341).

## 2026-11-01 — Ordinal control score and robustness to nuisance erasure: era axis vs decade words (`src/102_ordinal_control.py` -> `results/ordinal_control.{json,txt}`; `src/94_nuisance_steering.py` with `rho_E` -> `results/nuisance_steering_axis.{json,txt}`)
Metric: Spearman rho_E between the prompted decade index (six decade prompts, neutral excluded; n = 24 songs x 6 prompts x 3 seeds clips) and the clip's expected decade E under the codec-matched probe; 95% song-bootstrap CI in `ordinal_control.txt`. "Erased" = probe retrained after LEACE removal of style + bandwidth directions (rank 19; random same-rank projection leaves rho_E unchanged to within .002).
rho_E (MERT / MuQ), unerased -> erased (both): text-decade LoRA + three-branch ge10 .965 / .948 -> .864 / .807 (ge7 .941 / .898 -> .795 / .721); era axis nuisance-controlled `ax_ctrl` ge10 .910 / .899 -> .744 / .798 (ge7 .884 / .875 -> .714 / .752); era axis unconstrained `ax_free` ge10 .918 / .903 -> .734 / .774 (ge7 .883 / .873 -> .682 / .739); unmodified MusicGen ge7 .462 / .220; SongEcho_base .316 / .145, SongEcho_large .265 / .181 (unerased).
Mean E per prompted decade (MERT, ge10): text LoRA .26 1.15 2.25 3.31 4.05 4.49; ax_ctrl .29 .93 2.28 3.53 3.93 4.36; ax_free .20 1.21 2.81 3.55 4.00 4.50 (neutral 3.05 / 3.04 / 2.95); range last-first 4.23 / 4.07 / 4.30.
Observations: (1) the era coordinate alone, with no decade word, gives an ordinal control score of .91 (MERT) / .90 (MuQ), far above the unmodified model (.46 / .22) and the SongEcho adapters (.27-.32 / .15-.18), but below the text-prompt system (.965 / .948). (2) After nuisance erasure the ranking is text-prompt (.864 / .807) > axis (.73-.74 / .77-.80); the nuisance-controlled z (`ax_ctrl`) is NOT more robust than the unconstrained z (`ax_free`): differences of .01 to .02, not distinguished (no intervals computed for the erased scores). (3) Axis Delta for the middle decades stays near 0 for both z types (1980s -.001 / +.024 at ge10), E for the 1980s prompt 2.28 (ctrl) vs 2.81 (free).
Interpretation: the first version of the representation does not yet show an advantage of nuisance control over decade words; the claim "nuisance-controlled axis steers as well as words" holds only for the extremes. The six-way-head variants (`ax_ctrlc`, `ax_freec`), prefix-token injection (N-3), bandwidth-randomized targets (N-1) and the label-derived baselines (N-2) are the planned tests; no claim of an advantage is made yet.

Dose-response (G5), z = (1 - a) z_1960s + a z_2010s, ge10, seed 0, 24 songs x 3 seeds: mean E (MERT) a=0 .29, .25 .65, .5 3.03, .75 4.12, 1 4.36; MuQ .58, .93, 2.88, 4.06, 4.27 (neutral E 3.04 / 2.93). G2 (bandwidth-prompt effect on E at least halved, decade effect after erasure not lower by more than .1): NOT met; the bw_lo effect is larger with augmented training, the erased decade effect is within .08 of v1. Interpretation: random bandwidth augmentation of the targets does not remove the bandwidth shortcut of the decade words for this adapter; N-1 is closed negative for both the text adapter and the axis adapter (`ax_ctrl_aug`: 1980s/1990s still no effect).
Note: the first `run_tri_pbw.sh` linked the v1 neutral clips (`gen_lora`) into every bandwidth-prompt set regardless of the adapter; the v1aug run therefore used v1 neutral clips as its neutral condition (neutral E 3.05 instead of the v1aug neutral 3.10); the script now takes `NS`. The comparison above uses bw_hi/bw_lo clips only, which come from the respective adapter.

## 2026-11-02 — N-3 prefix-token injection launched (`src/98_era_axis_lora.py --prefix P`, `src/99b_era_axis_gen.py` reads `lora/<name>/prefix.txt`, driver `src/run_ax_prefix.sh <gpu> <name> <z file> <P>`)
Design: the neutral prompt gets P T5 sentinel tokens appended ("a song recording<extra_id_0>..."); z enters through Linear(8 -> P x hidden), zero-initialized, added only to the encoder states at those P positions (the other text positions and the chroma stream are untouched), instead of a bias on every text position (M-2). Everything else as M-2 (3000 steps, bs 8, lr 1e-4, z dropout .1, three-branch gm 3, ge 10, 24 songs x 3 seeds). Runs: `ax_ctrlc_pfx4` (leacec z, P=4, GPU 0), `ax_freec_pfx4` (advc z, P=4, GPU 1), `ax_ctrlc_pfx16` (P=16, GPU 2). Question: does localizing z in dedicated tokens improve middle-decade control, the erased-probe robustness or song identity (top-1) relative to the bias injection (ctrlc: 1980s .484, rho_E .957, erased 1980s .414). Results pending.

## 2026-11-02 — N-5a best-of-N with a nuisance-controlled scorer launched (inference-time; scripts `src/run_bon_gen.sh`, `src/run_bon_eval.sh`, `src/103_best_of_n.py`)
System: ctrlc era-axis adapter (bias injection, ge 10, gm 3). Pool: generation seeds 0-7 per (song, decade prompt, plus neutral) for the 24 validation songs (seeds 3-7 added by `run_bon_gen.sh`, GPUs 0-2, 06:30-09:30 JST). Selector: MERT codec-probe after LEACE erasure of style and bandwidth statistics (score = P(target decade)); evaluation on the kept clips with held-out probes (MuQ unerased and erased), selection-biased selector probe and unerased MERT reported separately, plus song-identity top-1 and chord agreement of the kept clips. N = 1 (random choice) / 2 / 4 / 8. Question: does selection with a nuisance-controlled scorer raise the held-out era effect, especially the 1980s/1990s Delta, without losing the melody? This is the best-of-N baseline that the sequential Monte Carlo variant (N-5b) must beat. Results pending.

## 2026-11-02 — N-5a best-of-N results (`results/best_of_n_ax_ctrlc_bon.{json,txt}`, `results/ax_ctrlc_bon_{eval,content}.json`; pool = 8 generation seeds, ctrlc ge10, 24 songs; selector = MERT erased probe)
Held-out MuQ probe Delta of the kept clips, N = 1 (random) / 2 / 4 / 8, 95% song-bootstrap CI in brackets for N=1 and N=8:
- MuQ unerased: 1960s .703 / .757 / .783 / .775; 1980s .365 [.312,.421] / .382 / .470 / .478 [.377,.574]; 1990s .307 / .302 / .337 / .392 [.321,.465]; 2010s .456 / .431 / .496 / .569 [.490,.640].
- MuQ erased ("both", held-out encoder and nuisance-controlled): 1980s .310 -> .467 [.373,.559]; 1990s .289 -> .378; 2010s .243 -> .319; 1960s .463 -> .565.
- Selector probe (MERT erased; selection-biased): 1980s .440 -> .766, 1990s .221 -> .550. MERT unerased 1980s .526 -> .745.

Interpretation: selection with the nuisance-controlled MERT scorer raises the held-out MuQ effect, also after erasure, by about .1-.15 at N = 8; the melody metrics do not change (top-1 dips to .812 at N = 4 without a monotone trend). Gains are largest in the selector's own probe (selection bias), smaller in the held-out encoder. Cost: 8x generation. Baseline for N-5b (sequential Monte Carlo); selection and evaluation use the same 24 songs, no separate selection set.

## 2026-11-02 — N-5b probe-guided SMC launched (`src/104_smc_gen.py`, driver `src/run_smc.sh <gpu> <beta> [N]`)
Method: N = 8 particles per (song, decade, seed) with the ctrlc era-axis system (ge 10, gm 3). Every 100 decoded frames (2 s) the partially decoded accompaniment of each particle (EnCodec decode, 32 -> 24 kHz, -23 LUFS) is scored with the nuisance-controlled MERT probe (LEACE-erased style + bandwidth, fitted on TRAIN; phi = log P(target decade)); log-weights accumulate beta x (phi_j - phi_{j-1}); multinomial resampling when ESS < N/2 permutes the token histories and the self-attention KV cache rows together. Frames from 1300 to 1500 are not scored; the returned clip is particle 0 of the final population. Smoke runs (24 songs list, first clip): beta 5, N = 4 collapsed to ESS ~1 at the first stages (partial audio of 2-4 s gives noisy scores), beta 1, N = 8 kept ESS 3.3 -> 6.4. Runs: beta 1 (GPU 0), 2 (GPU 1), 4 (GPU 2); 24 songs x 6 decades x 3 seeds each (432 clips, about 3 h); neutral reference clips copied from gen_ax_ctrlc_ge10. Compared with best-of-8 (N-5a) at equal particle count (8 candidates per clip) and with the N=1 system. Results pending.

Setting as the launch entry (N = 8, seg 100 frames, 24 songs x 6 decades x 3 seeds; neutral reference = the ctrlc neutral clips).
- MERT Delta 1960s/1970s/1980s/1990s/2000s/2010s: +.768 / +.656 / +.644 / +.514 / +.335 / +.631 (ctrlc N=1: +.741 / +.591 / +.484 / +.389 / +.225 / +.517); rho_E .977 (N=1 .957). MuQ (held-out encoder): +.731 / +.661 / +.407 / +.374 / +.368 / +.545 (N=1: +.709 / +.574 / +.336 / +.285 / +.329 / +.434); rho_E .958 (.925).

- Comparison with best-of-8 (N-5a, `best_of_n_ax_ctrlc_bon.json`, held-out MuQ unerased 1980s/1990s/2010s): SMC beta 4 .407 / .374 / .545 vs best-of-8 .478 / .392 / .569 vs N=1 .365 (bon pool average) / .307 / .456. Erased MuQ 1980s/2010s: SMC .358 / .314 vs best-of-8 .467 / .319. Both use 8 candidates per clip; the SMC clips come from separate generation seeds 0-2, the best-of-8 pool from seeds 0-7 (not a paired comparison).
Observation: SMC with beta 4 improves on the plain system in every decade and raises the ordinal control, without a loss of melody metrics, but is below best-of-8 on the held-out probe at equal candidate count. Interpretation (not tested): the partial-audio scores of short prefixes are noisy, so early resampling selects on noise; best-of-N scores the finished clip.

## 2026-11-03 — M-3 market encoder on accompaniment (dataset B; `src/105_market_encoder.py`, `results/market_encoder_{mert,muq}_{leace,leacel}.{json,txt}`)
Question: is there a nuisance-controlled market coordinate z in the accompaniment, as the era coordinate of M-1 (needed for a second control axis of the cover generator)? Setting: MERT layer 14 / MuQ layer 12 mean-pooled codec-resynthesized accompaniment (layers from `probe_codec_{enc}_B.json`), standardized on TRAIN (798 labeled clips); LEACE (fit on TRAIN) erases N = 10 style-tag PCs + 9 accompaniment statistics (`leace`) or additionally the accompaniment language-ID posterior (`leacel`, `NUIS=lang`); encoder Linear-GELU-Dropout-Linear to z (8-d) with a six-way softmax head, 300 epochs, 3 seeds; validation n = 102, chance .167. Commands: `python src/105_market_encoder.py {mert,muq}` and `NUIS=lang python src/105_market_encoder.py {mert,muq}`.
- Unerased codec-accompaniment probe (reference, `probe_codec_*_B.json`): validation accuracy MERT .333, MuQ .284.
- After erasure of style and statistics: head accuracy MERT .245 ± .016, MuQ .275 ± .014; logistic on the erased input .235 / .255. With language also erased: MERT .222 ± .026, MuQ .248 ± .036 (logistic on erased input .206 / .235). Nuisance R2 from z about 0 (ridge .009 / -.001).
Observation: market information in the regenerated-part signal (accompaniment) is weak (.33 / .28 unerased) and falls to .22-.28 after erasure, 5 to 11 points above chance with n = 102 (a 95% binomial interval is about +-.09). A market axis built on dataset B alone carries little accompaniment-borne signal; the market cue found earlier lives in the vocals and the language.

## 2026-11-03 — T-1 metadata-only candidate pairs for the two-axis study (`src/106_discogs_vi_pairs.py`, `results/discogs_vi_pairs.{json,txt}`)
Data: Discogs-VI 2024-07-01 metadata (Zenodo 13983028, `main.zip`, CC BY-NC-SA 4.0), no audio; downloaded to the server (`~/era_market/data/dvi/main.zip`, 1.4 GB). Command: `unzip -p data/dvi/main.zip main/Discogs-VI-20240701.jsonl | python src/106_discogs_vi_pairs.py` (first track of each version; eligible = valid year 1900-2024 and known country). Pair = two eligible versions of one clique with different release_id. The format lists contain no edition descriptors (vocabulary: Vinyl, CD, Shellac, Cassette, File, ...), so reissues cannot be removed by label; "strict" = track-artist id sets differ (another artist's performance), "any" includes same-artist pairs (re-release, re-recording, other edition: not separable without audio). Era move = year gap >= 20 and different decade; market move = different release country; "six" = both countries among US, UK, Brazil, Spain, Germany, Italy; "video" = both versions list a YouTube url in the release metadata (not the matched official uploads of Discogs-VI-YT). Pair counts use at most 60 eligible versions per clique (fixed seed); clique counts use all.
- 1,911,611 versions, 348,796 cliques; 1,643,410 eligible versions; 299,233 cliques with >= 2 eligible versions.
- Strict pairs (different artist): cliques / pairs: all 76,131 / 1,568,838; era move 41,603 / 654,124; market move 56,530 / 1,209,438; era + market 33,578 / 509,325; era + market within the six markets 17,081 / 148,215; era move + US-Spain 3,172 / 9,160. With video links on both versions: all 31,300 / 325,064; era + market 14,042 / 100,296; era + market six 7,482 / 32,038; era + US-Spain 1,343 / 1,883.
- Any pairs (same-artist allowed): all 297,846 / 11,002,752; era + market 96,965 / 3,295,568; era + market six 47,070 / 950,196; era + US-Spain 8,804 / 71,234.
Observation: metadata alone yields tens of thousands of cliques with a different-artist pair that differs by >= 20 years and in release country, and thousands with an era move between US and Spain releases; the limit for the two-axis study is audio access, not pair count. The market label is an edition property (earlier entry on release country vs artist), so pairs are candidates, not verified distinct recordings.

## 2026-11-03 — N-5 paired comparison: best-of-8 versus probe-guided SMC (`src/107_paired_bon_smc.py`, `results/paired_bon_smc.{json,txt}`)
Same 24 songs; pool = `ax_ctrlc_bon32` (seeds 0-31; best-of-N uses the first N seeds), SMC = `smc_ctrlc_N8_b{2,4}[_f500]` (8 particles, seeds 0-2), N = 1 = mean of seeds 0-2; selector = MERT probe after LEACE erasure of style and bandwidth; value = P(target decade) under the probe minus the song's neutral mean. Paired per-song difference best-of-8 minus SMC beta 4, song-bootstrap 95% CI, exact Wilcoxon p over 24 songs (18 comparisons, no multiplicity correction). Command: `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES= python src/107_paired_bon_smc.py`. No selection/evaluation song split was made: selector and probes are fitted on dataset-A TRAIN only and nothing is tuned on the 24 songs.
Held-out MuQ unerased (N=1 / SMC b4 / best-of-8 / best-of-16 / best-of-32; best-of-8 minus SMC b4 [CI], p, songs with best-of-8 higher):
- 1980s: .336 / .407 / .471 / .565 / .542; +.064 [-.034,+.163], p .218, 16/24. 1990s: .285 / .374 / .390 / .392 / .416; +.016 [-.077,+.109], p .833. 2010s: .434 / .545 / .573 / .622 / .597; +.027 [-.044,+.101], p .345. 1960s +.040 [-.014,+.095]; 1970s +.034 [-.054,+.118]; 2000s +.048 [-.033,+.126] (all p >= .197).
- MuQ erased: 1980s .273 / .358 / .467 / .542 / .526; +.109 [+.011,+.201], p .021, 17/24 (the only held-out MuQ cell with an interval above 0); other decades +.011 to +.081 with intervals containing 0, 1970s -.009.
- MERT unerased (selector family, optimistic): best-of-8 minus SMC b4 +.143 [.075,.216] (1970s), +.101 [.040,.169] (1980s), +.108 [.041,.171] (1990s), +.078 [.009,.140] (2000s), +.055 [-.004,.117] (2010s), -.007 (1960s).
Observation: on the held-out encoder (MuQ, unerased and erased, 12 cells) best-of-8 is above SMC beta 4 in 11 cells, and only one cell (erased 1980s) has an interval above 0; on the selector's own family (MERT unerased) five of six decades are above SMC with intervals excluding 0 in four. Both methods exceed N = 1 in every cell. At 24 songs SMC with 8 particles is not shown to differ from best-of-8 on the held-out probe; best-of-16 and best-of-32 are higher still in the 1960s-1980s and 2010s MuQ cells (N = 16 and 32 use 2x and 4x the candidates).

## 2026-11-03 — T-4 market axis, vocal route: pre-analysis from existing results and tool check
Existing measurements (dataset B, market, validation n = 102, chance .167; WORKLOG 2026-10-27 LEACE entries): market accuracy on vocals .608 falls to .392 (Whisper large-v3 language groups erased by LEACE; random same-rank erasure .606), mix .588 -> .382, accompaniment .422 -> .314. Accompaniment-only encoder after erasing style, statistics and language (M-3, 2026-11-03): .22-.25. Reading (interpretation): about half of the vocal market signal above chance is language; the residual (.39 vs .167, 95% binomial interval about +-.09) may be timbre, delivery or production and is the part a timbre-only conversion could change; moving the language part needs new lyrics (translation plus singing voice synthesis), which the slides place under SVS (lecture 7/9: SVC models timbre, SVS needs text and MIDI).
Tool check: FreeSVC (zero-shot multilingual SVC, ICASSP 2025; arXiv 2501.05586; code github.com/freds0/free-svc, repository LICENSE file MIT, Hugging Face model card alefiury/free-svc states CC BY-NC-SA 4.0 in its current version: to be reconciled before use); requirements pin torch 2.1.2, numpy 1.24 and webrtcvad/pyreaper builds, so a separate environment is needed. The source was cloned to `~/era_market/ext/free-svc` (not installed, no weights downloaded: the install was not run in this session).

## 2026-11-03 — T-4a timbre-only market test with FreeSVC (`src/108_svc_market.py`, `src/108_svc_config.yaml`, `src/109_svc_market_eval.py`, `results/svc_market_eval{,_self}.{json,txt}`)
Question: does converting the singer's timbre to a target market's voice move the vocal market probe toward that market? Setting: FreeSVC (Ferreira et al., ICASSP 2025; github.com/freds0/free-svc; weights `alefiury/free-svc`, model card CC BY-NC-SA 4.0, repository LICENSE MIT; own environment `venv_svc`, Python 3.10, torch 2.1.2; config `src/108_svc_config.yaml` = released config with ECAPA2 speaker encoder, SPIN content encoder and language embedding, which the released checkpoint requires). 102 dataset-B validation vocal stems (HTDemucs) x 6 market voices (voice = mean ECAPA2 embedding of the vocal stems of the first 10 train clips of the market, sorted by sample id); source language = Whisper large-v3 language group of the clip mapped to the FreeSVC language ids; 612 conversions, 0 failures (first run crashed on one clip because the pitch tracker returned a 2-D array; fixed by flattening). Probe: StandardScaler + logistic regression (C .01) fit on dataset-B TRAIN vocal embeddings (MERT layer 16, MuQ layer 2; validation accuracy on the original vocals reproduces .608 / .471). Commands: `cd ~/era_market/ext/free-svc; ~/era_market/venv_svc/bin/python ~/era_market/src/108_svc_market.py --outdir ~/era_market/svc_B` (control: `--selfvoice --outdir ~/era_market/svc_B_self`), then `python src/109_svc_market_eval.py` (control: `SELF=1`).
- Converted to the six voices (mean over voices): accuracy of the ORIGINAL market label MERT .446 (original vocals .608), MuQ .203 (.471); P(original label) change -.116 / -.124.
- Accuracy of the TARGET voice market: MERT .163, MuQ .175 (chance .167). Per target market, Delta = P(m | converted to m) - P(m | converted to the other five voices): MERT -.007 to +.001 (US -.007 [-.013,-.001], UK -.004, Brazil -.008, Spain +.001, Germany -.011, Italy -.010); MuQ UK +.014 [+.010,+.018], Brazil +.004 [+.001,+.007], Spain +.005, Germany +.004, US -.007, Italy -.009 [-.016,-.001].
- Self-voice control (each clip converted with its own speaker embedding, no timbre change): original-label accuracy MERT .451, MuQ .196; P(original label) change -.112 / -.122.
Observation: the accuracy loss on the original label (.608 to .446, .471 to .203) equals the loss of the self-voice control (.451, .196), so it comes from the conversion pipeline itself (domain shift of the converted vocal), not from the timbre swap; converting to a market voice does not move the probe toward that market (target accuracy at chance, per-market effects within about +-.015). Interpretation (not tested): the mean ECAPA2 speaker embedding of ten clips encodes speaker identity, not market; the market cue in vocals is mostly language and delivery, which FreeSVC keeps from the source. A vocal-side market move needs the language route (T-4b: new lyrics) or a market-specific vocal model.

## 2026-11-03/04 — T-4b language route pilot: translated lyrics + ACE-Step 1.5 cover mode (`src/110_lyrics_translate.py`, `src/111_ace_lyric_route.py`, `src/112_lyric_route_eval.py`, `results/lyric_route_eval.{json,txt}`)
Question: can the vocal-side market cue (language) be moved while the song is kept? Setting: 12 dataset-B validation clips of market US or UK whose Whisper large-v3 language group is English (first 12 by sample id); Whisper large-v3 transcript of the vocal stem; translation en -> es, pt, de, it with M2M-100 1.2B (facebook/m2m100_1.2B, MIT; the checkpoint is a pickle .bin and torch 2.5.1 is below the version transformers requires for its own loader, so the state dict was loaded with torch.load(weights_only=True) into the model built from the config; sentencepiece installed in the main venv); ACE-Step 1.5 turbo (8 steps) cover mode, src_audio = the original 30 s mix, caption "a pop song sung in <language>", lyrics = text, vocal_language = code, audio_cover_strength 0.5, seed 0; conditions orig (English transcript, control for the pipeline), es, pt, de, it = 60 clips. Commands: `python src/110_lyrics_translate.py --n 12`; `cd ext/ACE-Step-1.5; ~/era_market/venv_ace/bin/python ~/era_market/src/111_ace_lyric_route.py --strength 0.5 --outdir ace_lyric --seeds 0`; `python src/112_lyric_route_eval.py`. The transcripts and translations are not committed (lyrics of commercial songs; `results/lyrics_route_text.json` is git-ignored) and the ASR text was removed from the committed result file.
Read-outs (Whisper large-v3 on the generated mix; VINet cosine to the original mix; n = 12 per row):
- orig (control): P(English) .685, language top-1 hit .917, similarity of the ASR text to the intended lyrics .493, VINet cosine .496, VINet top-1 among 12 originals 0.00. Original mixes: P(en) .761.
- es: P(es) .505, top-1 hit .583, lyric similarity .362, VINet cosine .478, top-1 .17. pt: .509, .500, .374, .483, .00. de: .351, .333, .296, .448, .00. it: .360, .500, .281, .435, .00.
Observation: the sung language moves toward the intended language (top-1 hit .33-.58 against 1/99 chance; the English control .92), strongest for Spanish and Portuguese, weakest for German; intelligibility falls with the language (lyric similarity .49 control, .28-.37 translated); song identity is largely lost already in the control (cosine .50, top-1 0 of 12), and the translated versions are .02-.06 lower in cosine than the control. Interpretation (not tested): cover strength 0.5 lets the model rewrite the melody; a stronger source constraint or a vocal-only regeneration should keep the song. Sweep of the cover strength 0.8 and 0.95 started (same read-outs).

Language top-1 hit (es / pt / de / it; English control): strength 0.5: .58 / .50 / .33 / .50 (.92); 0.8: .50 / .50 / .67 / .42 (.92); 0.95: .58 / .58 / .75 / .50 (1.00). P(intended language): 0.5: .505 / .509 / .351 / .360; 0.8: .466 / .463 / .548 / .423; 0.95: .489 / .577 / .675 / .427.
Lyric similarity to the intended text (es / pt / de / it; control): 0.5: .362 / .374 / .296 / .281 (.493); 0.8: .506 / .334 / .472 / .441 (.494); 0.95: .379 / .410 / .418 / .484 (.621).
VINet cosine to the original mix (translated conditions; control): 0.5: .435-.483 (.496); 0.8: .485-.521 (.504); 0.95: .487-.522 (.506); VINet top-1 among the 12 originals: 0.5: 0-.17; 0.8: 0-.17; 0.95: 0-.33 (control .08).
Observation: raising the cover strength from 0.5 to 0.95 raises the cosine by about .03-.07 and the best-case language hit to .75 (German), but the song-identity top-1 stays at or below .33 and the control (same lyrics, same pipeline) has cosine .50 at every strength: the pipeline keeps only part of the song, and the strength parameter does not close the gap. n = 12 clips and one seed per cell, so differences of a few points between cells are not resolved. ## 2026-11-04 — T-9 era-specific vocal processing chains (`src/118_vocal_era_dsp.py`, `results/vocal_era_dsp.{json,txt}`)
Question (BLOCKERS B6): the era-axis system regenerates the accompaniment only; can a decade-specific processing chain on the ORIGINAL vocal move the era readings further? Setting: 24 evaluation songs (dataset A validation, `gen/picked.txt`); vocal stem (HTDemucs) processed with hand-parametrized chains (numpy/scipy; parameters fixed before the run and not tuned): 1960s = band-pass 120 Hz-7 kHz, tanh saturation (drive 2), compression (-24 dB, 3:1), plate-like reverb (RT60 .5 s, wet .12); 1980s = high-shelf brightening (+.6 x high-passed 4 kHz), chorus (4 ms depth, wet .25), gated reverb (RT60 1 s cut at .35 s, wet .25), compression (-26 dB, 3:1); 2010s = 90 Hz high-pass, compression (-30 dB, 6:1), soft limiting, brightening, short reverb (RT60 .25 s, wet .08). Probes: MERT, dataset-A TRAIN-fit logistic regression (C .01): vocal probe layer 20 (validation accuracy .394, reproduced), mix probe layer 12 (validation accuracy .477, reproduced). Commands: `python src/118_vocal_era_dsp.py` (vocal stage) and `MIX=1 python src/118_vocal_era_dsp.py` (adds the mix stage: era-axis accompaniment of `gen_ax_ctrlc_ge10`, seeds 0-2, remixed with dry or era-processed vocal, both -23 LUFS).
- Vocal probe, P(decade e) with chain e minus dry vocal (95% song-bootstrap CI): 1960s chain -.012 [-.031,+.006]; 1980s chain +.186 [+.127,+.245]; 2010s chain +.169 [+.095,+.244]. Off-diagonal: 1960s chain raises P(2010s) +.199 [+.115,+.280] and lowers P(1980s) -.082; 1980s chain lowers P(1960s) -.042 and P(2010s) -.043; 2010s chain lowers P(1960s) -.021 and P(1980s) -.064.
- Mix-level probe on the era-axis remix, P(e) with era-processed vocal minus dry vocal (n = 72 clips per decade): 1960s .228 -> .191 (-.037 [-.051,-.024]); 1980s .355 -> .423 (+.068 [+.049,+.088]); 2010s .594 -> .637 (+.043 [+.027,+.060]).
Observation: the 1980s and 2010s chains raise the intended decade on the vocal probe (+.19, +.17) and on the mix probe (+.07, +.04) on top of the era-axis accompaniment, and the vocal probe is chain-specific for these two; the 1960s chain does not raise the 1960s reading (vocal -.012, mix -.037) and the vocal probe reads the band-limited, saturated vocal as 2010s. Interpretation (not tested): after loudness normalization, the band-limited saturated signal resembles the compressed modern vocal in the probe's feature space, or the hand-set 1960s parameters are not the cues the probe learned. Next: tune chain parameters on dataset-A TRAIN vocals only (T-9c), keep the 24 evaluation songs untouched.

## 2026-11-04 — T-4g vocal-only regeneration with ACE-Step 1.5 base model, lego mode (`src/116_ace_lego_vocal.py`, `src/117_lego_remix.py`, `results/lyric_route_eval_ace_lego_mix.{json,txt}`)
Question (BLOCKERS B2): keep the song by keeping the original accompaniment and regenerate only the vocal in the target language. Setting: same 12 pilot clips and translations as T-4b; ACE-Step 1.5 base model (`acestep-v15-base`, `ACE-Step/acestep-v15-base`, MIT per the model card API; weights downloaded to `ext/ACE-Step-1.5/checkpoints/acestep-v15-base`), task_type lego, src_audio = original accompaniment stem, instruction "Generate the vocals track based on the audio context:", caption "a pop song sung in <language>", lyrics = text, vocal_language = code, 50 steps, guidance 7, seed 0; the generated vocal track (RMS-matched to the original vocal stem) is added to the original accompaniment (`117_lego_remix.py`); read-outs as T-4b on the remix. Commands: `python ~/era_market/src/116_ace_lego_vocal.py --outdir ace_lego --seeds 0` (from ext/ACE-Step-1.5, venv_ace), `python src/117_lego_remix.py`, `GEN=ace_lego_mix python src/112_lyric_route_eval.py`.
Results (n = 12 per row; es / pt / de / it; English control): language top-1 hit .17 / .25 / .33 / .17 (.75); P(intended language) .150 / .241 / .286 / .141 (.585); lyric similarity to the intended text .163 / .187 / .223 / .242 (.310); VINet cosine to the original mix .826 / .812 / .827 / .813 (.804); VINet top-1 among 12 originals .92 / .92 / .83 / .83 (.83).
Comparison with the cover route (T-4b, strength 0.95, es / pt / de / it): language hit .58 / .58 / .75 / .50, VINet cosine .500 / .522 / .501 / .487, top-1 .00 / .25 / .33 / .08.
Observation: keeping the accompaniment restores the song identity (cosine .81-.83, top-1 .83-.92, essentially that of the original accompaniment plus a vocal) but the sung language is realized much less often (hit .17-.33 against .50-.75 for the cover route; the English control itself drops from 1.00 to .75), so the two routes trade language realization against song identity. Interpretation (not tested): the lego vocal is quiet before normalization (peak .015 in the smoke test) and sits under the original accompaniment; the language read-out on the mix is lower than on the isolated track. A guidance 10 run and a read-out of the isolated generated track are running (`ace_lego_cfg10`, `GEN=ace_lego`).

## 2026-11-04 — T-4g follow-up: guidance 10 and the isolated generated vocal track (`results/lyric_route_eval_ace_lego_cfg10_mix.*`, `results/lyric_route_eval_ace_lego.*`)
Commands: `python ~/era_market/src/116_ace_lego_vocal.py --outdir ace_lego_cfg10 --cfg 10`, `LEGO=ace_lego_cfg10 python src/117_lego_remix.py`, `GEN=ace_lego_cfg10_mix python src/112_lyric_route_eval.py`; isolated track: `GEN=ace_lego python src/112_lyric_route_eval.py` (the VINet columns of that file are not meaningful: the track is the vocal only).
- Guidance 10 on the remix (es / pt / de / it; English control): language top-1 hit .33 / .25 / .33 / .33 (.75); P(intended language) .228 / .240 / .285 / .290 (.495); lyric similarity .202 / .246 / .193 / .283 (.296); VINet cosine .774 / .838 / .817 / .788 (.812), top-1 .92 / .92 / .92 / .83 (.92).
- Isolated generated vocal track (guidance 7): language top-1 hit .25 / .25 / .33 / .17 (English control 1.00); P(intended language) .207 / .244 / .293 / .141 (.653); lyric similarity .145 / .200 / .240 / .191 (.287).
Observation: the language is realized at .17-.33 also in the isolated track (English control 1.00), so the weak language reading in the lego remix is not caused by the accompaniment masking the vocal; guidance 10 changes the hit rate by +.08 / 0 / 0 / +.17 on 12 clips (not resolved). The lego mode of the base model sings the translated lyrics in the target language less reliably than the cover mode (T-4b, hit .42-.75) while keeping the song. Interpretation (not tested): the base model's lyric conditioning for non-English text is weaker than the turbo cover path, or the generated track follows the accompaniment context's language-less content. Next: combine the two routes (T-4i: the cover-route vocal over the original accompaniment).

## 2026-11-04 — T-4i/T-4j stem swap: cover-route vocal over the original accompaniment, and the market read-out (`src/119_stem_swap_route.py`, `src/120_swap_market_probe.py`, `results/lyric_route_eval_ace_swap.*`, `results/swap_market_probe.{json,txt}`)
Question: can the language carrier (cover route, T-4b) and the song carrier (original accompaniment) be combined, and does the combination move the vocal market probe? Setting: the 60 cover-route outputs of strength 0.95 (`ace_lyric_s095`) are separated with HT Demucs; the vocal stem (RMS-matched to the original vocal stem) is added to the original accompaniment stem of the clip (`ace_swap/`), the separated vocal alone is kept in `ace_swap_voc/`. Commands: `python src/119_stem_swap_route.py` (venv, GPU), `GEN=ace_swap python src/112_lyric_route_eval.py`, `python src/120_swap_market_probe.py`. Probe: dataset-B vocal market probe (MERT layer 16, C .01, validation accuracy .608 reproduced) on the loudness-normalized separated vocal.
- Mix-level read-outs of `ace_swap` (es / pt / de / it; English control): language top-1 hit .50 / .58 / .50 / .25 (.83); P(intended language) .408 / .506 / .428 / .256 (.620); lyric similarity .348 / .368 / .363 / .443 (.496); VINet cosine .813 / .825 / .807 / .837 (.803), top-1 .83 / .92 / .92 / .92 (.92). Song identity is that of the original accompaniment while the language hit is at the level of the cover route (T-4b, strength 0.95: .58 / .58 / .75 / .50).
- Vocal market probe on the separated generated vocal, 12 clips of source market US or UK: P(target market) es -> Spain .238, pt -> Brazil .577, de -> Germany .451, it -> Italy .570; P(target) under the English control (same pipeline) .046 / .049 / .186 / .244; original vocals .068 / .008 / .179 / .105. Difference to the control (95% song-bootstrap CI): Spain +.192 [+.055,+.349], Brazil +.528 [+.299,+.738], Germany +.265 [+.116,+.433], Italy +.326 [+.119,+.550]. Target top-1: .25 / .58 / .50 / .83 (control .00 / .00 / .17 / .33).
Observation: this is the first market-axis move in the project: translating the lyrics and singing them in the target language moves the market reading of the vocal toward the target market in all four markets with intervals above 0, while the song stays the original accompaniment. Caveats: 12 clips, one seed, US/UK source clips only; the vocal probe is known to read language (LEACE language erasure drops vocal market accuracy from .608 to .392, WORKLOG 2026-10-27), so the result shows that the language cue moved, which is the cue the market label rides on, and does not show that arrangement or vocal style moved; the lyric text comes from an ASR transcript of 30 s excerpts. Next steps in `TODO.md` (T-4k scale-up to all validation clips and all target languages, seeds, T-10 composition with the era axis).

- pt -> Brazil (n 64): .505 vs .083; +.421 [+.332,+.510]; .59 vs .03.
