Mix-level read-outs of the swapped clips (n; language top-1 hit; VINet cosine / top-1 among 77): control orig 77; .844; .815 / .92; en 34; .882; .834 / .94; es 63; .635; .804 / .89; pt 64; .672; .817 / .92; de 76; .763; .816 / .95; it 72; .625; .808 / .92. Original mixes: P(English) .461.
Observation: the pilot result (12 clips, US/UK source) replicates on 77 clips of all source languages with narrower intervals: translating the lyrics and singing them in the target language moves the vocal market probe toward the target market for all five targets (intervals above 0; the increase is .19 for Spain, .31-.42 for the others), the song identity stays at the original accompaniment's level (cosine .80-.83, top-1 .89-.95), and the language is realized in .63-.88 of the clips. Caveats as before: the probe reads language; one seed; the swap uses the generated vocal over the original accompaniment, so arrangement cues of the target market are not moved; ASR transcripts of 30 s excerpts. Not yet separated: how much of the effect remains when the language information is erased from the probe (T-4l).

## 2026-11-04 — T-4l the lyric-route market move after language erasure (`src/120_swap_market_probe.py` with `LEACE=1`, `results/scale_market_probe_leace.{json,txt}`)
Question: does anything beyond language move? Setting: same 77 clips, 386 generated vocals as T-4k; the vocal market probe is refit after LEACE erasure of the Whisper large-v3 language group (en, pt, es, it, de, other) from the dataset-B TRAIN vocal embeddings (validation accuracy of the erased probe .402; plain probe .608; earlier erased-probe figure .392 with iterative erasure). Command: `TEXT=lyrics_scale_text.json VOCDIR=ace_scale_swap_voc OUTNAME=scale_market_probe LEACE=1 python src/120_swap_market_probe.py`.
Delta P(target) vs the source-language control (95% CI), erased probe: Spain +.023 [+.001,+.044] (plain +.188); Brazil -.007 [-.049,+.031] (plain +.421); Germany -.012 [-.046,+.024] (plain +.314); Italy +.006 [-.014,+.028] (plain +.408); US+UK +.032 [-.040,+.107] (plain +.325). Target top-1 (erased probe) .02 / .25 / .11 / .04 / .56 against the control's .02 / .23 / .13 / .08 / .44.
Observation: after the language information is erased from the probe the market effect of the lyric route disappears (all intervals contain 0 except Spain at +.023, which is within .05 of 0): the move measured in T-4k is carried by the language cue. The lyric route therefore delivers the language component of the market, not the arrangement, delivery or production components. Interpretation: this is the expected limit of replacing the lyrics only; markets are then differentiated by the remaining cues (instrumentation, style, vocal delivery), for which the accompaniment-side routes (T-4e metadata style descriptors, reference-audio style transfer T-4m) are the candidates.

## 2026-11-04 — T-4m reference-audio style transfer for the market axis (`src/122_ace_reference_market.py`, `src/122_mref.sh`, `results/ace_mref_eval.{json,txt}`, `results/ace_mref08_eval.{json,txt}`)
Question: can a real accompaniment of the target market, given to ACE-Step as reference audio, move the accompaniment-side market reading (the arrangement part that the lyric route leaves untouched, T-4l)? Setting: 24 dataset-B validation clips (4 per source market); ACE-Step 1.5 turbo, cover mode, instrumental, source = original accompaniment stem, caption "a song recording", reference_audio = the accompaniment stem of one of the first three TRAIN clips of the target market (rotated over clips); condition `neutral` = same call without reference (pipeline control); cover strength 0.5 and 0.8; seed 0; read-out `src/37_eval_market.py` (codec-matched MERT and MuQ market probes trained on dataset-B TRAIN accompaniment; Delta = P(market X | reference of X) - P(X | neutral), Wilcoxon over 24 songs, no multiplicity correction). Commands: `bash 122_mref.sh 1` (generation and `GENDIR=<dir> python src/37_eval_market.py --out results/<dir>_eval.json --seeds 0`).
Delta P(target) per market, strength 0.5 (MERT / MuQ): US -.006 / .000; UK -.019 / -.079; Brazil +.105 (p .0096) / +.075 (p .0059); Spain +.021 / +.192 (p .0079); Germany -.056 / -.040 (p .0105, negative); Italy -.001 / -.021. Strength 0.8: US -.018 / -.047; UK +.030 / -.074; Brazil +.038 / -.025; Spain -.008 / +.181 (p .0028); Germany -.030 / -.019; Italy +.043 / +.061 (p .0457).
For comparison the hand-written text prompts of 2026-10-26 (MERT / MuQ): US +.035 / +.128; UK +.038 / +.009; Brazil -.012 / +.279; Spain -.053 / -.029; Germany +.089; Italy +.082 (market_summary.txt).
Observation: reference-audio transfer does not move the accompaniment market reading consistently: the only cells that are positive for both encoders are Brazil at strength 0.5 (+.105 / +.075) and Spain at both strengths on MuQ only (MERT +.021 / -.008); Germany is negative; the control pipeline (neutral) already differs by encoder. 24 songs, one seed, 18 uncorrected tests per strength. Interpretation (not tested): the accompaniment alone carries little market information (M-3: accuracy .22-.33 after erasure) so no route that changes only the accompaniment can be expected to move it much; what a reference can add (timbre, rhythm) is not what the probes use.

## 2026-11-04 — T-10 two-axis composition: era-axis accompaniment x lyric-route language (`src/123_two_axis.py`, `src/123_two_axis.sh`, `results/two_axis.{json,txt}`)
Question: are the two axes independent when composed? Setting: the 24 evaluation songs of dataset A; transcripts and translations as T-4k (`DS=A python src/121_lyric_scale_text.py`; 6 songs were dropped for a transcript shorter than 20 letters and 2 more by the unsupported-source check, 16 kept: 13 English-sung and 3 Spanish-sung, so Spanish is a target for 13 songs only); lyric route as T-4i (ACE-Step cover mode strength 0.95 on the original mix, separated vocal RMS-matched to the original vocal: languages orig, es, pt, de, it); accompaniment from the era-axis adapter (`gen_ax_ctrlc_ge10`, seed 0) for the decades 1960s, 1980s, 2010s and neutral; remix = generated vocal + era-axis accompaniment. Read-outs: mix-level era probe (MERT layer 12, validation accuracy .477), Whisper large-v3 language reading of the remix. Commands: `bash 123_two_axis.sh 1` (text, ACE shards, `DS=A SRC=ace_a DST=ace_a_swap python src/119_stem_swap_route.py`, `python src/123_two_axis.py`).
Era effect = P(decade e) of the mix with era-axis accompaniment minus with the neutral accompaniment, source-language vocal vs target-language vocal (interaction = target minus source, 95% song-bootstrap CI):
- 1960s: es +.068 vs +.064, interaction -.004 [-.031,+.029]; pt +.057 vs +.078, +.020 [-.028,+.072]; de +.057 vs +.059, +.002 [-.034,+.041]; it +.057 vs +.110, +.053 [-.006,+.121].
- 1980s: es +.170 vs +.166, -.004 [-.046,+.043]; pt +.166 vs +.153, -.013 [-.064,+.041]; de +.166 vs +.128, -.037 [-.079,-.001]; it +.166 vs +.162, -.003 [-.057,+.059].
- 2010s: es +.169 vs +.155, -.013 [-.073,+.047]; pt +.187 vs +.173, -.014 [-.104,+.063]; de +.187 vs +.167, -.020 [-.101,+.050]; it +.187 vs +.169, -.018 [-.083,+.039].
Language reading of the remix (P(intended language) with neutral / with 1980s accompaniment; source-language control with the same accompaniment): es .803 / .768 (.715 / .678); pt .611 / .594 (.743 / .709); de .819 / .822 (.743 / .709); it .770 / .752 (.743 / .709).
Observation: the era gain of the accompaniment is the same with the source-language vocal and with the translated vocal (11 of 12 interactions within +-.05, all intervals containing 0 except 1980s x German at -.037 [-.079,-.001], uncorrected among 12), and the realized language does not depend on the accompaniment (differences of the language reading between neutral and 1980s accompaniment of .00-.04). So the two controls compose without a measurable interaction on these 16 songs, one seed, one vocal per cell. Limits: probes are the same family as before; language is the market component that moves (T-4l); no human test.

## 2026-11-04 — T-4e metadata-derived style descriptors for the accompaniment (`src/113_market_styles.py`, `src/114_market_style_gen.py`, `src/115_market_style_eval.py`, `results/market_styles.{json,txt}`, `results/market_style_eval.{json,txt}`)
Question: can style words derived from the Discogs-VI metadata (instead of country names) condition the accompaniment toward a market? Step 1 (`113`): per release country x decade lift of each Discogs style among the 1.9M versions (P(style | market, decade) / P(style | decade); styles with at least 200 versions in the cell); strongest signatures: Brazil (1980s: Choro 30x, Forro 30x, MPB 28x at P .41, Samba 17x), Spain (Flamenco 16-21x in the 1960s, 1970s and 1990s, Rumba, Bolero; 1980s: Cool Jazz 3.5x, Ballad 2.4x), Germany (Schlager 8x in the 1960s, 3.6x in the 1980s), Italy and the others weaker; US and UK have lift near 1 for most styles because they dominate the global baseline (full table in the txt). Step 2 (`114`, MusicGen-Melody CFG 3, chroma of the original mix, 24 dataset-B validation clips, 2 seeds): conditions neutral, `hw_<market>` (hand-written 1980s prompts of 07_generate.py) and `ds_<market>` = "a <adjective> recording in the styles S1, S2 and S3" with the three highest-lift styles of (market, 1980s). Step 3 (`115`, Discogs-EffNet 400-tag activations of the generated accompaniment, essentia, CPU): target score = mean activation of the five highest-lift styles of the market among the 400 tags; Delta = prompted minus neutral clip of the same song and seed (n = 48 pairs, song-bootstrap 95% CI). Commands: `GENDIR=gen_mstyle python src/114_market_style_gen.py --seeds 0 1 [--reverse | --rot k]` (several workers), `GENDIR=gen_mstyle python src/115_market_style_eval.py` (venv_ess).
Delta target-style activation, hand-written / metadata-derived (neutral level; real accompaniment of the market's clips): US +.0003 / +.0037 (.0011; .0025); UK +.0012 / +.0023 (.0007; .0297); Brazil +.0174 / +.0545 [+.042,+.068] (.0036; .0283); Spain +.0009 / +.0028 [+.0003,+.0052] (.0091; .0074); Germany -.0052 / +.0032 [-.0002,+.0066] (.0139; .0049); Italy +.0327 [+.023,+.043] / +.0049 [+.0021,+.0075] (.0079; .0343).
Metadata-derived prompts used: Brazil "Choro, Forro and MPB", Spain "Cool Jazz, Ballad and Bolero", Germany "Schlager, Romantic and Europop", Italy "Canzone Napoletana, Italo-Disco and Chanson", US "Bluegrass, Gospel and Hawaiian", UK "Lovers Rock, Psychobilly and Mod".
Observation: the metadata-derived descriptor moves the target-style activation clearly for Brazil (+.055, above the activation of the real Brazilian clips, .028) and by a few thousandths elsewhere; for Italy the hand-written prompt (which names "italo disco") is much stronger than the metadata-derived one (+.033 vs +.005); for Germany neither works. The descriptors picked by lift are rare styles for the markets with weak signatures (US: Bluegrass, Gospel, Hawaiian), so lift alone is a poor descriptor selection. Interpretation (not tested): descriptors work when they are both distinctive in the metadata and meaningful to the text encoder (samba, MPB); generic words (Ballad, Vocal) do not move the tagger. Next: select descriptors by the contribution to the market's divergence (P x log lift) instead of lift (T-4o).

## 2026-11-04 — T-9c vocal chain parameters tuned on TRAIN vocals (`src/124_vocal_chain_tune.py`, `results/vocal_chain_tune.{json,txt}`)
Setting: 60 dataset-A TRAIN clips (10 per decade) held out from the vocal era probe used as objective (probe refit on the other TRAIN clips, MERT layer 20, C .01); objective = mean P(target decade | processed vocal) - P(target | dry vocal) over the 60 clips; grids: 1960s low cut {80,150,300} Hz x high cut {4,7,10} kHz x saturation drive {1,2,4}; 1980s high-shelf gain {.3,.6,1.0} x reverb wet {.15,.25,.40}; 2010s compression ratio {4,6,10} x brightening {.2,.4,.8}; the selected setting is evaluated once on the 24 evaluation songs (vocal probe fit on all TRAIN; mix probe with the era-axis accompaniment of `gen_ax_ctrlc_ge10`, seeds 0-2). Command: `python src/124_vocal_chain_tune.py`.
Tuning objective, best (default): 1960s +.034 at (80 Hz, 10 kHz, drive 2) (default not in the grid); 1980s +.274 at (shelf 1.0, wet .40) (default +.177); 2010s +.173 at (ratio 10, bright .8) (default +.160).
Evaluation on the 24 songs, Delta vs dry vocal (95% song-bootstrap CI), vocal probe / mix-level with era-axis accompaniment: 1960s default -.012 / -.037, tuned +.007 [-.009,+.026] / -.034 [-.047,-.021]; 1980s default +.186 / +.068, tuned +.266 [+.202,+.332] / +.106 [+.081,+.130]; 2010s default +.169 / +.043, tuned +.178 [+.103,+.257] / +.043 [+.026,+.061].
Observation: tuning on TRAIN vocals raises the 1980s effect (vocal +.19 to +.27, mix +.07 to +.11), leaves the 2010s effect unchanged, and does not make the 1960s chain work (vocal +.007, mix still negative). The best 1980s setting lies at the upper edge of the grid (shelf 1.0, wet .40), so the optimum is not bracketed. The "median control" of the script coincides with the default for the 1980s and 2010s (the median setting of the grid is the default), so it is not a random-chain control; a proper control with random draws is open (T-9e). Interpretation (not tested): the 1960s reading of the probe may rely on cues absent from these chains (tape hiss, wow and flutter, narrower telephone-like bandwidth, mono room tone).

## 2026-11-04 — T-4o descriptor selection by P x log(lift) (`src/113_market_styles.py` `top_kl`, `src/114_market_style_gen.py` `dk_` conditions, `src/125_mstyle_dk.sh`, `results/market_style_eval.{json,txt}` now with hw, ds and dk families)
Setting as T-4e; `dk_<market>` = "a <adjective> recording in the styles S1, S2 and S3" with the three styles of largest P(style | market, 1980s) x log(lift) among styles with lift above 1: US "Bluegrass, Country and Soul"; UK "Soul, Synth-pop and Vocal"; Brazil "MPB, Samba and Bossanova"; Spain "Ballad, Cool Jazz and Disco"; Germany "Schlager, Easy Listening and Synth-pop"; Italy "Italo-Disco, Chanson and Canzone Napoletana". Same read-out (target score = five highest-lift styles of the market among the 400 tags; 24 clips x 2 seeds = 48 pairs). Commands: `bash 125_mstyle_dk.sh 0 2 3` (113 recomputed with `top_kl`, generation of the new conditions, `115`).
Delta target-style activation, hand-written / lift (ds) / P x log lift (dk): US +.0003 / +.0037 / +.0018 [+.0011,+.0026]; UK +.0012 / +.0023 / +.0006; Brazil +.0174 / +.0545 / +.0490 [+.0368,+.0621]; Spain +.0009 / +.0028 / +.0021 [-.0006,+.0049]; Germany -.0052 / +.0032 / .0000 [-.0034,+.0036]; Italy +.0327 / +.0049 / +.0060 [+.0020,+.0099].
Observation: choosing the descriptors by P x log(lift) does not change the picture of T-4e: Brazil responds strongly (+.049, close to the lift-selected +.055), US, UK, Spain, Germany and Italy by at most a few thousandths, below the hand-written Italy prompt (+.033). The result is consistent with descriptors working when the style words are concrete musical vocabulary of a strongly separated tradition (MPB, samba, bossa nova); generic words (Ballad, Soul, Synth-pop) do not move the tagger. Not tested: combined descriptors with decade words, longer descriptions, or a text-encoder-aware selection; open in `TODO.md`.

## 2026-11-04 — T-9e extended vocal chains and random-chain control (`src/126_vocal_chain_tune2.py`, `results/vocal_chain_tune2.{json,txt}`)
Protocol of T-9c (60 TRAIN clips for tuning against a probe fit on the other TRAIN clips; one evaluation on the 24 songs); new grids: 1980s shelf {1.0,1.5,2.0} x reverb wet {.40,.55,.70}; 1960s chain with tape cues: band (120 Hz-7 kHz) or telephone-like (300 Hz-3.4 kHz) x hiss {-45,-35,-25} dB below the vocal RMS x wow {0,1,3} ms (0.7 Hz delay modulation), saturation drive 2; 2010s fixed at the T-9c best; random control = 20 settings drawn uniformly from the union of the ranges, vocal-probe Delta on the 24 songs. Command: `python src/126_vocal_chain_tune2.py`.
Tuned settings: 1960s (300 Hz, 3.4 kHz, hiss -45 dB, wow 3 ms), tuning objective +.126; 1980s (shelf 2.0, wet .55), +.331 (edge of the grid in shelf again); 2010s (ratio 10, bright .8).
Evaluation (24 songs), Delta vs dry vocal, vocal probe / mix-level with era-axis accompaniment: 1960s default (no tape cues) +.002 [-.017,+.023] / -.037 [-.050,-.024]; 1960s tuned +.097 [+.056,+.145] / -.008 [-.024,+.009]; 1980s default +.186 / +.068; 1980s tuned +.322 [+.253,+.395] / +.132 [+.103,+.163]; 2010s default +.169 / +.043; 2010s tuned +.178 / +.043.
Random chains (20 draws per decade from the same parameter ranges), vocal-probe Delta mean (min, max): 1960s +.043 (+.023, +.091); 1980s +.266 (+.159, +.338); 2010s +.170 (+.158, +.177).
Observation: tape cues make the 1960s chain work on the vocal probe (+.097, from +.002) and remove the negative mix-level effect (-.037 to -.008, interval contains 0) but do not yet produce a positive mix-level gain; the tuned 1960s setting is at the upper end of the random draws (max +.091), so the choice of values within the chain family matters little compared with the family itself; for the 1980s the vocal effect grows with shelf and reverb up to +.32 (mix +.13), at the edge of the explored range. These effects are probe-based (same family as before); no listening check.

## 2026-11-04 — T-4n voice conversion with a voice-matched reference (`src/108_svc_market.py --nearest`, `results/svc_market_eval_nn.{json,txt}`)
Variant of T-4a: for each market the reference is the TRAIN vocal stem of that market whose ECAPA2 embedding is nearest (cosine) to the embedding of the source clip (instead of the mean of ten clips); everything else as T-4a (102 validation vocals x 6 markets, 612 conversions, no failures). Commands: `python ~/era_market/src/108_svc_market.py --nearest --outdir ~/era_market/svc_B_nn` (venv_svc, from ext/free-svc), `SVCDIR=svc_B_nn python src/109_svc_market_eval.py`.
Results (MERT / MuQ): accuracy of the target-voice market .178 / .193 (chance .167); accuracy of the original label after conversion .426 / .199 (T-4a mean-voice: .446 / .203; self-voice control .451 / .196); Delta P(target | own-voice vs other voices) per market within +.010 [-.001,+.023] (Italy, MERT) and -.003 to +.006 for the others; MuQ all within +.003.
Observation: a voice-matched reference does not move the market reading either (all per-market effects within about +-.01), so the negative timbre result of T-4a does not depend on how the reference voice is chosen; the loss on the original label again equals the conversion-pipeline loss of the self-voice control.

- 1980s (tuned chain shelf 2.0, wet .55): P(1980s) dry/neutral .098, chain/neutral .129, dry/era .355, chain/era .487; chain main effect +.082 [+.055,+.110]; accompaniment main effect +.307 [+.256,+.359]; interaction +.101 [+.063,+.141].
- 2010s (tuned chain ratio 10, bright .8): .370, .421, .594, .637; chain main effect +.047 [+.028,+.070]; accompaniment main effect +.220 [+.156,+.285]; interaction -.007 [-.022,+.011].
Observation: for the 2010s the two controls add up (interaction within +-.02); for the 1980s the vocal chain is worth more on top of the era accompaniment (+.132) than on the neutral accompaniment (+.031), a super-additive interaction of +.10 on the probe. Interpretation (not tested): the 1980s reading of the probe is driven by joint cues (gated reverb on the vocal together with synthesizer accompaniment), so the cues reinforce each other.

## 2026-11-04 — T-4p descriptors selected on TRAIN clips by their tag score, and an independent probe read-out of all descriptor families (`src/128_dsel_gen.py`, `src/129_dsel_pick.py`, `src/130_dsel.sh`, `results/dsel_selected.json`, `results/market_style_eval.{json,txt}` with the `dt_` family, `results/mstyle_probe_{hw,ds,dk,dt}.{json,txt}`)
Setting: for each market the candidate words were the style names of top_lift and top_kl of (market, 1980s) (up to 14); each word was used in "a <adjective> recording in the style <word>" on 6 dataset-B TRAIN clips (one per market, seed 0) and scored by the same target-style activation as T-4e (Discogs-EffNet tags, five highest-lift styles of the market) minus the neutral clip; the three best words form `dt_<market>`; evaluation as T-4e on the 24 evaluation clips x 2 seeds. Selected words: US Honky Tonk, Gospel, Funk; UK Roots Reggae, Rockabilly, Vocal; Brazil Bossanova, Bossa Nova, Choro; Spain Ballad, Bolero, Disco; Germany Dixieland, Contemporary Jazz, Easy Listening; Italy Easy Listening, Swing, Chanson. Commands: `bash 130_dsel.sh 0 2 3`; independent read-out: `COND_PREFIX=<hw_|ds_|dk_|dt_> GENDIR=gen_mstyle python src/37_eval_market.py --out results/mstyle_probe_<family>.json --seeds 0 1` (codec-matched MERT and MuQ market probes trained on dataset-B TRAIN accompaniment, as M-3 and 2026-10-26; Delta = P(market | prompt of the market) - P(market | neutral), Wilcoxon over 24 songs).
Tag read-out (dt): Brazil +.0630 [+.050,+.077]; Germany +.0197 [+.014,+.026] (ds +.0032, dk .0000); Italy +.0192 [+.013,+.025] (ds +.0049, dk +.0060, hand-written +.0327); UK +.0068; Spain +.0036; US +.0012.
Independent probe read-out, Delta P(target market) MERT / MuQ: hand-written: US +.040 / +.154, UK +.030 / -.007, Brazil -.030 / +.269, Spain -.070 / -.035, Germany +.085 / +.137, Italy +.132 / -.017; lift (ds): US +.086 / +.163, UK +.008 / +.048, Brazil +.145 / +.490, Spain -.044 / +.059, Germany -.169 / -.240, Italy +.040 / -.086; P x log lift (dk): US -.003 / +.006, UK -.016 / -.062, Brazil -.006 / +.199, Spain -.068 / -.059, Germany -.077 / -.174, Italy +.115 / -.015; selected on TRAIN (dt): US +.028 / +.044, UK -.001 / +.036, Brazil +.117 / +.313, Spain -.038 / -.026, Germany -.060 / -.066, Italy -.044 / -.072.
Observation: (1) the tag score improves for Germany and Italy after selection on TRAIN clips, but the selected words (Dixieland, Easy Listening, Swing) are the words that raise the tagger's activation of the market's target tags, not words characteristic of the market, and the independent market probes read the same clips as less German (-.06 / -.07) and less Italian (-.04 / -.07): the gain is an effect of optimizing against the tagger that is also the read-out, not evidence of a market move. (2) On the independent probes the only market that moves in the intended direction for both encoders and for several descriptor families is Brazil (ds +.145 / +.490, dt +.117 / +.313, dk -.006 / +.199) and, for the lift descriptor, the US (+.086 / +.163); Germany and Italy move the wrong way for the metadata descriptors. Interpretation (not tested): descriptors work for strongly separated, concrete traditions (Brazil); for the others the 24 clips, the probes' accuracy (.28-.33 unerased) and the prompt vocabulary do not support a measurable move.

## 2026-11-04 — T-4q descriptors selected with the market probes, read out with the tagger (`src/131_dsel_probe_pick.py`, `src/131_dsel_probe.sh`, `results/dsel_selected_probe.json`, `results/market_style_eval.{json,txt}` family dp, `results/mstyle_probe_dp.{json,txt}`)
Swap of the instruments of T-4p: the words were selected on the 6 TRAIN clips by the mean of the MERT and MuQ market-probe probability gain over the neutral clip, and read out on the 24 evaluation clips by both the tagger (independent of the selection) and the probes (the selection instrument). Command: `bash 131_dsel_probe.sh 0 2 3`. Selected words (dp): US Soul, Gospel, Bop; UK Mod, Novelty, Psychobilly; Brazil Forro, Bolero, Choro; Spain Ballad, Bolero, Vocal; Germany Blues Rock, Disco, Synth-pop; Italy Bop, Europop, Easy Listening.
Probe read-out (selection instrument), Delta P(target market) MERT / MuQ: US +.037 / +.034; UK -.003 / +.068; Brazil +.348 / +.573; Spain +.070 / +.016; Germany +.190 / +.119; Italy +.186 / +.032.
Tag read-out (independent instrument), Delta target-style activation: US +.0006; UK +.0010; Brazil +.0779 [+.060,+.097]; Spain +.0011; Germany -.0063 [-.009,-.003]; Italy +.0075 [+.005,+.010].
Observation: each selection inflates the instrument that was used to select: tagger-selected words (dt) gave Germany +.020 on the tagger and -.060 / -.066 on the probes; probe-selected words (dp) give Germany +.190 / +.119 on the probes and -.006 on the tagger. The only market that moves on both instruments for the metadata-derived and the selected descriptors is Brazil (tagger +.055 to +.078, probes +.117 to +.348 MERT and +.199 to +.573 MuQ). Interpretation: optimizing words against one automatic instrument does not transfer to the other (selection effect), so claims about descriptors need an instrument that is independent of the selection, and for Germany, Italy, Spain, UK and the US the descriptor route has not produced a move supported by two instruments.

## 2026-11-04 — T-9f era-specific processing of the whole mix (mix-bus chains) (`src/132_mixbus_chain.py`, `results/mixbus_chain.{json,txt}`)
Question: the vocal-only 1960s chain left the mix-level reading negative (T-9, T-9e); does processing the full mix work? Setting: chain families of T-9e applied to the whole remix; tuned on 60 dataset-A TRAIN real mixes (10 per decade) with a mix-level probe (MERT layer 12) fit on the other TRAIN mixes (objective = P(target) of the processed minus dry mix); grids: 1960s 18 settings (band x hiss x wow), 1980s shelf {.6,1,2} x wet {.25,.4,.55}, 2010s ratio {6,10} x bright {.4,.8}; evaluated once on the 24 evaluation songs on remixes = dry original vocal + era-axis accompaniment (`gen_ax_ctrlc_ge10`, seeds 0-2) and, for additivity, the neutral-accompaniment remix; probe fit on all TRAIN. Command: `python src/132_mixbus_chain.py`.
Tuned settings (objective): 1960s (120 Hz-7 kHz, hiss -45 dB, wow 3 ms) +.025; 1980s (shelf 2.0, wet .40) +.049; 2010s (ratio 10, bright .4) +.123.
Delta P(decade) from the mix-bus chain (95% song-bootstrap CI), on the era-axis remix / on the neutral-accompaniment remix: 1960s +.062 [+.025,+.105] / +.091 [+.059,+.127]; 1980s +.084 [+.031,+.141] / +.049 [+.010,+.097]; 2010s +.092 [+.067,+.119] / +.177 [+.148,+.209].
Observation: processing the whole mix yields a positive mix-level gain for all three decades, including the 1960s (+.062) where the vocal-only chain did not (-.037 to -.008); the gain on the era-axis remix is smaller than on the neutral remix for the 1960s and the 2010s (the accompaniment already carries part of the cue) and larger for the 1980s (+.084 vs +.049). The tuning objective on real TRAIN mixes was small for the 1960s (+.025); the probe is the same family as the era read-outs of earlier entries.

## 2026-11-04 — T-4f syllable-matched translations through the lyric route (`src/133_syllable_translate.py`, `src/133_syllable.sh`, `results/syllable_translate_stats.json`, `results/syl_market_probe.{json,txt}`, `results/lyric_route_eval_ace_syl_swap.{json,txt}`)
Question: does choosing, among 8 beam candidates, the translation whose syllable count is closest to the source transcript (syllables = vowel groups; a crude proxy for singability) change the lyric route? Setting: same 77 clips, targets and pipeline as T-4k (cover mode strength 0.95, seed 0, stem swap, vocal market probe); only the translation text differs. Command: `bash 133_syllable.sh 1`. The text files are not committed.
Syllable gap to the source (mean absolute difference, top beam -> selected): es 14.2 -> 12.2; pt 13.7 -> 10.6; de 7.9 -> 5.8; it 15.0 -> 12.5; en 13.9 -> 10.1 (relative difference, top beam -> selected: es .245 -> .215, pt .325 -> .249, de .100 -> .079, it .390 -> .327, en .130 -> .116).
Vocal market probe, Delta P(target) vs control (T-4k seed 0 in parentheses): Spain +.199 [+.143,+.256] (+.188); Brazil +.464 [+.378,+.552] (+.421); Germany +.330 [+.253,+.404] (+.314); Italy +.360 [+.283,+.430] (+.408); US+UK +.357 [+.269,+.445] (+.325).
Language top-1 hit (T-4k in parentheses): es .683 (.635), pt .672 (.672), de .763 (.763), it .514 (.625), en .912 (.882); lyric similarity to the intended text: .510 / .475 / .486 / .489 / .474 (.458 / .441 / .485 / .508 / .432); VINet cosine .803-.828 (.804-.834).
Observation: re-ranking the beams by syllable count reduces the gap by 15-30% but leaves 6-12 syllables per 30 s excerpt; the market move, the language hit, the intelligibility and the identity are within the sampling error of the plain translations (differences of the probe Delta -.05 to +.04). A proper singable translation needs the melody (note durations), not a text-only syllable count; the melody-aware route is T-4h.

## 2026-11-04 — Guide topic 1 on the PA1 data: proxy pairs from Discogs-VINet similarity (`src/134_proxy_pairs.py`, `results/proxy_pairs.{json,txt}`)
Question (guide: relate the predicted era shift of two recordings to the release-year gap): the PA1 manifest hides ids, so candidate same-composition pairs are the dataset-A clip pairs with Discogs-VINet cosine >= T (precision unverified), restricted to different decade labels. Expected decade E of every labeled clip is obtained out-of-fold (5-fold, MERT mix / vocals / accompaniment probes with the layers and C of `probe_A.json`). Per pair the predicted shift = E(later-labeled clip) - E(earlier-labeled clip). Control: 1000 random cross-decade pairs. Command: `python src/134_proxy_pairs.py`. Nearest-neighbour statistics (VINet cosine): dataset A has 40 pairs with cosine >= .8 (28 with different labels), 335 with >= .75 (219 different), 1,961 with >= .7 (1,307 different); dataset B 20 / 151 / 951 pairs.
Results (fraction of pairs whose predicted order agrees with the label order; mean predicted shift in decades vs mean label gap; Spearman of shift and label gap), mix / vocals / accompaniment:
- T = .8 (28 pairs): .75 / .71 / .61; +.69 / +.58 / +.45 vs gap 2.43; .35 / .37 / .28.
- T = .75 (219 pairs): .79 / .72 / .76; +1.15 / +.71 / +1.03 vs 2.39; .44 / .23 / .40.
- T = .7 (1,307 pairs): .81 / .73 / .79; +1.30 / +.72 / +1.18 vs 2.35; .49 / .35 / .44.
- random cross-decade pairs (1,000): .83 / .76 / .80; +1.36 / +.87 / +1.20 vs 2.26; .54 / .35 / .49.
Observation: the predicted shift of the VINet-similar pairs is smaller than the label gap (mean shift .45-1.30 decades against a gap of 2.4) and indistinguishable from random cross-decade pairs (order agreement .61-.81 vs .76-.83, Spearman .23-.49 vs .35-.54); the shift of the closest pairs (T .8) is lower than for random pairs (not tested: 28 pairs). The PA1 subset therefore carries almost no same-composition pairs (the 99th percentile of the nearest-neighbour cosine is .83) and this test cannot say whether a recording follows its own release era or the original's: it needs verified cover pairs (Discogs-VI audio or ids, pending the TA).

## 2026-11-04 — best-of-N over generation seeds for the lyric route, selector = language reading (`src/135_bon_language.py`, `src/135_bon_language.sh`, `results/bon_language.{json,txt}`, `results/scale_market_probe_seed{2,3}*`)
Idea (the N-5a selection transferred to the market axis): generate the lyric route with 4 seeds (0-3: `121_lyric_scale_seed.sh` for seeds 2 and 3, seeds 0 and 1 from T-4k), select per (clip, target language) the seed with the highest Whisper large-v3 P(intended language) of the swapped mix, and evaluate on the vocal market probe (plain; language-erased) of the selected candidate; compare with the average over the four seeds and the oracle (best candidate by the probe itself, upper bound). Same 77 clips and translations as T-4k. Command: `bash 135_bon_language.sh 1 2` (per-clip probabilities via `120_swap_market_probe.py` `_clips.json`).
Delta P(target market) vs the source-language control, plain probe: selected / average over seeds / oracle: Spain +.234 [+.173,+.296] / +.193 / +.351; Brazil +.563 [+.478,+.645] / +.486 / +.680; Germany +.371 [+.303,+.438] / +.312 / +.479; Italy +.464 [+.393,+.536] / +.389 / +.557; US+UK +.323 [+.257,+.391] / +.308 / +.489. Language-erased probe, selected / average: Spain +.024 / +.017; Brazil -.005 / +.007; Germany -.038 / -.029; Italy +.005 / +.004; US+UK +.050 / +.027.
Share of clips with P(intended language) above .5, selected / average over seeds: es .84 / .56; pt .81 / .58; de .86 / .65; it .71 / .46; en .97 / .79.
Observation: selecting among four seeds by the language reading raises the share of clips that realize the target language from .46-.79 to .71-.97 and the plain-probe market move by .015-.077 (selected minus average), reaching 70-83% of the oracle gain; the language-erased probe still shows no move (|Delta| <= .05), so selection improves the language component only. The selector (Whisper language reading) is independent of the market probe, so the plain-probe gain is not a selector-instrument effect, in contrast to T-4q.

## 2026-11-04 — N-7 pilot: ACE-Step 1.5 era LoRA (`src/136_ace_lora_data.py`, `src/136_ace_lora.sh`, `src/136_run_trainer.py`, `src/137_ace_lora_gen.py`, `src/137_ace_lora_eval.sh`, `results/ace_lora_gen_{none,e3}_eval.json`, `results/six_decades_ace_lora_gen_{none,e3}.{json,txt}`)
Question: does a LoRA on a stronger generator carry the era axis? Setting: training set = the 1,026 labeled dataset-A TRAIN clips, accompaniment stem (HT Demucs, instrumental), caption = the era prompt of the clip's decade (`prompts_era.py`), lyrics "[Instrumental]"; ACE-Step 1.5 turbo, Side-Step `fixed` trainer, LoRA rank 64 alpha 128 (44.0M trainable parameters), batch 4, lr 1e-4, bf16, 3 epochs (about 60 s per epoch on one H200; final loss .821). Environment fixes: `torchaudio.load` replaced by a soundfile loader in the wrapper (the installed torchcodec is built for CUDA 13), dataset and output directories placed inside the ACE-Step checkout (the trainer's path-safety check), `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES` with `--device cuda:0`. Generation: cover mode (strength 0.5, 8 steps) on the original accompaniment stem of the 24 evaluation songs with the six decade prompts and neutral, seeds 0 and 1, original vocal remixed; control: the same call without the LoRA. Read-out: codec-matched MERT and MuQ era probes (`10_eval_gen.py`, `78_six_decades.py`). Commands: `bash 136_ace_lora.sh 1 3`, `bash 137_ace_lora_eval.sh 1 ace_lora_gen_e3 <ext/ACE-Step-1.5/ace_lora_out_e3/final> 0.5`, `bash 137_ace_lora_eval.sh 3 ace_lora_gen_none none 0.5`.
MERT Delta P(target decade) 1960s / 1970s / 1980s / 1990s / 2000s / 2010s: no LoRA +.063 / +.094 / +.143 / +.038 / -.062 / +.079; 3-epoch LoRA +.241 [+.174,+.312] / +.109 / +.453 [+.352,+.553] / -.045 / -.018 / +.133 [+.066,+.209]. MuQ: no LoRA +.107 / +.086 / +.185 / +.069 / -.001 / +.015; LoRA +.164 / +.131 / +.321 / +.048 / -.036 / +.044.
Observation: with three epochs the era LoRA on ACE-Step raises the 1960s, 1980s and 2010s effects over the same pipeline without it (MERT +.24 vs +.06, +.45 vs +.14, +.13 vs +.08) while 1990s and 2000s stay at zero; the effects are comparable to the MusicGen era adapter for 1980s (+.48 there) and smaller for the others. A 20-epoch run is training (`ace_lora_out_e20`).

## 2026-11-04 — N-7 pilot continued: training length, checkpoints and training seeds (`results/ace_lora_gen_{e1,e2,e5,e10,e20,e3_s1,e3_s2}_eval.json`, `results/six_decades_ace_lora_gen_*`; `src/136_ace_lora.sh <gpu> <epochs> [seed]`)
Same generation and read-out as the 3-epoch entry (24 songs, six decade prompts and neutral, seeds 0 and 1, cover strength 0.5, codec-matched probes). The checkpoints e1, e2 are the epoch-1 and epoch-2 saves of the 3-epoch run, e5 and e10 are saves of the 20-epoch run (mid-schedule of its cosine learning-rate decay), e20 is the final adapter of the 20-epoch run; e3_s1 and e3_s2 are the 3-epoch recipe with training seeds 1 and 2 (seed 0 = the first 3-epoch run).
MERT Delta P(target decade), 1960s / 1980s / 2010s (final loss): no LoRA +.063 / +.143 / +.079; e1 +.099 / +.188 / +.111 (.895); e2 +.109 / +.155 / +.076 (.820); e3 seed 0 +.241 / +.453 / +.133 (.821); e3 seed 1 +.170 / +.444 / +.160 (.820); e3 seed 2 +.157 / +.416 / +.124 (.822); e5 +.063 / +.132 / +.015 (.806); e10 +.047 / +.231 / +.031 (.798); e20 +.078 / +.385 / +.069 (.797). MuQ 1980s: no LoRA +.185; e3 seeds 0 / 1 / 2 +.321 / +.290 / +.307; e20 +.113.
Observation: the 3-epoch recipe reproduces over three training seeds (1960s +.16 to +.24, 1980s +.42 to +.45, 2010s +.12 to +.16; MuQ 1980s +.29 to +.32), against +.06, +.14, +.08 without the LoRA; the mid-schedule checkpoints (e1, e2, e5, e10) are close to the no-LoRA values and the 20-epoch final adapter is weaker than the 3-epoch ones for the 1960s and 2010s (+.08, +.07) but not for the 1980s (+.39). The training loss decreases monotonically (.895 to .797) while the era effect is not monotonic in epochs, so the loss is not a selection criterion; I did not select a checkpoint on the evaluation songs, all runs are reported. Interpretation (not tested): checkpoints saved before the learning rate has decayed behave differently from the annealed final adapters of short runs, and longer training overfits the caption style; the 1990s and 2000s effects stay at zero for all adapters.

Same 24 songs x seven prompts x 2 seeds as the N-7 entries (remix = original vocal + generated accompaniment).

## T-11: independent per-decade read-out (Discogs-EffNet embedding + logistic probe), 1990s/2000s check

Read-out: `src/36_discogs_tagger.py --gen <dir> --seeds ... --eras neutral,1960s,...,2010s` (probe CV acc .475, held-out val acc .523, six classes, 24 source clips, CPU). Outputs `results/discogs_decade_{ax_ctrlc,ace_e3,ace_none}.json`. Sources: `gen_ax_ctrlc_ge10` (seeds 0-2), `ace_lora_gen_e3`, `ace_lora_gen_none` (seeds 0-1). Values below: mean P(target decade) / share of clips whose argmax is the target.

| generator | 1960s | 1970s | 1980s | 1990s | 2000s | 2010s |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| MusicGen + z adapter (ge 10) | .80/1.00 | .39/.58 | .45/.88 | .37/.53 | .34/.60 | .39/.64 |
| ACE-Step + era LoRA (3 ep) | .25/.46 | .17/.15 | .45/.73 | .28/.40 | .12/.00 | .24/.46 |
| ACE-Step, no LoRA (prompt only) | .06/.00 | .10/.00 | .27/.35 | .36/.77 | .15/.00 | .27/.46 |

Reference (unconditioned original clips, identity codec): argmax shares .29 .17 .04 .12 .17 .21 (probe is weak on real audio, val acc .52).
Measured: with this independent read-out the MusicGen adapter reaches the target for 1990s (.53) and 2000s (.60), well above the 1/6 chance level; the ACE LoRA never has the 2000s as argmax (0 of its clips) and 1970s only .15; the no-LoRA ACE output is dominated by 1990s/2010s regardless of the requested era.
Interpretation: the MERT/MuQ probe's low 2000s recall (B8) is a property of that read-out, not necessarily of the generator; the Discogs-EffNet probe is also weak (.52), so both read-outs are reported. Confusions are mostly adjacent decades (1990s to 1980s .33, 2000s to 1990s .29, 2010s to 2000s .24).

## M-5: CultureMERT-95M as a market read-out (B1)

`src/140_culturemert_market.py` (layer outputs captured by forward hooks, 12 transformer layers; logistic probe, C by 5-fold CV on TRAIN; 798 train / 102 validation; chance .167). Files `results/culturemert_market.{json,txt}`.
Mix: best layer by CV 8, validation .255 (best validation over layers .363). Accompaniment stem: best layer by CV 2, validation .304 (best over layers .363). Reference: MERT-v2-30s probe on the mix, validation .559 (layer 14, `results/probe_B.json`); accompaniment-only M-3 encoder .22-.33.
Measured: the culture-aware encoder does not read the 1980s release market better than MERT-v2 on the same clips (mix .26 vs .56) and sits in the same .30 band as M-3 on the accompaniment. Interpretation: the accompaniment carries little market information for these encoders; the MERT-v2 mix result is largely language (B7, language-erased probe near chance). MERT-v1-95M could not be loaded (.bin weights, torch < 2.6 refuses torch.load), so the same-architecture control is missing.

## P-3: Da-TACOS cover-analysis pairs with MusicBrainz release country (metadata only)

Data: Da-TACOS metadata (Yesiler et al., ISMIR 2019; Zenodo 4717628, `da-tacos_metadata.zip`, CC BY-NC-SA 4.0; features not downloaded, no audio). `python3 src/141_datacos_pairs.py <dir> > results/datacos_pairs.json`.
Cover-analysis subset: 5,000 works, 10,000 performances, 4,968 works with two release years, median year gap 10, 2,485 gaps >= 10 years and 1,548 gaps >= 20 years; 6,821 performances carry a MusicBrainz recording id. Benchmark subset: 15,000 performances, 26,802 pairs with gap >= 10 and 14,222 with gap >= 20 (1,000 works with year info, the rest are singletons).
MusicBrainz country: `src/142_mb_country.py` (public API, 1 request per second, first recording id per performance, release country and date of every release of that recording) running on the server (tmux `mb`, about 2 h for 6,821 ids), output `results/datacos_mb_country.json` (60-id test: all 60 answered; release country counts US 70, GB 37, XE 28, AU 22, DE 19, IT 18, no country 20, XW 16).
Limit: the audio and chroma features for generation are not part of the metadata; features are on Zenodo (hpcp 7 GB, crema 1.3 GB) and audio must come from elsewhere.

## 2026-11-05 — P-3c HPCP chroma as the MusicGen-Melody chroma input (`src/143_hpcp_chroma.py`, `src/99b_era_axis_gen.py` with `HPCP_CHROMA=1`, `results/ax_ctrlc_ge10_hpcp_{eval,content}.json`)
Question: can a chroma computed without the processor (essentia HPCP) condition MusicGen-Melody equivalently, so that cover pairs without original-stem access can be used? Method: HPCP (44.1 kHz, frame 4096, hop 512, blackmanharris62, whitening, 12 bins), bins rolled by 3 (HPCP bin 0 is A at 440 Hz, processor chroma bin 0 is C), mean-pooled to 235 frames, one-hot per frame by argmax, passed in place of the processor chroma. Same 24 clips x 6 eras x 3 seeds as `ax_ctrlc_ge10` (ge 10), command `env HPCP_CHROMA=1 SUF=_hpcp bash src/run_ax.sh 0 ax_ctrlc A_eraz_mert_leacec0.0_s0 10` after `python src/143_hpcp_chroma.py` (CPU).
Measured (432 clips each): target-decade hit MERT .854 / MuQ .824 (processor chroma .819 / .764); content top-1 .873, sim_own .893, chord agreement .398 (processor chroma .854, .879, .347). Without the roll the content collapsed (top-1 .083).
Interpretation: HPCP chroma is a usable substitute for the processor chroma in this setting (single run, one seed set).

## 2026-11-05 — N-7b (3) ACE-Step era LoRA with year-augmented captions (`src/144_ace_lora_year.sh`, `YEAR_CAPTION` in `src/137_ace_lora_gen.py`, `results/ace_lora_gen_yr_e3_eval.json`, `results/six_decades_ace_lora_gen_yr_e3.{json,txt}`)
Question: do captions with a year ("..., recorded in 1994", year drawn uniformly inside the clip's decade, seed 0) make the 1990s and 2000s appear? Setting as the N-7 pilot (rank 64, alpha 128, lr 1e-4, 3 epochs, cover strength 0.5); generation appends "recorded in <decade>4" to the decade prompt. Command: `bash src/144_ace_lora_year.sh 1 3`.
Measured (MERT effect [95% CI], top-1): 1960s +.182 (.42), 1970s +.133 (.67), 1980s +.400 (.81), 1990s +.016 [-.029,+.071] (.08), 2000s +.006 [-.018,+.037] (.04), 2010s +.163 (.42). MuQ 1990s +.059 (.17), 2000s +.014 (.12). Caption-only LoRA (WORKLOG N-7): 1990s -.045, 2000s -.018.
Interpretation: year captions do not make the 1990s or 2000s move; the other decades are within the earlier range (single training seed). A 10-epoch run of the same recipe is running.

## 2026-11-05/06 — round results: one-hot+16 with HPCP chroma, baseline regeneration, year-caption LoRA 10 epochs (`results/ax_onehot_pfx16_ge10_hpcp_{eval,content}.json`, `results/ax_ctrlc_ge10_regen_{eval,content}.json`, `results/ace_lora_gen_yr_e10_eval.json`, `results/six_decades_ace_lora_gen_yr_e10.txt`)
Commands: `HPCP_CHROMA=1 SUF=_hpcp bash src/run_ax.sh 0 ax_onehot_pfx16 A_eraz_onehot_s0 10`; `SUF=_regen bash src/run_ax.sh 2 ax_ctrlc A_eraz_mert_leacec0.0_s0 10`; `bash src/144_ace_lora_year.sh 1 10`.
Measured (432 clips each, target-decade hit MERT / MuQ; content top-1 / sim_own / chord): one-hot+16 with HPCP chroma .868 / .840; .880 / .891 / .397 (one-hot+16 with processor chroma, seed 0: .889 / .812). Baseline regenerated (seeds 0-2): .819 / .764; .854 / .879 / .347, identical to the first run (generation deterministic), so seeds 1-2 audio exists again.
Year-caption ACE LoRA, 10 epochs, MERT effect: 1960s +.085, 1970s +.164, 1980s +.388, 1990s +.006 [-.048,+.061], 2000s -.012 [-.024,+.001], 2010s +.100. Longer training did not move the 1990s or 2000s and lowered 1960s and 2010s relative to 3 epochs (+.182 / +.163).
Interpretation: HPCP chroma and the one-hot+16 prefix combine without loss; the ACE-Step LoRA route does not reach the 1990s and 2000s with captions, year captions or longer training. The Discogs-EffNet read-out of the regenerated baseline (`results/discogs_decade_ax_ctrlc_regen.json`) was running.

## 2026-11-06 — grid measurement script (`src/145_grid_metrics.py`, `results/grid_metrics.{json,txt}`)
Command: `python src/145_grid_metrics.py results/ax_ctrlc_ge10_eval.json results/ax_onehot_pfx16_ge10_hpcp_eval.json results/ace_lora_gen_e3_eval.json results/ace_lora_gen_yr_e3_eval.json`. Per requested decade and probe: target-hit, gain of P(target) over the neutral condition with clip-level bootstrap 95% CI, probability mass on the adjacent decades and on far decades, expected-decade response with Spearman over the requested decade, requested-by-read confusion matrix. The row format is the one of `10_eval_gen.py`, so the market axis plugs in with the same call.
Measured (MERT, hit / gain): MusicGen baseline 1960s 1.00/+.741, 1990s .69/+.389, 2000s .72/+.225, 2010s .90/+.517; one-hot+16 with HPCP chroma 1960s 1.00/+.887, 1980s .99/+.757, 1990s .67/+.370, 2000s .78/+.258; ACE-Step LoRA (3 epochs) 1990s .06/-.045 and 2000s .00/-.018 with far-decade mass .52 / .66 (MusicGen baseline .10 / .04). Spearman(requested, expected decade) = 1.000 for both MusicGen rows and the year-caption LoRA, .943 (MERT) for the caption LoRA.
Interpretation: the ordinal response is monotone for the MusicGen adapters; the ACE-Step failures at 1990s/2000s are misses onto far decades, not onto the neighbours. Cross-axis leakage on a real 2D grid is open: the only measured interaction is the T-10 era x language table (`results/two_axis.txt`, interactions within about +-.05 of 0).

## 2026-11-07 — launched: N-8 training-free era steering and the era dose sweep (`src/146_steer_vectors.py`, `src/146_steer_gen.py`, `src/run_steer.sh`)
N-8: difference-in-means steering vectors v[decade, layer] on frozen MusicGen-Melody (48 decoder layers, hidden 1536): mean over 64 TRAIN clips per decade and all positions of the hidden state under the decade prompt minus under the neutral prompt, teacher-forced on the clip's accompaniment codes with its chroma (`python src/146_steer_vectors.py --name dm64 --n 64`, saved `steer/dm64.npy`). Generation: neutral prompt, three-branch guidance (gm 3, ge 10) as the era adapter, beta * v[decade] added to layers 12-35 of the era row only (`BETA=4|16|64 LAYERS=12-35 bash src/run_steer.sh <gpu> dm64 b4|b16|b64`, GPUs 0-2); same 24 songs, 6 eras, seeds 0-2, same probe and content read-outs.
Dose sweep: one-hot+16 prefix adapter with HPCP chroma at the interpolation points a0, a0.2, ..., a1 between the 1960s and 2010s prototypes (`HPCP_CHROMA=1 SUF=_hpcp_dose bash src/run_ax.sh 3 ax_onehot_pfx16 A_eraz_onehot_s0 10 a0 a0.2 a0.4 a0.6 a0.8 a1`), for the monotonic-response and smoothness read-out.
Results follow in later entries.

## 2026-11-07 — results: N-8 training-free steering (β 4/16/64) and the era dose sweep
Commands: `BETA=<4|16|64> LAYERS=12-35 bash src/run_steer.sh <gpu> dm64 b<β>` and `HPCP_CHROMA=1 SUF=_hpcp_dose bash src/run_ax.sh 3 ax_onehot_pfx16 A_eraz_onehot_s0 10 a0 a0.2 a0.4 a0.6 a0.8 a1`; scoring `python src/145_grid_metrics.py results/steer_b{4,16,64}_eval.json results/ax_onehot_pfx16_ge10_hpcp_eval.json`. Files: `results/steer_b{4,16,64}_{eval,content}.json`, `results/ax_onehot_pfx16_ge10_hpcp_dose_{eval,content}.json`.
Steering, MERT hit per decade (60s/70s/80s/90s/00s/10s): β4 0/0/0/.03/0/.69; β16 0/0/0/.22/0/1.00; β64 0/0/0/0/0/.26. MuQ hit β16 .03/0/0/1.00/0/.99; β64 0/0/0/1.00/0/0. Spearman (MERT) −.60/.54/−.49 for β4/16/64. Far-decade mass for 1960s–1980s targets .38–1.00. Content (432 non-neutral rows): top-1 .157/.106/.081, own-chroma similarity .891/.901/.903, chord agreement .186/.178/.178 (adapter rows: top-1 ≈ .88, chord ≈ .40, WORKLOG N-2).
Dose (one-hot+16 adapter, HPCP chroma; mean expected-decade index over probe probabilities, 0 = 1960s, 5 = 2010s; 72 rows per point): MERT a0 .10, a0.2 .41, a0.4 1.80, a0.6 4.18, a0.8 4.39, a1 4.54; MuQ .36, .88, 2.19, 3.82, 4.13, 4.35.
Interpretation: prompt-difference steering at these scales did not move the requested decade (hit ≈ 0 for five of six targets) and lowered content retention; the decades it did reach (1990s, 2010s) appeared for several targets, a pull toward fixed decades rather than a graded effect. The dose response is monotone but sigmoidal: most of the 1960s→2010s change sits between a0.2 and a0.6 (MERT index 0.41→4.18), flat after a0.6.
Decision by the `TODO.md` rules: steering below threshold → round 2026-11-07b (real-audio vectors, positions ≥ 10 averaged, per-layer calibrated strength, layers 0–47; `src/147_steer_vectors_real.py`, `CALIB=1` in `src/146_steer_gen.py`, `src/run_round2.sh`); dose monotone → era × language grid is next once a GPU frees.

## 2026-11-07 — results: N-8b real-audio steering vectors with calibrated strength, and three pipeline controls
Commands: `python src/147_steer_vectors_real.py --name real64 --n 64`; `CALIB=1 BETA=<c> LAYERS=0-47 bash src/run_steer.sh <gpu> real64 rc<c>` for c = 0.02, 0.05, 0.1, 0.2 (`src/run_round2.sh`); controls `bash src/run_ctrl.sh <gpu> <vector name> <tag>` (era 1990s, 24 clips, 3 seeds; `BETA=0 LAYERS=12-35 KROWS=1` with dm64; `CALIB=1 BETA=0.05|0.2 LAYERS=0-47 KROWS=4` with real64). Files: `results/steer_rc*_{eval,content}.json`, `results/ctrl_*_{eval,content}.json` (embedding npz git-ignored).
Round 2b, MERT hit per decade (60s/70s/80s/90s/00s/10s): c .02 1.00/0/0/1.00/0/1.00; c .05 0/0/0/.97/0/1.00; c .1 0/0/0/1.00/0/1.00; c .2 0/0/0/.99/0/.90. Content top-1 over 432 non-neutral rows: .127/.150/.086/.062 (c .02/.05/.1/.2); chord agreement .176–.187.
Controls (1990s target, 72 rows each): β = 0 (same pipeline, no steering): content top-1 .958, chord .343, MERT hit .07, MuQ hit .14; real64 c .05 with all four codebook rows steered: content top-1 .347, MERT hit 1.00 but MuQ hit .00 (mean MuQ probability .79 on the 2000s); c .2: content top-1 .236, MERT hit .97, MuQ hit 1.00.
Interpretation: the generation pipeline retains content with no steering (.958), so the content loss in all steering rows comes from adding the vectors (the guidance term ge = 10 multiplies any change of the era row tenfold). The same audio is read as different decades by MERT and MuQ at c .05, and the hit pattern is nearly independent of the requested decade, so these hits reflect an off-distribution signature the probes read, not a graded era effect. The single-row hook of rounds b4–b64 and rc steered one of four codebook rows; the controls fix this (`KROWS`). Training-free difference-in-means steering is recorded as a null baseline for this model.
Next: era × language grid (`src/151_two_axis_grid.py`, tmux `grid`, flag `two_axis_grid.done`) and dose densification a0.3–a0.55 (tmux `dose2`, flag `dose2.done`).

## 2026-11-07 — results: era × language grid on the full six-decade grid (`src/151_two_axis_grid.py`, `results/two_axis_grid.{json,txt}`)
Command: `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=3 python src/151_two_axis_grid.py` (from `~/era_market`; accompaniment `gen_ax_onehot_pfx16_ge10_hpcp` seed 0, vocals `ace_a_swap_voc` languages orig/es/pt/de/it seed 0; 16 songs, 13 for es; mix-level era probe MERT layer 12, validation accuracy .477).
Measured: P(requested decade) gain from the era accompaniment with the source-language vocal 1960s +.172, 1970s +.120, 1980s +.308, 1990s +.011, 2000s +.125, 2010s +.187. Interaction (target-language minus source-language vocal) over 24 era × language cells: 23 intervals contain 0; one excludes it (1990s, de, −.009 [−.017, −.001]); largest magnitudes 1980s de −.071 [−.173, +.029], 2010s de −.083 [−.160, +.004]. P(intended language) per accompaniment (neutral, 1960s … 2010s): es .79/.78/.81/.83/.79/.84/.79 (source control .68–.73); de .81/.66/.79/.79/.80/.85/.80; it .81/.76/.75/.81/.76/.83/.77; pt .54/.50/.59/.54/.61/.64/.55 (source control .71–.77).
Interpretation: within these limits (one seed, 16 songs, weak mix-level probe) the era effect of the accompaniment did not depend on the vocal language and the language read-out did not depend on the era accompaniment, apart from a dip for German with the 1960s accompaniment (.66 vs .79–.85) and a weak Portuguese read-out (.50–.64, below the source-language control). The grid composes because the axes enter through separate stems (accompaniment vs vocal); this says nothing yet about a single model controlling both.

Per-cell FAD with CLAP is therefore not used as evidence of the era control; MERT/MuQ-based FAD and a larger generated sample are open (TODO).

One-hot+16 adapter, target-decade FAD minus neutral-generation FAD: 1960s −10.2, 1970s +3.2, 1980s +3.5, 1990s +5.6, 2000s +1.5, 2010s −0.6; ctrlc: −8.0, +3.1, +0.9, +1.6, +2.8, −3.2. Dose a0 → a1 (adapter): FAD to real 1960s 17.2, 17.5, 18.8, 26.3, 31.2, 33.0 (a = 0, .2, .4, .6, .8, 1), to real 2010s 39.0, 38.0, 31.8, 23.6, 21.2, 22.1; dose2 points fall between (a .3 17.7/36.3, .35 18.2/34.1, .45 20.8/27.8, .5 21.0/22.4, .55 23.8/23.7).
Interpretation: with MERT embeddings FAD follows the dose (distance to real 1960s rises and to real 2010s falls along a, with a flat end at a .8–1 for the 2010s) and the 1960s target moves toward real 1960s audio by 8–10 FAD units, so the CLAP result of the same day (no separation) reflects the embedding, not the control; the middle decades (1970s–2000s) do not come closer to their real decade than the neutral generation does (+0.9 to +5.6), matching their low probe gains. FAD here is an existing metric on a shared encoder; it is reported as a quality/closeness number next to the independent EffNet read-out.

## 2026-11-08 — results: language dose on the market axis (`src/153_lang_dose_text.py`, `src/153_lang_dose.sh`, `src/153_lang_dose_eval.py`, `results/lang_dose.{json,txt}`)
Commands (from `~/era_market`): `bash src/153_lang_dose.sh 2` (code-switched lyrics, first f of the translated text then the rest of the source text, f = 25/50/75 % for es and de; ACE-Step cover strength 0.95, seeds 0–2; HT Demucs stem swap onto the original accompaniment) and, after the read-out function was completed, `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=2 python src/153_lang_dose_eval.py`. 16 songs (13 for es), f = 0 and 100 % from the existing source-language and translated cover-route outputs of the same seeds. Read-outs: Whisper large-v3 language-token probability of the mix, dataset-B vocal market probe (MERT layer 16) on the isolated vocal; 95 % CI from a song bootstrap.
Measured (f = 0 / 25 / 50 / 75 / 100 %): es P(es) .160 / .085 / .200 / .397 / .610, P(source language) .710 / .473 / .352 / .205 / .033, P(Spain) of the vocal .083 / .064 / .105 / .231 / .322; de P(de) .004 / .039 / .277 / .456 / .665, P(source language) .710 / .568 / .372 / .215 / .054, P(Germany) of the vocal .201 / .229 / .294 / .434 / .517. Spearman between f and the mean read-outs: es +.90 (language and market), de +1.00 (both).
Interpretation: the language read-out and the vocal market read-out rise together with the target-language share, so a continuous control along this axis exists for the vocal route (de intervals at f = 0 and 100 % do not overlap: P(Germany) .201 [.142,.264] vs .517 [.397,.637]). Because T-4l showed that the vocal market probe's move disappears after language erasure, this is a language dose, i.e. a continuous version of the language-carried market axis, not a language-free market control; the first quarter of the dose barely moves the read-outs (es P(es) .085 at f = 25 % is below f = 0 %, whose .160 includes songs whose source language is Spanish).

Commands: `bash src/run_grid5.sh 0 1` (accompaniments `gen_ax_onehot_pfx16_ge10_hpcp_grid{A,B}`), evaluation `src/155_two_dose_grid.py` -> `results/two_dose_grid.{json,txt}`. 16 songs, 3 seeds, targets es (Spain) and de (Germany); read-outs: MERT-probe expected decade index of the accompaniment, Whisper P(target language) of the mix, vocal market probe P(target market).
Measured (song-bootstrap 95% CI):
- Era effect a1-a0 (expected decade index) at f = 0/25/50/75/100%: es +0.69 [+0.40,+1.08], +0.80, +0.68, +1.00, +0.89; de +0.69, +0.82, +0.75, +0.62, +0.71; all CIs exclude 0. Era index rises with a (e.g. es f=0: 3.68, 4.00, 4.29, 4.30, 4.37).
- Language effect f100-f0 on P(language) at a0..a1: es +0.71 to +0.73, de +0.72 to +0.78; CIs exclude 0 at every a. Market effect on P(market): es +0.284, de +0.315, identical for every a because the vocal probe reads the vocal stem only (no era dependence by construction).
- Spearman along era dose: era +1.00; along language share: P(language) +0.90 to +1.00, P(market) +0.90 to +1.00; era index vs f -0.60 (es), -0.70 (de) (small drift of the era read-out, 3.4-3.7 at a0, no systematic cell-level effect on the dose differences above).
- es has n=13 songs at f>0 cells (3 songs lacking vocal output), de n=16.
Interpretation: the two axes act on separate read-outs and the effect sizes of each axis are similar across levels of the other (overlapping CIs); consistent with independent control, with the caveat that the vocal and accompaniment come from separate stems.

## 2026-11-08 MuQ per-cell FAD (existing metric FAD, arXiv 1812.08466)
Command (server, venv_mcl): `python src/156_cell_fad_muq.py --tags ax_onehot_pfx16_ge10_hpcp ax_onehot_pfx16_ge10_hpcp_dose ax_onehot_pfx16_ge10_hpcp_dose2 --out results/cell_fad_muq` -> `results/cell_fad_muq.{json,txt}`. Embedding: MuQ layer 9 (codec-matched probe layer), reference = MuQ layer 9 of the codec-resynthesised real accompaniments of dataset A per decade, PCA 32 on real references, n=72 generated clips per cell. Real-half floor 13.5-29.4 (1980s 29.4 highest).
Measured: target-minus-neutral FAD (negative = closer to the target decade): 1960s -11.65, 1970s -0.23, 1980s -0.85, 1990s +3.67, 2000s -4.11, 2010s +1.68. Dose a0 -> a1: FAD to real 1960s 32.5 -> 50.3, to real 2010s 60.1 -> 46.0 (not monotone at every step, e.g. 2010s 41.1 at a0.8 vs 46.0 at a1; 1960s 51.7 at a0.6 vs 47.2 at a0.8).
Interpretation: MuQ-FAD shows the 1960s and 2000s shifts and the dose crossover between the two reference decades, but is noisier than MERT-FAD (monotone) and flat or wrong-signed for 1970s-1990s/2010s; with n=72 and floors of 14-29 these differences are of the order of the floor. Same caveat as before: encoder shared with the MuQ probe.

## 2026-11-08 text-to-motion style metrics on the era axis (R-Precision, MM-Dist, Diversity, MultiModality)
Command (server, venv_mcl): `python src/157_gen_metrics.py --tags ax_onehot_pfx16_ge10_hpcp ax_ctrlc_ge10_hpcp --enc {mert,muq} --out results/gen_metrics_{mert,muq}` -> `results/gen_metrics_{mert,muq}.{json,txt}`. Embedding MERT layer 22 / MuQ layer 9, PCA 32 on real codec-resynthesised accompaniments; classes = 6 real-decade centroids; n=72 generated clips per target (24 songs x 3 seeds); real rows n=193 per decade.
Measured (MERT, top-1 R-Precision; chance .167): adapter 1960s .958, 1970s .847, 1980s .889, 1990s .722, 2000s .278, 2010s .389; ctrlc 1960s .986, 1970s .819, 1980s .653, 1990s .708, 2000s .222, 2010s .375; real clips (nearest-centroid) .642/.409/.326/.326/.176/.466. MuQ top-1: adapter .931/.708/.292/.417/.333/.236; ctrlc .944/.611/.306/.389/.236/.264. MM-Dist (MERT) adapter 5.32-6.55 vs ctrlc 5.54-6.69 (real own-centroid 6.13-6.81). Diversity (MERT) generated cells 6.41-7.62 (adapter), 6.90-8.05 (ctrlc), neutral 8.59/8.33, real 8.65-9.66. MultiModality (MERT, same song, different seeds) adapter 4.42-5.06, ctrlc 4.77-5.45, neutral 5.82/5.42.
Interpretation: top-1 above chance for every target, highest for 1960s-1980s and lowest for 2000s/2010s (as in the probe hit rates); the generated cells exceed the real-clip nearest-centroid rate because generated clips cluster tighter (diversity 6.4-8.0 vs real 8.7-9.7); adapter is closer to the target centroid than ctrlc on MM-Dist for 5 of 6 targets in MERT. Centroids come from the same real data the probes are trained on (circularity caveat unchanged); lower diversity/multimodality than the neutral generation suggests a narrower output distribution under conditioning.

## 2026-11-08 FAD with sample-size extrapolation (Gui et al., arXiv 2311.01616, abstract read: FAD biased by sample size; extrapolate to infinite sample size)
Command (server, venv_mcl): `python src/158_fad_inf.py --tags ax_onehot_pfx16_ge10_hpcp ax_onehot_pfx16_ge10_hpcp_dose ax_onehot_pfx16_ge10_hpcp_dose2 --enc {mert,muq} --out results/cell_fad_inf_{mert,muq}` -> `results/cell_fad_inf_{mert,muq}.{json,txt}`. Subsets N = 36..72 of the 72 generated clips x 20 draws, linear fit FAD = a + b/N, a = FAD_inf.
Measured: FAD_inf is 3.5-4.5 (MERT) and 7-11 (MuQ) below the n=72 value in every cell. MERT target cells vs real own decade (FAD_inf, full-n in brackets): 1960s 13.7 (17.2), 1970s 22.5 (25.9), 1980s 24.9 (28.2), 1990s 24.5 (28.3), 2000s 19.7 (24.0), 2010s 18.1 (22.1); neutral generation to the same decades 22.9/18.3/19.7/18.0/17.6/18.2 (1960s..2010s). MERT dose a0 -> a1: to real 1960s 14.1 -> 29.0, to real 2010s 35.0 -> 18.3, crossover between a0.5 (16.8 vs 18.1) and a0.6 (22.4 vs 19.5). MuQ dose a0 -> a1: to real 1960s 25.5 -> 41.8, to real 2010s 51.6 -> 36.8; MuQ to-2010s curve non-monotone at a0.2 (53.5) and a0.35 (55.1).
Interpretation: extrapolation does not change the ordering of the dose curves; the 1960s target moves closer to the real 1960s than the neutral generation (13.7 vs 22.9) while the 1970s-1990s targets are not closer than neutral in MERT (22.5/24.9/24.5 vs 18.3/19.7/18.0), so FAD supports the 1960s and the 1960s-2010s dose axis only; the middle decades are not separated by FAD in this embedding.
